# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.
The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/).

---

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **Set the kiosk display scale from Settings (Advanced → "Display scale") — no more SSH for a panel whose EDID lies about its size.** The auto-scaler (`detect-display-scale.sh`) derives the kiosk device-scale from the panel's physical PPI (EDID size ÷ resolution), which works for honest panels but is blind when the EDID misreports its size. Confirmed live on a 13.3" 1920×1080 monitor that reports its physical size as **350×190 mm** (a 15.6"-class value) → **141 PPI → raw 1.081 → snaps to 1.0 → no scaling**, when the intended factor is **1.25**. A new picker (`Auto / 100 % / 125 / 150 / 175 / 200`) exposes the existing `DISPLAY_SCALE` override: the server (`displayScaleCtrl`, `GET/POST /api/display-scale`) manages the `DISPLAY_SCALE=` line in `browser.conf`, which `start-server` already sources and `detect-display-scale.sh` already honours — **no launcher change**. The **Auto** choice shows what auto-detection currently resolves to (e.g. "Auto · 100 %") so the user picks knowingly; default is **Auto**, so the whole fleet's behaviour is unchanged until a value is set. It's a physical-screen setting in the brightness category: read-open, **write localhost-only** (a remote client sees it disabled), and it **takes effect on the next kiosk relaunch** (it's a browser launch flag). 14 new controller unit tests. See `docs/display-scale-override-design.md`.
- **Display scale — "Relaunch kiosk to apply" button (no SSH/reboot to apply a change).** The scale is a browser launch flag, so a change only takes effect on the next kiosk relaunch. A button under the picker now does that in place: `POST /api/relaunch-kiosk` (localhost-only) spawns `deploy/relaunch-kiosk.sh` **detached**, which stops the autostart launcher, kills the `--kiosk` browser (TERM→KILL, family-agnostic via the `--kiosk` flag), clears Chromium singleton locks, and re-launches via `~/.local/bin/start-server` under `setsid` — the field-tested manual recipe, wrapped. It is explicitly **not** a `pi-weather-server` restart (that bounces the Node server, not the browser). Two safety touches: a **two-tap confirm** (the screen blanks ~15 s on relaunch), and the button appears **only when a relaunch would actually change the applied scale** — `GET /api/display-scale` now reports `applied` (read from the running Chromium's live `--force-device-scale-factor`), so re-selecting the value already in effect shows nothing. Git-pull-deployable (the script lives in `deploy/`, not `~/.local/bin`). 3 new unit tests (528 total).
- **"Show test alerts" — a localhost-only toggle (Settings → Local preferences) to reveal NWS test/exercise alerts for R&D.** Companion to "Show advisory alerts": a per-device opt-in (off by default) that surfaces the otherwise-hidden test alerts (CAP status ≠ "Actual") so the maintainer can see how the app renders them. **Localhost-only by construction** — the toggle row only appears for a local client (`isLocal`), and the server honors the `showTest=1` query param it drives **only for local requests** (`req.isLocal`, the unspoofable socket peer; a remote client neither sees the toggle nor receives the data, even with a forged param). A revealed test alert is unmistakable: a neutral outlined **TEST** badge beside the source badge **and** a "TEST ·" title prefix — the real severity tier is kept so the authentic render is visible. `GET /api/weather-alerts` and `GET /api/nearby-alerts` gain an optional `showTest=1` param (`docs/api.md`); EN/FR/ES.

### Changed
- **Client build toolchain moved to Babel 8 (`@babel/core` / `preset-env` / `preset-react` 7 → 8).** Dependabot proposed the three `@babel/*` majors as three separate PRs (#280/#281/#282), each of which failed the client build with `ERESOLVE`: the packages are peer-interdependent (`preset-env@7` requires `core@^7`), so a single-package bump can never resolve — they must land together. Done here as one coordinated bump. Babel 8 changes two defaults that touch our minimal `.babelrc`: `preset-env`'s default target moved from `">= 0%"` (ES5-everywhere) to `"defaults"` (modern browsers), and `preset-react`'s default runtime moved from `classic` to `automatic`. Both are now **pinned explicitly** in `.babelrc` — `preset-env { targets: "defaults" }` (right for the kiosk fleet's recent Chromium/Firefox) and `preset-react { runtime: "automatic" }` (aligned with React 18's JSX transform) — so the build is deterministic and no application code changed. Net effect: `dist/bundle.min.js` shrinks **~107 KiB (−5.3 %)** from the modern targets doing less down-transpilation. Build passes with 0 errors; smoke-tested on localhost (full UI render, live data, 0 console errors, Settings interaction). **Build-tooling only — Babel runs at build time on the dev machine, never on the Pis (they `git pull` the committed `dist/`), so there is no fleet runtime impact.** Note for other build machines: Babel 8 requires Node `^22.18 || >=24.11`. Supersedes Dependabot PRs #280, #281, #282.

### Security
- **`form-data` → 4.0.6 (clears 2 high Dependabot alerts), plus axios 1.18.0 for cross-origin redirect-header hardening.** `form-data` (a transitive dependency via axios, present in both the server and client trees) carried a high-severity CRLF-injection advisory (GHSA-hmw2-7cc7-3qxx — unescaped multipart field names / filenames); it's bumped to 4.0.6 in both lockfiles, clearing **both** high alerts. Real-world exposure here was low (the app never builds multipart bodies from attacker-controlled field names), but it clears the security board. Taken in the same pass: **axios 1.17.0 → 1.18.0** (server + client) — 1.18.0 strips caller-specified sensitive headers on cross-origin redirects, so an outbound API key (Tomorrow.io / Mapbox / Anthropic / LocationIQ) can't leak to another origin if a request were ever redirected off-host; **`@anthropic-ai/sdk` 0.102.0 → 0.104.1** (server, minor); and **js-yaml → 4.2.0** (client dev-tree, clears a moderate quadratic-complexity DoS advisory surfaced by the same audit). Client `dist` rebuilt for the bundled axios bump; `npm audit` reports **0 vulnerabilities** on both trees. Server deps require `npm install` on each Pi to take effect (deployed via the updater recipe, not a bare `git pull`). Supersedes Dependabot PRs #259/#260 (form-data) and #261/#263 (axios); #262 (postcss-preset-env, dev) deferred as trivial.

### Fixed
- **Dock: the debug button no longer paints behind the "Services · OK" status chip on the 10.1" HMIRaspi kiosk (1024 px CSS).** The v3 `BottomDock` lays `ControlButtons` (`flex: 1; min-width: 0`) beside the `HealthIndicator` chip; its button groups are `flex: 0 0 auto` (never shrink) and pack left, so when the row is wider than the space the flex box grants it, the rightmost buttons **overflow to the right, under the chip**. On an HMIRaspi — a 10.1" 800×1280 panel rotated to landscape with `DISPLAY_SCALE=1.25`, so Chromium sees 1024 px CSS → LayoutPi — with the debug trio present (`isLocal` via RPi Connect + `DEBUG=true`), the row ran ~55 px past its box and the last System button (**debug**) rendered behind "Services · OK", making it untappable. Root cause is a stale breakpoint: the chip's icon-only collapse (and the group-label hide) fired only at `max-width: 980px`, tuned for the 7" official screen (~932 px) — the 1024 px HMIRaspi sat just above it, keeping both the full-text chip *and* the group labels. Fixed by collapsing the chip to its glanceable dot across the **whole** non-desktop band (`HealthIndicator/styles.css`, `max-width: 980px → 1279px` — the exact complement of the AmbientLayers `min-width: 1280px` desktop breakpoint), which realises the intent already documented in the component ("the text label CSS-hides on the 7" Pi kiosk … so the dot still reads as a glanceable health signal on narrow docks"). Frees the ~100 px the row needed. The three group labels (Carte / Vues / Affichage / Système) are **kept** on the Pi dock — the maintainer's call; they carry the toolbar's category rhythm — so the full-text chip is the nicety that yields. The full "Services · OK" text still shows on LayoutDesktop (≥ 1280). CSS-only, no server change; git-pull-deployable (dist committed).
- **Priority-views (v3.3) model now activates on the CSS *viewport* height, not the device screen height — a display-scaled small panel gets the compact model like the 7", regardless of browser.** `priorityViewsEnabled()` (`client/src/ui/piLayout.js`) keyed its auto-detect on `window.screen.height ≤ 540`, which has two blind spots the CSS viewport height doesn't. (1) **Blind to the display scale:** the v2/v3.2 stacked rail overflows as a function of the CSS height available to it (`native ÷ DISPLAY_SCALE`), but `screen.height` reports the *device* height — so a 1024×600 panel auto-scaled to 1.25 (CSS viewport **819×480**, exactly as height-starved as the 7" 800×480, the overflow that motivated v3.3) reported `screen.height` 600 > 540 and was left on the **overflowing** stacked rail. (2) **Browser-divergent:** `screen.height` reports native px on Chromium but CSS px on Firefox under a `layout.css.devicePixelRatio` pref, so the same hardware could gate differently per kiosk browser. Both fixed by switching the auto-detect to `matchMedia("(max-height: 540px)")` — the CSS viewport height, consistent across browsers and correct under the display scale. The property the device signal was chosen for is preserved: the v3 font-size preference is `zoom` scoped to the `.rail` subtree and **never changes the viewport**, so the media query is stable across the S/M/L text sizes (the model can't flip on the same screen). The `localStorage.forcePriorityViews="on"` dev override is unchanged. **No change for any currently-deployed panel** — the 7" 800×480 stays ON (CSS 480 ≤ 540), the 10.1" 1024×640 panels stay on the v3.2 rail (640 > 540); the behavioural delta is limited to a display-scaled ~480-CSS-height panel whose native `screen.height` was > 540 (a 1024×600-class 7"). Extending the model to the roomier 1024×640 panels is a separate threshold decision, not this signal fix. Display-only, no server change. See `docs/v3.3-priority-views-design.md` §Gating.
- **Kiosk relaunched via the "Relaunch kiosk" button no longer dies on the next server restart / fleet redeploy / in-app update.** The `POST /api/relaunch-kiosk` flow (`displayScaleCtrl`) spawns `deploy/relaunch-kiosk.sh` from inside the Node server, which lives in `pi-weather-server.service`'s cgroup. The old `setsid nohup start-server` kept the launcher there, and `setsid` changes the session but **not** the systemd cgroup — so `start-server` and ~8 of 9 Chromium processes (zygote/GPU/renderers; only the main process self-scopes into its own `app.slice` scope) stayed in the service cgroup. A later `systemctl --user restart pi-weather-server` (no `KillMode` set → default `control-group`; the same restart issued by a fleet `git pull` redeploy **and** by the in-app updater `POST /api/update`) then SIGTERMed that whole cgroup, killing the kiosk — with nothing to respawn it (`start-server` runs the browser in the foreground; the XDG autostart fires once per session). Reproduced deterministically: a button-relaunched kiosk went from running to gone across one server restart. **Fix:** `relaunch-kiosk.sh` now launches the kiosk via `systemd-run --user --scope` (legacy `setsid nohup` fallback only when `systemd-run` is absent), which re-parents `start-server` — and thus the whole Chromium tree — into a transient scope unit that is a **sibling** of `pi-weather-server.service` under `app.slice`, exactly where the XDG-autostart kiosk already lives (which is why those survive a restart). Launch output is now also appended to `~/.local/state/pi-weather-station/kiosk.log` so a failed relaunch is no longer silent. Affected only the Pis where the relaunch button had been used (the 2 with a `DISPLAY_SCALE` override); autostart-launched kiosks were never in the service cgroup. Git-pull-deployable (script lives in `deploy/`). The deeper "no respawn after a browser crash" gap is tracked separately on `ROADMAP.md` (dedicated `Restart=always` kiosk service). See `incident_kiosk_cgroup_killed_by_server_restart.md`.
- **NWS test/exercise alerts (CAP status ≠ "Actual") no longer render as real alerts.** NWS disseminates Test / Exercise / System / Draft messages on the *live* alerts feed at real CAP severity — e.g. the National Tsunami Warning Center's periodic *Test Tsunami Warning* that covers the entire US + Eastern-Canadian coast at severity **Extreme**. With no status filter these surfaced everywhere a real alert does: a full red "Tsunami Warning" banner (the literal "TEST" was even scrubbed from the headline by the intro cleaner), a coast-wide red **map polygon** via the nearby-alerts overlay, and the **Sense HAT** LED matrix. NWS alerts are now tagged `isTest` (`server/govAlertSources/_shared.js → isTestStatus`) and **default-hidden at the orchestrator** (`govAlertsCtrl.getActiveAlertsAt` + `getNearbyAlertsAt`), so the gate covers the banner, the map overlay **and** the Sense HAT in one place — not just one HTTP endpoint. Hidden by default everywhere; never sent to remote clients. While here, the NWS zone-geometry enrichment (`nws.js`) is now **lazy** (a hidden test alert triggers no zone fetch at all) and **bounded to ~10 concurrent fetches** (the tsunami test alone spans **437** zones — previously 437 simultaneous requests to api.weather.gov). New `test/nwsTestStatus.test.js` + `test/alertTestStrip.test.js` (537 total).
- **Current-conditions Gust tile (`MetricsGrid` 2×2) no longer shows a permanent "—".** The v3.2 decision-grade 2×2 (Wind / **Gust** / UV / Humidity) reads `windGust` from the *current* Tomorrow.io timestep, but `windGust` was never in `CURRENT_FIELDS` (`server/proxyCtrl.js`) — only `HOURLY_FIELDS` / `DAILY_FIELDS` request it (for the Vent chart's gust series). So the tile rendered a dash for every location while the forecast chart right beside it clearly showed gust forecasts (e.g. "19 mph gusts") — a confusing mismatch reported from the field. Fixed by adding `"windGust"` to `CURRENT_FIELDS`; the field travels in the same Tomorrow.io call (no extra request volume), and the addition bumps `CURRENT_FIELDS_HASH`, which orphans every cached `current` entry on the fleet so the next fetch picks up the new shape (one fresh fetch per Pi, no manual cache wipe). Server-side, no client change — the tile already handles the value and its null fallback.
- **Current-conditions Visibility tile (`MetricsGrid` 2×2 / Conditions view) no longer shows a permanent "—".** Same root cause as the Gust tile above: the tile reads `visibility` from the *current* Tomorrow.io timestep, but `visibility` was never in `CURRENT_FIELDS` (`server/proxyCtrl.js`) — it wasn't in `HOURLY_FIELDS` / `DAILY_FIELDS` either. So the tile rendered a dash for every location. Fixed by adding `"visibility"` to `CURRENT_FIELDS`; the field travels in the same Tomorrow.io call (no extra request volume), and the addition bumps `CURRENT_FIELDS_HASH`, which orphans every cached `current` entry on the fleet so the next fetch picks up the new shape (one fresh fetch per Pi, no manual cache wipe). Server-side, no client change — the tile already converts the value to the user's distance unit (mi/km) and handles its null fallback.
- **Update-available badge no longer pins the Pi GPU (kiosk ran ~12 °C hotter with the fan cycling).** The toolbar's "update available" dot (`ControlButtons`, shown whenever `updateAvailable`) pulsed via `animation: update-pulse … infinite`, animating `transform` + `opacity` — both *composited* properties. On the Raspberry Pi's V3D GPU an infinite composited animation keeps the Chromium GPU process recompositing every vsync for as long as it runs, and the badge is up most of the time across the fleet (any installed-vs-latest gap leaves it on between releases). A field Pi (Weather-Station2) sat at ~66–67 °C / ~20 % GPU-process with the fan cycling on a thermal trip point; applying the pending update made the badge disappear and the temperature dropped to ~54 °C immediately, isolating the badge as the load. Fixed by making the pulse **finite**: `update-pulse 2s ease-in-out 4 forwards` — it pulses 4× (~8 s) when the badge mounts (enough to catch the eye when an update is detected) then `forwards`-holds a steady dot, so the compositor falls back to idle while the update is still pending. A static orange dot still flags "update available". CSS-only; covers both the local and remote badge instances. Surfaced via an on-Pi thermal investigation; see the GPU-idle note for why a `box-shadow`/colour pulse would not have helped (it just moves the per-frame cost from the GPU compositor to the CPU renderer).
- **Pi/mobile clock (`TimeBlock`) ticked at 1 Hz despite showing only HH:mm.** The v3 clock slab re-rendered and re-ran its `Intl.DateTimeFormat` work every second via a 1 s `setInterval`, even though it displays no seconds — 60× more renderer wakeups than the display can change, continuously, on an always-on kiosk. This is the exact waste already fixed in `HeroBand` (the desktop clock) but never ported to `TimeBlock`, which is the clock actually mounted on the 7" Pi and the mobile layout. Switched to a **minute-aligned chained `setTimeout`** (mirrors HeroBand): each tick recomputes the next minute boundary so the phase self-heals after background-tab timer throttling / sleep-wake / NTP step, with a `visibilitychange` resync for a backgrounded mobile tab returning to view. Renderer wakeups for the clock drop ~60×. Display-identical, no behaviour change.
- **Debug panel (v3) — CPU temperature and fan speed are live again, and colour-coded.** The v2 Debug panel polled `/api/debug/cpu-temp` + `/api/debug/fan-speed` every 5 s and coloured the temperature (green/amber/red); the v3 `DebugPanel` migration wired only the one-shot `/api/debug` snapshot, so the Server KPI rows showed a value **frozen at panel-open time** and with no colour. Restored both: the live 5 s poll now lives inside `BucketServer` (so it starts/stops with the Server-KPI section's own mount, zero polling when it isn't pinned, automatic cleanup on close), and the reading escalates **neutral → amber (≥ 60 °C) → red (≥ 75 °C)**. The thresholds are re-grounded on the actual **CM5 / Pi-5 active-cooler fan trip points** (50 / 60 / 67.5 / 75 °C, hardware-critical 110 °C) rather than the v2 comment's Pi-4 figure: amber spans the fan-ramp band (audible by trip 3 ≈ 67.5 °C), red marks trip 4 (fan maxed). No green "good" tier — the night-red palette is intentionally mono-red, so the code only escalates. Server endpoints unchanged (they already existed); display-only on the v3 client. Surfaced during the on-Pi thermal investigation that produced the two fixes above — the live readout would have been the right tool for it.
- **AI weather summary — French/Spanish summaries no longer drift to English under imperial units.** The Claude prompt opened with the language instruction ("Write … in French"), but a prompt dense with imperial/US unit tokens (°F, mph, miles) anchored Haiku to English by recency, so `lang=fr`/`es` with imperial units returned an *English* summary while metric returned the correct language — the same English-anchoring that already forced the hard-coded radar-paragraph label. Fixed in `server/aiSummaryCtrl.js`: the opening now says "entirely in {language}", the unit instruction notes the unit system doesn't change the writing language, and a closing reminder is appended at highest recency (right before generation, non-English only). Verified fr/es + imperial → correct language across fresh builds; metric unchanged. Server-side (no flag) — affects the whole fleet, and the legacy v2 AI summary too (same endpoint).
- **Settings → Advanced → "AI · radar analysis" toggles no longer wrap word-per-word on the 7" kiosk.** The `.grid2` toggle grid promoted to 2 columns at ≥ 760 px, which deliberately put the 800 px kiosk in 2 columns — leaving each toggle's multi-line sub-text only ~150 px, so it wrapped one word per line. The breakpoint moved to ≥ 1280 px: the whole kiosk family (< 1280) now renders single-column (full-width sub-text), and only the wide desktop settings panel keeps 2 columns. CSS-only.
- **Temperature chart — the peak value label no longer clips at the top of the plot area.** On the Temp tab, the max/min key points get a value label drawn *above* the point (`chartjs-plugin-datalabels`, `align: "top"`). When the data max coincided with the top gridline (e.g. an 82° peak landing exactly on the 82° tick), the peak sat flush against the plot-area top and its label overflowed past the canvas edge — only the bottom of the digits showed. The `layout.padding.top: 16` headroom wasn't enough (the label height + offset ≈ the padding, so its top row clipped). Fixed by adding a `grace: "10%"` band to the temp y-axis (`MetricChart`) so the scale always extends a fraction of the data range beyond the max/min — the peak sits below the top gridline with room for its label, pixel-independent across font-size zoom and any temperature range. Temp only (wind/precip carry no key-point labels). Display-only.
- **NWS tropical products (Hurricane Local Statement / Tropical Cyclone Statement) now parse cleanly — no leaked `**` markers, no raw dash rules, no mis-filed sections.** These longer-form products mix two header styles the alert parser didn't fully handle: a Markdown-bold headline (`**Potential Tropical Cyclone One Expected to Bring Heavy Rainfall…**`) and "setext" section headers (a title on one line, a rule of dashes on the next — `SITUATION OVERVIEW` / `POTENTIAL IMPACTS` / `PRECAUTIONARY/PREPAREDNESS ACTIONS` / `NEXT UPDATE`). `alertParser` only knew the `* WHAT:` asterisk-bullet / `KEYWORD...` / `Heading:` forms, so on a Houston/Galveston PTC One statement the `**` printed verbatim, and each dash-underlined header **leaked into the previous section's body as raw text — dragging its ASCII dash rule with it** — while its content got mis-filed under the preceding asterisk bullet (the *Situation Overview* prose rendered under the **Storm Information** icon, *Next Update* under **Additional Sources**). Fixed in `client/src/ui/alertParser.js`: a pre-pass strips `**` pairs (we render plain text, and `**` never appears in the bullet markup) and rewrites each setext header into a canonical `* HEADER:` bullet (dropping the dash rule) so the existing split + lead-extraction path picks it up and classifies it (`POTENTIAL IMPACTS` → impact, `PRECAUTIONARY/PREPAREDNESS ACTIONS` → action). The product's **two-level hierarchy is preserved**: these dash-underlined headers are NWS *Level-1* sections that group the `* ` bullets beneath them (`NEW INFORMATION` → *Changes / Current Watches / Storm Information*; `POTENTIAL IMPACTS` → *Flooding Rain* …), so the parser flags them (`group: true`) and `AlertDetailInline` renders them as a **full-width eyebrow divider** (no icon column) with the icon-led bullet sections beneath reading as subordinate — instead of laying everything out flat, which made a body-less container like `NEW INFORMATION` look like an orphan title with its items floating beside it. The Special Marine Warning "the lead IS the content" case (a single-line `* Until 845 PM CDT.` whose whole content is the lead) is preserved. Display-only, no server change. 3 new regression tests (`test/alertParser.test.js`, 503 total). Surfaced on the v3 alert detail panel during the 2026-06-16 Gulf system.
- **NWS `- ` sub-items in an alert now render as a real bulleted list instead of a run-on paragraph.** Storm coordinates under *Storm Information* (`- About 220 miles…` / `- 27.3N 97.6W` / `- Storm Intensity…`) and the per-hazard bullets under *Flooding Rain* / *Wind* / *Surge* / *Tornadoes* are line-leading `- ` items in the NWS text. The v3 body rendered each section's detail by splitting on blank lines only, so the single newlines between those items folded to spaces and the whole list collapsed into one wall of text. A new pure helper `splitBody()` (`alertParser`) walks the detail into ordered paragraph/list blocks — a line-leading `-`/`•` + whitespace starts a list item (soft-wrapped continuation lines fold in), while an inline ` - ` separator (`Galveston TX - 27.3N`) and numeric ranges (`1-3 feet`) are left intact — and a shared `RichText` renderer in `AlertDetailInline` emits a real `<ul>`. Matches how AccuWeather renders the same product; we additionally keep the cleaner structure (no `**`, no dash rules, group hierarchy). Display-only. 4 new `splitBody` unit tests (`test/alertParser.test.js`, 508 total; verbatim copy synced).
- **Bare URLs in an alert description no longer overflow and clip at the slab's right edge.** NWS description bodies embed plain-text URLs (e.g. `http://www.weather.gov/safety/flood…`) — a single token with no break opportunity. The v3 alert-detail body (`AlertDetailInline`) had no `overflow-wrap`, so a long URL ran past the content width and got cut off at the right edge (observed on a Houston Flood Watch). Added `overflow-wrap: anywhere` to the body text classes (`.text`, `.sectionDetail`, `.groupBody`) — the same defensive treatment `AlertBanner` already applies to its title — so the URL wraps within the slab. The URL stays plain text (kiosk-safe; per the QR-only rule it's never turned into a clickable `<a>`). Display-only, CSS-only.
- **Alert polygons get a dark casing in light mode so the boundary stays glanceable against the cream basemap.** The alert-zone polygons painted a tier-coloured stroke alone (`tierColour`, 2 px + 15 % fill), but the warm alert hues lose luminance contrast against the warm, light basemap — orange ≈ 2.3:1, yellow ≈ 1.3:1, both **below the 3:1 floor** for a graphical boundary (only red, ≈ 3.7:1, read acceptably). The dark-casing contrast trick already used for the radar rings (`buildRingLayers` draws a `RING_OUTLINE_COLOR` `#3a3938` outline under the bright stroke) was **never applied to the alert polygons**. A new shared `buildAlertPolygonLayers()` (`WeatherMap/geometry.js`) now renders the polygon as stacked layers — a solid dark casing (weight 2 + `RING_OUTLINE_EXTRA_WEIGHT`) beneath the coloured stroke + 15 % fill — **in light mode only**, lifting the boundary edge to ≈ 8.5:1 while keeping the tier colour for severity identity. Dark mode already contrasts the same hues against the near-black basemap, and nightRed deliberately collapses to a single red family (Phase 3 A1) that a grey casing would break — both keep the single coloured layer. Applies to both the single highlighted-alert overlay and the nearby-alerts survey polygons; the ascending-severity paint order is unchanged. Display-only, no server change. Surfaced while comparing alert-polygon contrast across the light / night / nightRed palettes.
- **Overlapping nearby-alerts polygons now show the most severe tier on top — a Flood Warning no longer reads as a yellow Advisory.** When a location sits under several alerts at once (a narrow Flood Warning nested in a broad Flood Watch + Advisory, or two zone-based alerts sharing one county polygon), the survey painted the polygons in the order the server hands them back — **worst-first** — and Leaflet paints later-inserted layers on top, so the *least* severe polygon (yellow Advisory, drawn last) ended up over the *most* severe (red Warning, drawn first). Where the geometries overlapped the red was buried under the yellow fill/border, and for two alerts sharing an identical zone the area read entirely yellow even though a Warning covered it. Fixed by rendering the survey polygons in **ascending** severity (`TIER_PAINT_ORDER`) so the worst tier is inserted last and paints on top; nested polygons now show the broad lower-tier ring with the severe inner polygon dominant, and a shared zone takes its worst tier's colour. Display-only, no server change. Reported by k5map (issue #252).
- **Nearby-alerts tap popup no longer "sticks" (re-appears) after tapping "Re-center here".** The re-center button moved the location correctly but the survey popup popped back up at the tapped point instead of closing. Root cause is an event-timing race, not the re-center logic: clicking the button removes the popup from the DOM **synchronously inside the click handler**, which strips Leaflet's popup-click guard (the `_leaflet_disable_click` flag lives on the popup container) **mid-propagation** — by the time the originating click reaches the map, Leaflet no longer sees it as a popup click, fires a map `click`, and the debounced `handleMapClick` re-opens a survey popup at that point. (Leaflet's own built-in popup close button sidesteps this by calling `DomEvent.stop`; our custom button didn't.) A synthetic `.click()` never reproduced it because React doesn't flush the unmount mid-dispatch — only a real, trusted pointer click does. Fixed by **deferring `setSurveyPopup(null)` to the next tick** so the popup stays mounted until the click finishes propagating, letting Leaflet's guard swallow the click exactly as it does for any other in-popup click (verified live: a real click inside a popup that is *not* removed never leaks a map click). Touch + mouse; no server change. See `incident_survey_popup_recenter_click_leak.md`.
- **Alert severity chip now prints the real product type — a Heat Advisory reads "Avis", not "Veille".** The `SeverityChip` derived its word purely from CAP severity (`minor→Advisory`, `moderate→Watch`, `severe/extreme→Warning`), but those words are NWS/ECCC **product types**, not severity levels — so a Heat **Advisory** (CAP severity *Moderate*) printed "Veille" (watch), falsely claiming it was a watch. Surfaced live on a Houston/Nichols Heat Advisory. Fixed by **decoupling the two axes the chip conflated**: the **colour** still comes from severity (yellow/orange/red — the intentional 3-tier collapse), but the **word** now comes from the actual product type parsed from the English event name (new pure helper `eventProductType()` in `ui/alertLogic.js`: Warning > Watch > Advisory > Statement, with a severity-derived fallback). Works for both sources (NWS `event`, ECCC `alert_name_en` are both English). New locale key `alert.severityStatement` (Statement / Bulletin / Boletín) for *Special Weather Statement*. While here, the chip's internal colour-tier slugs + tokens were renamed from product-type names (`advisory/watch/warning`, `--sev-adv/watch/warn-*`) to severity names (`low/med/high`, `--sev-low/med/high-*`) so the colour layer no longer borrows product-type vocabulary — purely internal, colour output unchanged. 4 new regression tests (`test/severityProductType.test.js`).
- **NWS zone-based alert polygons no longer have a hole over `GeometryCollection` zones.** A Houston Flood Watch drew its footprint over every surrounding county *except Houston itself* — a gap exactly at the user's location. NWS serves most of these alerts by **forecast zone** (`feature.geometry: null`, resolved from `properties.affectedZones`), and a few zones — e.g. `TXZ213` "Inland Harris" over Houston — are returned as a GeoJSON **`GeometryCollection`** (mixed Polygon + MultiPolygon) rather than a single polygon. `nwsZones.js` accepted only `Polygon`/`MultiPolygon`, so it **silently dropped** the GeometryCollection zone, leaving its polygon out of the merged alert footprint while every plain-polygon neighbour drew normally. The alert **banner** stayed correct throughout (it comes from NWS's authoritative server-side point query, independent of the app's client-side zone→geometry resolution) — only the rendered polygon had the hole. Fixed by normalizing a `GeometryCollection` to a `MultiPolygon` in `getZoneGeometry` and handling it recursively in `mergeAsMultiPolygon`, so every downstream consumer (Leaflet render + the `pointInPolygon` / `circleIntersectsPolygon` server tests) sees a clean MultiPolygon. Affects any NWS zone-based alert nationwide that covers a GeometryCollection zone — Houston merely surfaced it. 7 new regression tests in `test/nwsZones.test.js`.
- **Nearby-alerts radius ring no longer hides under a radar ring at 50 / 100 km (km mode).** The alert-radius slider's km stops (50, 60, …, 100) include the two values where the dashed survey ring lands exactly on a radar analysis ring (inner 50 km, outer 100 km) — the dotted ring drew pixel-for-pixel over the radar ring and read as invisible. (In mi mode the radar rings are 30 / 60 mi while the alert ring is still drawn from km, so they already sat ~1.7 / 3.4 km apart and were visible — this is why miles never showed the bug.) When the drawn ring coincides with a *currently-drawn* radar ring (inner whenever radar analysis is on; outer only with the extended radius), it's now nudged 3 km outward so it stays legible. Display-only: the survey *query* radius is unchanged, and mi mode is untouched (the 0.5 km coincidence test never trips there).
- **Nearby-alerts radius ring now reads as blue in light mode.** The ring's light-mode colour was a muted slate (`#3a5a78`) that shared the exact red channel (and a close green channel) with the radar calm-rings' grey (`#3a3938`) — only the blue channel differed, so against the light, colourful basemap it read as "grey with a hint of blue" and was indistinguishable from the two radar rings (reported on-screen). Bumped to a clearly-saturated blue (`#1565c0`) so the survey ring is visibly distinct from the radar rings, as the design always intended. Light mode only; dark mode (`#6a8ca8` cool blue vs the warm-grey radar rings) already read correctly. A rationale comment in `geometry.js` records the red-channel collision so the value isn't "tidied" back to a muted slate later.

### Changed
- **Radar focus mode (MIN) on the 7" Pi is now a pure radar view — the compact place/temp hero overlay is gone.** Entering radar focus (tapping the focus square under the Leaflet +/- zoom controls, or the dock's scrubber/timeline button in the priority model) collapsed the rail and pinned a small `HeroOverlayMin` place/temp/condition card over the top-left of the fullscreen radar. Per the maintainer's direction, focus mode should let the radar truly fill the screen: the hero overlay is removed so nothing sits over the map (the gov-alert `FloatingMiniBanner` still overlays when an eligible alert is active — a deliberate safety exception so focus mode never silently hides a severe alert). The at-a-glance place/temp readout is one tap away in the rail (toggle the focus square back to MID). Applies to both the v3.2 focus toggle and the v3.3 scrubber-into-MIN path. The now-orphaned `HeroOverlayMin` component was deleted. Display-only, no server change.

### Added
- **AI summary view on the 7" Pi (v3.3 priority model).** The dock's IA (sparkle) button — a dead toggle in the priority glance since the AI prose left the rail — now opens a full-rail maximized **AI view** (`AiView`) presenting Claude's existing 3-paragraph summary as labelled sections titled by their **real period**: « Maintenant » / « Ce soir | Cette nuit | Demain » / « Analyse radar ». Mounted lazily (only in the `ai` layout state) so the paid Anthropic call fires on open, not as background overhead; the summary is fetched through a new shared `useAiSummary` hook (extracted from the legacy inline slab) with clean loading / unavailable states. To title the second section concretely, **`GET /api/weather-summary` now returns a `period` field** (`"evening"` / `"overnight"` / `"tomorrow"` / `null`, server-derived from the local hour + data availability; `docs/api.md` updated) — an additive, integrator-visible response change shared with the legacy summary. The view is behind the opt-in `localStorage.forcePriorityViews` flag (7" 800×480 only). EN/FR/ES.
- **v3.2 "3 états radar" — the 7" Pi radar screen now has three layout states (MIN · MID · MAX), one tap apart.** A single `piLayoutState` enum (`"min" | "mid" | "max"`) drives the whole Pi shell via `data-pi-state`, replacing the old two-state focus boolean (kept as a derived `piRadarMaximized` shim so the Leaflet-side consumers — WeatherMap, MapResizer — migrate independently). **MID** (default) is the split screen, but the info column is re-conceived as the radar's *companion* rather than a mini-dashboard: the map answers "where is the precip", the column answers "what does it feel like / when does it change". Top-to-bottom — the gov-alert stack · a slim clock · the hero anchored on **feels-like** · a new **NowcastLine** · air quality · a decision-grade 2×2 (**Wind / Gust / UV / Humidity**) · indoor · a "Prévisions" button into MAX; the forecast chart and the AI prose leave the glance. **MIN** is radar fullscreen (the old focus mode), now with a compact place/temp **HeroOverlayMin** pinned over the map and the dock hidden so the radar truly fills the screen. **MAX** is forecast-forward: the map shrinks to a frozen ~190 px thumbnail (map chrome hidden, RainViewer frame animation paused to spare the Pi GPU) and the forecast chart fills the full-width rail — reached from the "Prévisions" button or by **tapping the NowcastLine**, left via the chart's restore button.
  - **NowcastLine** (new, the keystone): surfaces the radar nowcast verdict the app *already computes* (`getRadarAlertState` — the same state machine the RADAR banner and the auto-tab selector consume) as one glanceable temporal line ("Précipitations fortes qui s'intensifient" + a trend arrow), collapsing to nothing in calm weather. It was previously only shown as a banner above the orange/red alert threshold; now it also rides the lean column and is the contextual shortcut into the forecast.
  - **MetricsGrid** re-spec'd from Wind / Humidity / UV / Pressure to the decision-grade **Wind / Gust / UV / Humidity** (pressure drives no everyday household call; gust + humidity do) — applies to all layouts.
  - LayoutDesktop / LayoutMobile are unchanged — they keep their existing in-rail behaviour (`piLayoutState` is `null` there, and the shared ChartTabs / WeatherMap / MapResizer / MetricsGrid changes are guarded on it). EN/FR/ES. 508 tests green; the full branch passed an adversarial multi-agent review pass. From the Claude Design "Prototype v3.2 — 3 états radar" handoff. *Follow-ups (see ROADMAP): re-surfacing the AI summary on Pi (a MAX tab or dock button), the elastic metrics grid under stacked alerts, and the context-promoted "spotlight" metric tile.*
- **v3.2 alert cards — a new air-quality (`AIR`) alert, plus a compact self-navigating gov card on the Pi rail.** Reworks the MID rail's top-of-rail alert stack so it matches the Claude Design handoff (compact one-row cards, one per source, stacked):
  - **Air quality becomes a banner-producing source (tag `AIR`).** When the server-normalized AQ `category` reaches the health-risk band — `high` (AQHI ≥ 7 / IQA > 100 / EPA AQI > 150) → orange, `veryHigh` → red — a new compact `ambient/AirAlertCard` escalates above the inline `AirCard`: an `AIR` category badge + the index's source badge (`AQHI`/`IQA`/`AQI`) + the reading in the project's existing AQ vocabulary (« 8 · Élevé »), with station / dominant pollutant / observation age behind a tap (`DetailsPopover`). Below the band the reading stays in the inline `AirCard` only — escalating "moderate" would keep the card up nearly year-round. The inline `AirCard`'s AQ row is **suppressed** (`suppressAqRow`) while the alert card is up so the AQHI isn't shown twice (pollen row, if present, stays). New pure `getAirAlertState()` in `ui/alertLogic.js` (keys on the normalized `category`, so one threshold covers AQHI / IQA / EPA AQI), 3 regression tests.
  - **The gov alert card goes compact + self-navigating on the 7" rail.** The `AlertBanner` gov head collapses to a single row — abbreviated severity chip (« AVERT. »), source badge, ellipsized title — and the multi-alert `1 / N` counter becomes a **tap-to-cycle** control that steps through the eligible alerts. This replaces the `AlertMiniCards` "other alerts" stack, **dropped from the Pi rail** to reclaim vertical space (it stays on desktop/mobile, which keep the full Phase-4 head) — but its **"restore N hidden alerts" pill is kept on Pi** (restore-only mode), so an alert dismissed via the detail footer's *Masquer* stays recoverable (without it, a dismissed alert was unrecoverable on the 7" until the 4 h auto-resurface — field-caught 2026-06-18). Tapping the row still expands the full description (`AlertDetailInline`); the full title is one tap away. On Pi the banner ✕ relocates to the detail footer (it crowded the cycle counter). New `SeverityChip` `abbreviated` mode + `alert.severity*Short` keys (EN/FR/ES) — the full word (« AVERTISSEMENT » ≈ 140 px) squeezed the one-row title to nothing.
  - **`AlertBanner`'s radar branch is suppressed on Pi** — v3.2 gives the radar verdict its own always-present surface (`NowcastLine`), so the banner no longer double-renders the same verdict on the MID rail (desktop/mobile keep the radar banner). New badge documented in `CLAUDE.md` + `AirAlertCard` JSDoc. EN/FR/ES. 511 tests green.
- **Auto-select forecast tab (opt-in, Phase 1).** A new **Settings → Local preferences → "Auto-select forecast tab"** toggle (per-device, `localStorage`, **off by default**) lets the forecast chart's metric tab (Temp / Wind / Precip) follow active weather: an ECCC/NWS alert or a forecast threshold (gust ≥ 90 km/h, precip ≥ 70 % over the next 6 h, apparent temp outside −25…32 °C, thunder/freezing-rain now) points the tab at the metric that explains it. A small source-badge reason chip (`NWS` / `ECCC` / `FCST`) marks an auto-switch and clears when the hazard passes; any manual tab tap holds the user's choice for 20 min; a **new** severe/extreme alert always wins. Motivated by — and built for — non-touch displays (a stable monitor with no mouse/keyboard) where the "don't yank an active reader" stage-0 inhibit is skipped (`navigator.maxTouchPoints === 0`): there's no one to interrupt, and the right tab is showing before anyone walks up. Deterministic hazard router (`client/src/ui/autoTabSelector.js`, pure + regression-tested), **null-on-calm**, switches **metric only** (never the 24h/5d period), with hysteresis + dwell anti-flap. No radar yet (Phase 2), no server change. EN/FR/ES. Design of record: `docs/auto-forecast-tab-selection-design.md`.
- **Auto-select forecast tab — Phase 2 (radar nowcast + two gating refinements).** Wires the radar class into the opt-in auto-tab selector and tightens two behaviours. (1) **Radar nowcast** — an active radar echo (RainViewer analysis via `getRadarAlertState`, maxSev ≥ 2 at ≥ mid confidence) now drives the tab to **Precip**, ranked below gov alerts and above the bare forecast. (2) **A2 priority override** — a **red + approaching + high-confidence** radar echo outranks an *orange* (moderate) gov advisory: it surfaces an imminent storm the radar sees before any warning is issued, while a **red** (severe/extreme) gov alert still wins outright and the new-severe-alert puncture still beats everything. (3) **B2 active-reader inhibit scoped to the forecast card** — the touch "don't switch under a reader" gate now keys on recent interaction with the *forecast card itself* (`slabRef` pointer/scroll, 60 s grace) instead of any global screen activity, so the tab can switch while you use the radar but not while you read/scroll the forecast (a tab switch is gentler than the routine RainViewer layer refresh). Non-touch displays (the stable monitor) stay uninhibited; the 20-min manual hold still wins on a tab tap. The A2 override carries **ENTER/EXIT hysteresis** (a "warm-band"): once it has committed Precip it holds the tab while the echo stays red and at least one axis (approaching *or* high confidence) still holds, so a single-tick flicker of the radar trend/confidence near a threshold can't seesaw the tab against a persistent orange gov advisory — only a genuine two-axis recede hands the tab back. The shared priority ladder is now one `pickHazardTab()` helper used by both the gated switch and the chip verdict so they can't drift. Still opt-in, default OFF, metric-only, null-on-calm. No server change. 15 new reducer tests (500 total).
- **The clock's date opens a "Saisons" popover — the next four turning points, year-round.** Tapping the date (desktop: behind the clock-panel hairline; Pi/mobile: the caption above the clock) opens a small popover listing the **next four solstices/equinoxes** with their full names, dates and day-counts (« Solstice de juin · 20 juin · dans 8 j » …). The **date** is the trigger — always on screen — so the list is reachable **all year**, not only near an event. The in-window « Solstice dans 8 j » countdown stays as enriching text beside the date (desktop: inline within the trigger; Pi/mobile: its own line below the clock). Reuses the existing `DetailsPopover` (portal-anchored, palette-aware, Esc / outside-tap to close) — the same shape as the sun/moon popovers — with the house tap affordance (dotted underline, `:active`, ≥ 44 px hit area, zero hover). New shared `Seasons` component (`SeasonsTrigger` + a `seasonCountdownLabel` helper, one home across HeroBand and TimeBlock) + `nextSolarEvents()` astronomy helper; restores the long-form `astronomy.solarEvent.*` names (the list rows) and adds `seasonsTitle` + `solarEventDays` keys (EN/FR/ES). `docs/ui-layout_{en,fr}.md` updated.

### Changed
- **Pi rail affordance redesign (v3.2 / v3.3) — one consistent tap vocabulary across the 7" rail.** Three affordances are now used consistently: a **maximize square** drills into the *same* topic at full size, a **dock button** *changes* topic, and a **dotted underline** opens a *popover* in place. Three concrete changes follow:
  - **NowcastLine is now a status-only radar line.** It no longer maximizes to the forecast on tap (a topic-jump disguised as a drill-in) — it is a plain container, not a button. The calm copy is re-anchored to the radar: instead of restating the sky ("Clear and sunny"), the dry-calm state now reads **"No rain within 100 km"** (the radar outer-ring radius + the active distance unit; "No rain within 60 mi" in miles) with a leading **RADAR** source badge so the line is honestly radar-sourced. The sky-adaptive sun/moon/cloud icon is kept (its low contrast on the quiet calm tier is fixed — the glyph now uses body ink instead of inheriting the soft-accent edge). The light-snow / light-precipitation sub-states keep their explicit wording (radar shows weak echoes there, so "no rain" would be wrong). A new **radar-freshness gate** shows **"Radar unavailable"** when the newest *actual* RainViewer frame is more than 15 minutes old (≈ 1.5 publish cycles), so the calm copy never asserts clear skies on stale data — the freshness is measured on the frame's own timestamp (surfaced from `WeatherMap` onto the radar state), not our fetch-success. New keys `nowcast.calm.noRainWithin` / `nowcast.calm.radarUnavailable`; updated `nowcast.aria` ("Radar status: …", no longer "tap to open the forecast"); removed the dead `nowcast.openForecast`.
  - **Forecast moves to a dedicated dock button.** With the NowcastLine maximize gone, the forecast (MAX view) is reached from a new **forecast button** in a new **"Views" group** of the BottomDock (icon: Carbon `chart-column`). The existing IA/sparkle view-open relocates into the same group, separating "open a full-rail view" (Views) from "manipulate the map in place" (Map). The forecast button is present on the Pi dock in **both** v3.2 and v3.3 so the user is never stranded; its down-state is a plain class toggle when MAX is active (no animated highlight — kiosk-GPU rule). New keys `controls.openForecast` + `controls.groupViews`.
  - **AQI / pollen rows: chevron → dotted underline.** The AirCard rows dropped the trailing chevron in favour of the house dotted-underline popover signal on the "42 AQI" value term (and the same on the pollen worst-allergen term), matching the city-name / moon underlines. The whole row stays the ≥ 44 px tap surface and the detail popover is unchanged.

  EN/FR/ES; `docs/ui-layout_{en,fr}.md` updated. No server change. From the "Pi rail affordance redesign" LLD (`docs/rail-affordance-redesign-design.md`).
- **Dependency maintenance (non-security).** Routine Dependabot bumps with no behaviour change: `@anthropic-ai/sdk` 0.104.1 → 0.104.2 and `body-parser` 2.2.2 → 2.3.0 (server), `postcss-preset-env` 11.3.0 → 11.3.1 (client dev-tree). Lockfiles only (all covered by existing caret ranges — no manifest change); the client `dist` rebuild was byte-identical (the postcss-preset-env patch doesn't alter the compiled CSS). `npm audit` stays at 0 on both trees; build green, 511 tests pass. Clears the last two open Dependabot PRs (#267, #262).
- **Nearby alerts — the radius ring can now be hidden independently of the polygons.** The dashed survey-radius circle and the alert polygons were both gated on the single "Nearby alerts" dock toggle, so you couldn't keep one without the other. A new **Settings → Local preferences → "Show alert radius ring"** toggle (per-device, `localStorage`, **on by default** — the existing look is unchanged) now gates only the ring; the alert polygons stay tied to the dock toggle. Turning it off keeps the alerts' real geometry on the map while dropping the round proxy circle. Kept in *Local preferences* (not Advanced) deliberately: it's a per-screen display choice that any viewer — including a read-only remote/LAN client that can't write `settings.json` — can flip, alongside "Show advisory alerts". Requested by mlcampbe, who wanted the polygon boxes without the ring. EN/FR/ES (`SettingsPanel` inline-trilingual). No server change, no new endpoint.
- **Desktop clock panel — three-tier grammar + seasonal countdown (v3.1 Phase 2 follow-up, C1 · C2; Claude Design v2.3 reference).** A small refinement to the main card, **desktop band only**:
  - **C1** — the HeroBand clock card restructures into the hero's three tiers: a **weekday eyebrow** (« JEUDI », micro mono uppercase) / the focal time (unchanged) / a **hairline + date** (« 11 juin », meta mono). It reuses the hero card's paddings, eyebrow rhythm and 1 px hairline exactly, so tier 1 and the bottom hairline land on the same lines across the two cards — the band reads as one system. The eyebrow carries a real datum (the weekday, the most-glanced clock info), not decorative structure. Size and role are unchanged (B1 still holds — no astro chips). The hero meta-line is now single-line on desktop (it *is* the band's shared hairline); the Pi/mobile TimeBlock keeps its compact stacked form and its wrapping meta-line (a 7" rail is too narrow to forbid wrapping).
  - **C2** — the solstice/equinox countdown (shown in the 14-day window before each event) now joins the date behind the hairline on desktop (« 11 juin · Solstice dans 9 j », same dim ink, no accent — ambient calendar info, not an alert); the Pi/mobile TimeBlock keeps it on its own line under the time. Copy is tightened to the generic event type (« Solstice » / « Équinoxe » instead of « Solstice de juin ») — inside the window only one event is ever upcoming, so the month is redundant. This also fixes a latent i18n bug where the English countdown rendered « in 9 day(s) » (the plural variants were never selected — `count` is now passed). New `astronomy.solarEventShort.{solstice,equinox}` keys (EN/FR/ES); shared `solarEventType()` helper. `docs/ui-layout_{en,fr}.md` updated.
  - **Polish (production pass)** — bumped the **day-mode `--c-border`** from `0.10` → `0.16` alpha: dark-on-cream at 10 % read as near-invisible, so the new C1 hairline and every light-mode card edge / grid + air-card divider were getting lost (kept below the hybrid/alert weight 0.18; day only — the other three palettes read fine). Equalized the two tier-3 line boxes (`line-height: 1.25`) so the band's shared hairline lands on the same line in both cards (the hero meta-line's box is inflated by its 1.2em moon glyph).

### Added
- **Automatic kiosk display-scale for high-density panels.** Small but dense screens (e.g. a 10.1" 800×1280 DSI panel at ~150 PPI) rendered the UI physically tiny because the compositor sat at scale 1 and Chromium laid the page out as a normal-density 1280×800 monitor — buttons came out ~7.5 mm where the 7" reference gives ~8.4 mm, and the extra CSS real-estate packed more in. A new `deploy/detect-display-scale.sh` reads the panel's **physical** size + native resolution from `wlr-randr` (Wayland/wlroots: labwc, wayfire, sway) or `xrandr` (X11), computes the diagonal **PPI**, and derives a scale that brings the **effective** density to `TARGET_PPI` (130 — the density of the 7" official touchscreen the v3 UI was tuned on), snapped to clean quarters (1.25, 1.5, …). `start-server` applies it at every boot — **`--force-device-scale-factor`** for the Chromium family (it sets the device scale absolutely, so it's correct regardless of the compositor scale), a managed **`layout.css.devicePixelRatio`** profile pref for Firefox — so the same panel gets the same physical UI size whatever the screen. The factor is emitted **only when scaling actually helps**, leaving the 7" official screens (≈ baseline) untouched, and it self-corrects if a panel is ever swapped. `install.sh` deploys the helper alongside `start-server` and **reports the detected scale** so the installer can sanity-check a panel with bad EDID dimensions. Override via **`DISPLAY_SCALE`** in `browser.conf` (`auto` / a pinned number / `off`). Detection-only on screens at/below the baseline; no client rebuild.

## [3.1.0] - 2026-06-12

Version jumps 2.19 → 3.1.0 to align with the v3.1 design-pass naming: every screen-level phase of the program (P1 toolbar, P2 main card, P3 radar controls, P4 alerts, P5 forecast, P6 settings, P7 debug) has shipped; P8 (colour-system consolidation) is captured in the roadmap. The v3 Ambient UI has been the default since 2.18 — this release closes its design pass.

### Changed
- **Main card — v3.1 Phase 2 redesign ("Carte principale"; Claude Design P2 v2.1 reference; audit F4 · F5 · F6).** The last screen-level phase of the v3.1 pass — the hero, the air readings and the metric grid get the explicit treatment, on all three layouts:
  - **Hero pyramid.** The hero card interior now reads in three tiers: a mono micro place-row (tap → the existing location popover, dotted-underline affordance), the dominant temperature (Geist Mono 500 tabular, °C top badge) with the condition icon/text and the always-on feels-like beside it, and a new **sun/moon meta-line** under a hairline. The feels-like line (shared `FeelsLikeLine`) gains the §4 **signed delta chip** — `RESSENTI 6° −6°` when |feels − temp| ≥ 2°, value alone below that (the threshold gates the ornament, never the presence — the v2.15.16 always-on ruling stands).
  - **Sun/moon migration (B1·a).** The meta-line (shared `AstroMetaLine`) is now the **only** sun/moon home on screen: sunrise → sunset (inline-SVG arrow, no Unicode glyph) opening the existing `SunDetailsPopover`, and the parametric moon glyph with the **phase name inline** (F4: "Gibbeuse croissante" — short family name "Gibbeuse" on the 7" rail, B4.7) + illumination %, opening `MoonDetailsPopover`. The HeroBand clock panel (now its own card beside the hero) and the TimeBlock keep their date/time/size/position/kitchen-clock role but **lose their astro chip rows and popovers** — one popover, one trigger per screen. Mobile keeps TimeBlock first, above the alert stack.
  - **Air card (§3 option B).** AQI leaves the metric grid for a dedicated **AirCard** above it — rows of `value · MONO LABEL · category pill · chevron` (full-row tap target opening the existing AQ detail popover). The opt-in **pollen** reading joins it as a second row (worst allergen + tier pill; tap → the 6-allergen detail, now with per-allergen tier words and "—" for null allergens); the row hides when the setting is off or upstream has no coverage — the AQI-only card is the canonical view. This also retires the F5 "IQA · IQA" unit/label duplication (the scale label is THE label now).
  - **Strict 2×2 grid with a 4th tile: pressure.** The metric grid is now always Vent · Humidité · UV · **Pression** (new Tomorrow.io field `pressureSurfaceLevel` in `CURRENT_FIELDS` — cache hash bumps once, no extra request volume). A new **pressure-unit preference** joins the Settings units block: **hPa / inHg / kPa** (kPa = the Environment Canada / MétéoMédia reading convention), persisted in `localStorage` like the other units, wired into the Metric/Imperial preset (metric → hPa, imperial → inHg), seeded from the browser locale on fresh installs, and derived once from the precipitation unit (`in` → inHg) on existing installs so US users see their barometer convention without touching Settings. No setting can break the 2×2 anymore (the pollen `spanFull` row is gone). The UV cell goes full-surface tappable with an **SVG chevron** replacing the timid ⓘ (F6); cells without a detail surface carry no chevron (an affordance that leads nowhere is worse than none). The grid's residual `:hover` affordances are dropped for the kiosk `:active` pattern (accent-soft + scale 0.985).
  - **Category tokens, nightRed gap closed.** New `--mx-cat-{good,mod,bad,vhigh}` palette tokens (normative P2 v2.1 table; good=low · mod=moderate · bad=high · vhigh=veryHigh, UV "Extrême" aliases vhigh) drive the air-card pills (ink + `color-mix` bg/border) and the UV qualifier — replacing the palette-blind inline severity colours. In **nightRed all four tiers collapse to the red family and the word alone carries the meaning** — the formerly-missing nightRed override on these cells.
  - EN/FR/ES throughout (new `metrics.pressure` + `astronomy.moonPhaseShort.*` keys); existing qualifier vocabulary reused («Risque faible / Modéré / Élevé / Très élevé») — zero new tier words. `docs/ui-layout_{en,fr}.md` updated; 453 server tests green.

### Fixed
- **Mobile maximized radar no longer hides an active government alert (P4 audit 2026-06-11).** On LayoutMobile, maximizing the radar card promotes it over the entire scroll column — AlertBanner included — so a severe alert (up to a Tornado Warning) could be active with zero on-screen indication while the user studied the radar. The `FloatingMiniBanner` chip (the same severe-alert overlay the Desktop/Pi focus modes already mount, same `useEligibleGovAlerts` eligibility) now appears inside the maximized card, right-aligned under the restore button via a new `belowControls` placement variant that clears both occupied corners (restore button top-right, Leaflet zoom stack top-left) and caps its width so the zoom stack stays tappable on narrow phones. Tapping the chip restores the mini card, revealing the full banner + detail. Mini mode is unchanged (the column's AlertBanner is visible there); no new strings (the chip renders the alert's own title); z-order stays under the dock toast. `docs/ui-layout_{en,fr}.md` updated.
- **`eccc.alerts` request counter no longer over-counts ~5× — it now counts upstream fetches, not cache hits.** Investigated from the SenseHat Pi's counters (2026-06-11): `eccc.alerts` showed 729 calls/day (~66/h — exactly the Sense HAT daemon's 60 s poll cadence) vs `nws.alerts` at 134/day, which read as "the ECCC path bypasses its cache and polls the government feed once a minute". The 5-min national-feed cache was in fact working — real upstream traffic was already ~134/day, same as NWS — but `increment("eccc", "alerts")` sat in `tryAlerts` *after* the point-in-polygon scan, so the quota counter ticked once per invocation (every HAT/kiosk poll) instead of once per HTTP request, unlike every other `requestCounter` call site (NWS returns early on a cache hit, before its increment). The increment and the `serviceStatus` record now live in `getFeed()` on actual feed refresh only, and concurrent pollers landing on an expired cache share a single in-flight fetch (new single-flight guard on the feed) instead of racing duplicates. Counter semantics are now uniform across services: a number in the Debug panel's quota table means real upstream requests. New regression suite `test/ecccAlertsCounter.test.js` (6 tests — one fetch + one tick per cache window, single-flight, failure never quota-counted; suite 447 → 453). Server-only, no client rebuild.
- **Settings rail safe-area handling aligned with the Debug rail (Phase 7 split).** Harmonization pass after comparing the two rails: the Debug rail has owned the top + left safe-area insets since its Phase 7 header removal (toolbar owns top + right), while the Settings rail still parked a lone `padding-top` on `.body` — leaving the vertical rail flush against the left screen edge, where a landscape-notched iPhone (PWA) could clip the chips under the notch. The Settings panel now mirrors the Debug split exactly: the rail owns top + left (`max(8px, env(safe-area-inset-top))` / `max(4px, env(safe-area-inset-left))`), the content side (`paneInner`) owns top + right, the horizontal mobile rail (≤ 600 px) owns all three top-adjacent insets, and the 7" compact block keeps the insets alive via `max()` for the landscape-phone case. Zero-inset devices (the kiosk, desktop browsers) resolve every `max()` to the previous constants — rendering is pixel-identical there. Icon sizing was audited at the same time and needed no change (both rails: 22 px normal / 18 px compact). The intentional rail differences stay: Debug keeps its multi-select affordances (pinned counter, corner checkboxes, MAJ badge), Settings keeps its single-select grammar and mobile horizontal flip. CSS-only.

### Added
- **Test wave for the silent-regression hotspots (quality-audit lot D) — 131 new tests, suite 291 → 422.** (1) `updateChecker`'s commit-type vocabulary — five historical silent failures are now locked as regression cases, with the classification extracted into a pure `parseCommitLine` helper (byte-identical behaviour). (2) The client radar geometry (`tierForIntensity`, compass tables, `offsetLatLon`, `buildSamplingPoints`) with the **server contracts hardcoded** so a drift on either side trips the tests. (3) A `verbatimSync` guard that makes the five verbatim-copy test files' "fails loudly on drift" claim actually true — every copied declaration is mechanically compared to its source (normalised), zero skips. (4) The AI summary's calm-day fast path (the gate that skips billed Claude calls), including the cross-file contract on the radar text's "Active" label. (5) The new AQI suite above.
- **Documentation catch-up (quality-audit lot D).** `docs/api.md` gains the 9 undocumented live endpoints (the 7-route Sense HAT control family, `GET /api/pollen`, `POST /api/debug/radar-compression-report`) with verified gating and response shapes, and its version marker is current. `CLAUDE.md`'s architecture map adds the 15 missing server modules, **corrects the inverted Sense HAT description** (no "JSON drop file" exists — the two display daemons poll `GET /api/sensehat` over HTTPS, mutually exclusive via `/api/sensehat-mode`), and adds Open-Meteo to the external-services table. The readme's stale fork version is fixed.
- **Nearby alerts — a configurable-radius "look around me" overlay (display-only).** An optional map layer that paints every active NWS/ECCC weather-alert polygon within a radius of your location over the radar. **Off by default** — a new **BottomDock toggle** (warning-triangle glyph) turns it on, with a **count badge** showing how many alerts are in range, coloured to the worst tier present (red > orange > yellow). A **persistent radius ring** marks the survey extent — a cool-blue dotted circle (red dash-dot in night-red, for night-vision), kept visually distinct from the radar risk rings. The radius is set in **Settings → Advanced → Nearby alerts** (50–100 km / 30–60 mi, default 50, persisted in `advanced.alerts.radius`), and the radar legend gains an alert-tier key + an in-radius count with an honest "+N not mapped" note for alerts that have no drawable polygon. **Strictly display-only** — it surveys, it never fires: the banner, the SenseHat pulse, and alert eligibility are untouched. Want the full text of one? Drag your location into the zone and the normal point-based banner + detail light up. Requested by a SkywarnPlus user (k5map) who wanted a settable radius rather than the point-only banner. EN/FR/ES throughout. **Tap any polygon** for a lightweight popup — the alert's source + severity + title (a scrollable, worst-first list when several overlap at the tapped spot) — with a **"Re-center here"** action that drops your location onto the tap point so the full point-based banner + `GovAlertDetail` open. The popup chrome is palette-aware (it doesn't flash white in night-red).
- **Nearby alerts overlay — Phase 1 (server groundwork).** New `GET /api/nearby-alerts?lat&lon&radiusKm` returns the active NWS/ECCC alert polygons whose area comes within a radius of the point — the data layer for the upcoming **display-only** "Nearby alerts" map overlay (configurable 50–100 km radius; full design in `docs/design-references/nearby-alerts.html`). Unlike `/api/weather-alerts` (point-in-polygon at the exact spot), it fetches broadly — NWS by the state(s) the circle spans (resolved from the circle's bounding-box corners via `api.weather.gov/points`, point→state cached 24 h), plus ECCC's whole national feed — then culls to the circle with a new hand-rolled `circleIntersectsPolygon` helper (`server/govAlertSources/_shared.js`, **no turf.js dependency**). Its edge test catches a large multi-county alert that clips the circle even though its centroid sits far outside (verified live: an OKC 100 km survey correctly picked up a Heat Advisory whose near edge reached ~42 km in while its vertices were ~99 km out). Returns `{ alerts, residualCount, radiusKm }`; `residualCount` flags alerts with no mappable polygon. Strictly display-only — feeds nothing that fires (banner / SenseHat / eligibility unchanged). New geometry regression suite `test/nearbyAlerts.test.js` (228 server tests green).
- **Debug panel now shows the Sense HAT board revision (v1 / v2).** New `detectSenseHatVersion()` reads the HAT ID-EEPROM `product_ver` exposed at `/proc/device-tree/hat/product_ver` — confirmed empirically across both fleet boards (v1 = `0x0001`, v2 = `0x0002`; both share `product_id 0x0001`). Surfaced as a `senseHat` field in `GET /api/debug`'s system info and rendered in the v3 DebugPanel's Server-config section ("none" / "aucun" / "ninguno" when no HAT is attached). Purely informational — handy for fleet inventory across the 8 Pis. Note: the official v2's only hardware deltas are an added colour sensor (I2C 0x39) and the version bump; the LED matrix (`RPi-Sense FB` at 0x46) and IMU (LSM9DS1) are unchanged, so the v1 brightness thresholds carry over and no per-version display behaviour is needed (this corrected an earlier assumption that v2 used a different LED driver). `/proc` read, side-effect-free; 4 regression tests in `test/sensehatDetect.test.js`.
- **Sense HAT radar mode — a coarse top-down radar view on the 8×8 LED matrix, plus a new `auto` mode.** The Sense HAT display selector grows from two modes (Weather / Clock) to **four: Weather · Clock · Radar · Auto**. The new modes reproject the radar analysis disk (0–100 km, or 0–50 km when extended radius is off) onto the matrix: a new pure function `buildRadarGrid()` in `radarAnalyzerCtrl.js` bins the existing per-sample intensities (the same cached pipeline that feeds `GET /api/radar-risk` and the map's per-sample dots — **no extra tile fetches**) into an 8×8 grid, each cell taking the worst-case (max) tier of the samples that land in it. The disk is *inscribed* in the square (corners fall outside and stay dark, so the lit area reads as a circular blob); north is up, east is right, and the cells use the same NEXRAD scheme-6 tier colours as the on-screen radar legend, with clear = off. **Radar mode** shows the grid whenever echoes are in range and a soft-white "you are here" centre dot when the sky is clear (so a dark matrix never looks like a fault). **Auto mode** runs a priority tree — gov alert (existing red/orange breathing pulse) > precipitation falling *on you* (the weather glyph, because radar reflectivity can't tell rain from snow) > radar "something's incoming" (echoes present but dry where you are) > plain sky state — so every weather glyph still plays when it's actually true and the radar earns a distinct "what's approaching" job instead of cannibalising the animations. Wired end-to-end: `GET /api/sensehat` now carries `mode` plus an optional `radar: { grid, litCells, radiusKm }` (computed only in radar/auto mode); `sensehatModeCtrl.js` accepts the four modes (clock runs its own daemon, weather/radar/auto share the weather daemon and differ only in the script's per-poll personality, so switching among them needs no service restart); `tools/sensehat_weather.py` gains the radar render path, the arbitration tree, a radar-specific night brightness floor (`BRIGHTNESS_RADAR_NIGHT = 0.6`, since a lone tier-1 cyan pixel at the default 0.35 falls under the LED visibility floor), and `--state radar` / `--state radar-clear` test slugs for on-hardware colour/brightness/rotation validation. A terminal previewer (`tools/radar_grid_preview.js`, ANSI + PNG + `--live LAT LON`) renders synthetic and real grids without a Pi. The four-way selector is inline-trilingual (Météo/Horloge/Radar/Auto) in the ambient SettingsPanel. Server + tools + client; built clean (`npm run prod`, 0 errors), 206 server tests green.
  - **Hardware-validation follow-up (live on a Pi during a Texas Panhandle storm):** (1) **Fixed the four-option selector overflow** — the new `Auto` button spilled outside the segmented control's border because the four `nowrap` buttons couldn't compress below their combined min-content inside a single `grid4` cell; the Sense HAT block now spans the full row (new `.gridSenseHat` modifier) so the seg gets the whole pane width and its `flex:1` buttons share it evenly, with the brightness slider stacking below. (2) **Added a "Luminosité radar · nuit" slider** (radar/auto modes) so a fully-bright RGB matrix isn't overwhelming in a dark room — mirrors the clock-brightness control: new `advanced.sensehat.radarBrightness` (default 60 %), served in the `/api/sensehat` `radarBrightness` field and applied by `_render_radar` as the night multiplier (daytime stays full); `GET`/`POST /api/sensehat-radar-brightness` persist + restart `pi-sensehat.service` for immediate effect. Orientation (north up) and tier colours (blue/orange/red crisp; green↔yellow is the NEXRAD scheme's inherent close pair) confirmed on real LEDs.
- **Optional advisory-level (yellow-tier) alerts.** A new per-device toggle in **Settings → Local preferences → "Show advisory alerts"** surfaces NWS/ECCC advisory alerts (Flood / Heat / Wind Advisory — CAP severity *minor/low*) that the banner stack hides by default. **Off by default**, persisted to `localStorage` like the other local prefs, so the quieter red/orange-only default is unchanged for everyone who doesn't opt in. Requested by a flood-prone user (k5map, TX) whose Flood Advisories frequently escalate to Warnings. The preference threads through the shared `selectEligibleGovAlerts` filter (via `useEligibleGovAlerts`), so all four banner surfaces agree; the yellow severity strip was added to `AlertBanner` + `FloatingMiniBanner` (the severity chip + mini-cards already styled it) via a new palette token `--c-advisory` — the alert-gold `#f0c000` the map's alert-zone polygon already paints, so the banner strip and the map zone match, collapsing to red in night-red like the other severity strips. Label deliberately says "advisory", never "yellow".

### Changed
- **Radar overlay typography — larger bases, and the font-size preference finally reaches the map.** The S/M/L preference is applied as `zoom` on the scrollable subtrees only (deliberate — the map must stay at native resolution), so the Phase 3 overlays never followed it; their mock-calibrated micro-type (8-9.5 px) also read too small on real screens (maintainer feedback). Every text size on the radar legend, timeline, on-map radius chips and focus toast now computes as `calc(base × --c-font-scale)` with bases raised ~1-1.5 px — labels follow the preference while geometries (40 px buttons, 4 px track, hit areas) stay fixed. The legal attribution strip stays fixed-size (it must fit its 16 px corridor). On narrow rails the forecast panel drops its decorative PRÉVISIONS title (it was truncating to "PRÉ…" and would starve the header at fontSize L); the metric tabs carry the identity there.
- **Forecast panel — v3.1 Phase 5 redesign ("Prévisions, nommées"; audit F9 · F13).** The chart slab becomes a named **Forecast panel**: period pills (`24 h` / `5 jours`) sit next to the PRÉVISIONS title — period is orthogonal to metric, any combination works — and four **labelled metric tabs** (Temp · Vent · Précip · Heures/Jours, icon + word) replace the cryptic carousel dots (F9). Each metric gets a dedicated mono-metric chart (new `MetricChart`): **Temp** = accent line + 10 % area fill + value labels on the max/min key points (new dep `chartjs-plugin-datalabels`); **Vent** = speed line + dashed teal **gust** line + a wind-direction arrow row under the axis (new Tomorrow.io fields `windGust`/`windDirection` hourly, `windGustMax`/`windDirection` daily — same request volume, cache hash bumps once); **Précip** = accumulation bars + dashed probability line. Axes finally read clean (F13): `14°` instead of `14 C`, with non-obvious units (kph/mm) shown once on the top tick. **Numeric summary pills** under each chart (max · min · peak/trough times; avg · gusts · dominant compass; total · max prob · heaviest hour) so the story reads without the curve, plus line-style key pills. An optional **"Précip" overlay chip** (maintainer decision) superimposes subdued accumulation bars + probability on the Temp/Vent tabs — the continuation of the old paired charts, off by default. The hour-by-hour grid keeps its field-tested 8→24-cell density (maintainer decision over the mock's 16) and its cells now show **mm accumulation** instead of probability ("0.8 mm" / "·" when dry). Per-metric colours come straight from the Phase 5 reference (orange/teal/blue; all-red in nightRed). The 5-day declension (not specced in the mock) mirrors the 24 h tabs, with the grid tab reading "Jours". Maximize keeps its mechanics, 44 px hit area, and adopts the shared bracket-icon pair. The legacy paired HourlyChart/DailyChart stay untouched for the v2 tree until its scheduled removal. EN/FR/ES (+ new 8-point compass keys); `docs/ui-layout_{en,fr}.md` updated.
- **Radar map controls — v3.1 Phase 3 redesign (Claude Design v2.1 reference; audit F7 · F8 · F20).** The floating controls over the Leaflet map get the explicit treatment. **(1) Timeline** becomes a full-width bottom bar (rail-aware right inset) — transport cluster (play/pause, step ±1, 1×/2×/4×) with inline-SVG glyphs, the frame timestamp now carries a **"now-tag" chip** ("Il y a 6 min" / "Maintenant" — never a bare `−6 min`, F8) that flips to a dashed **"Prévision · +N min"** chip on nowcast frames, a frame-count sub-line, the return-to-now pill, and a **source/freshness chip** ("RainViewer · 10 min", cadence derived from the live frame spacing) that turns warn-tinted when the frame-list refresh fails (no silent staleness). The track gains a labelled **"Maintenant" marker** at the past→nowcast boundary, a hatched **scrubbable** future zone (extrapolation ≠ observation), and runtime-derived tick labels (−2 h … +30 min). The scrub surface is still the field-hardened invisible native range input (pointer-capture + keyboard a11y preserved). **(2) Legend** is rebuilt bottom-left as three sections — *Rayons d'analyse* (unit-aware, outer ring only when extended radius is on; F7/F20), *Précipitations* (the **real RainViewer colour-scheme-6 six-segment bar**, identical in all four palettes — nightRed keeps the true tile colours, only the chrome collapses to red), *Alertes à proximité* (tier key + honest count) — and **on-map "50 km / 100 km" chips** appear at the rings' SE intersection (hidden on mobile and past zoom 13, like the rings). On the 7" kiosk with the timeline open, the legend collapses to an **"(i) Légende" chip** opening the full legend as an overlay (scrim + ✕ + Escape) instead of vanishing; the mobile layout gets a compact strip whose (i) opens the same overlay as a bottom sheet. **(3) Zoom** goes 40 × 40 px (36 mobile) with localized tooltips. **(4) The radar-focus toggle** leaves the Leaflet bar and becomes a standalone 40 px button below the zoom stack, with a cross-platform four-corner-bracket SVG pair (the old ⛶ glyph rendered inconsistently across platforms) and a confirmation toast; the **mobile card's maximize button adopts the same icon pair** (Carbon arrows retired). **(5) Attribution** anchors flush bottom-right at the dock edge — visible in every state, including the mobile mini-card. New radar-scoped CSS tokens (`--rc-*`, `--map-*`) live in `WeatherMap/styles.css` per palette (deliberately not in `ui/tokens.js`); kiosk surfaces keep the no-hover rule (`:active` only). EN/FR/ES throughout; `docs/ui-layout_{en,fr}.md` updated.
- **Debug panel — Phase 7 mockup parity (F25–F28), the deferred visual decisions land.** Four refinements from the Phase 7 design audit, closing the panel's parity gap with the Claude Design mockup:
  - **F25 — the multi-select is now visible.** The rail head shows a "Shown N / 5" counter pill and each entry carries a corner checkbox (empty ring when unpinned, accent fill + ✓ when pinned), so the rail no longer reads as single-select tabs. Decorative only (`aria-pressed` already carries the state); both shrink on the 7" kiosk; no hover fills (Pi Chromium sticky-fill lesson).
  - **F26 — TRUE/FALSE tags become dot+word status pills.** DEBUG and ALLOW_REMOTE render as a tinted `● ON / ACTIF / ACTIVO` pill; the word carries the meaning so night-red stays legible. ALLOW_REMOTE keeps its warn-orange when on.
  - **F27 — connectivity becomes a hero card atop the Server bucket.** 36 px coloured LED (TCP tier; HTTPS as fallback), plain-words state ("Online · fast network" / slow / degraded / offline), colour-coded TCP + HTTPS readings with the probe target, and the two existing threshold scales moved inside the card. Deliberate mockup divergence: the dual TCP/HTTPS scale (field-tested, tells link-bound from TLS-bound slowness) is kept over the mockup's single scale.
  - **F28 — the raw Dependabot URL becomes a labelled CTA** ("Check security alerts on GitHub" + launch icon + a dim destination meta line). The direct-link exception for the localhost-only Debug panel is now codified in `CLAUDE.md` next to the QR-only rule.
- **Map zoom cap raised 16 → 18 — building-level detail is back.** The May-2026 cap traded zoom depth for stability after an M4 iPad Pro froze at z=17; the AppContext slice split revealed (and fixed) the dominant cause — every zoom step used to re-render the entire app through the monolithic context, now it only re-renders radar-slice consumers, and max-zoom interaction is field-confirmed smooth on the kiosk. `keepBuffer: 2` + `updateWhenIdle` stay in place against Safari's tile-memory pressure (the incident's other half). The radar overlays deliberately keep their z=12 caps — those are DATA-resolution bounds (RainViewer native z=8, ECCC ~1 km/px), not perf caps; past them the radar would only be stretched pixels. **Validation gate before any further raise: a sustained z=9→18 zoom/pan session on the original iPad.**
