- **The AppContext is split into seven frequency-grouped slices (context-split step 2b) — the structural fix for the app-wide re-render cascade.** The monolithic 197-entry context is now composed of seven memoized slices, each exposed as its own context: `AppActionsContext` (all 84 stable functions — its identity effectively never changes, so action-only consumers stop re-rendering entirely), `SystemContext`, `LocationContext`, `UiPrefsContext`, `WeatherDataContext`, `AlertsContext` and `RadarStateContext` (the hot radar state). The legacy `AppContext` union remains as a compatibility layer built BY CONSTRUCTION as the spread of the seven slices (disjointness and completeness mechanically verified — 197 = 197), so unmigrated consumers keep working unchanged. Seventeen hot consumers migrated to their slices (WeatherMap + RadarTimeline/RadarLegend, both charts, HeroBand/HeroCompact/TimeBlock, MetricsGrid/IndoorBlock/AiSummaryInline, ControlButtons, the alert cluster, `useEligibleGovAlerts`, both `ui/hybrid.js` hooks) — **and, decisively, the five tree ANCESTORS too** (`App`, `AmbientLayers`, the three layouts): the adversarial review caught that without the ancestors, the union re-mint still re-rendered the whole tree from the root by parent propagation, nullifying the narrow subscriptions below. With the root cut over to the cold slices, a radar-risk poll now re-renders only radar consumers, a weather poll only weather consumers — the clock, dock and charts no longer repaint on every context heartbeat. A cross-check auditor verified all 44 slice destructures field-by-field against the membership map (0 mismatches — the failure mode here is a silent runtime `undefined`, invisible at build time). Remaining on the union: the long tail (Settings/Debug panels, layouts, v2 tree), to migrate opportunistically.
- **AppContext identity churn eliminated (context-split step 2a).** The functions step 1 inventoried as still re-minting on hot state are now permanently identity-stable: the four UI toggles (functional updates where pure, mirror refs where side-effecting — localStorage writes and cross-panel closes stay outside the updaters per StrictMode purity), the gov-alert cycle/select pair (govAlerts mirror ref — the 10-min poll no longer mints identities), and `buildAdvancedSubtree` + its 8 dependent savers/sliders (one `advancedStateRef` mirroring the 17 `advanced.*` atoms). Behaviour is byte-identical on every synchronous path; one deliberate, beneficial delta on the debounced paths: a delayed `advanced` PATCH now reads sibling values at FIRE time instead of schedule time, so a cross-write landing inside the 500 ms debounce window is no longer clobbered by stale sibling values (a latent fix of the documented 2026-05-23 clobber class). Adversarial-reviewed: mirror writers traced exhaustively, no timing window found.
- **AppContext function identities are now stable (step 1 of the context split).** All 42 action functions the provider exposes through the context value (35 in `AppContext.js`, 6 in `useUiPreferences`, 1 in `useScreenSaver`) are wrapped in `useCallback` with verified exhaustive dependencies — previously every provider render minted fresh identities, defeating any downstream `React.memo`, re-running consumer effects keyed on these functions, and blocking the upcoming context-slice work. Zero behaviour change (adversarial-reviewed for stale closures: none); two obsolete `eslint-disable` directives became removable because the effects can now list the memoized callbacks honestly. The functions whose identity still churns on hot state (`buildAdvancedSubtree` and its 8 dependents, the toggle quartet, the gov-alert pair) are inventoried in the PR as the step-2 worklist.
- **The v3->v2 boundary is untangled - the legacy-tree removal is now a safe, mechanical operation.** The audit found that executing the documented v2-tree removal would have BROKEN the default v3 UI: three active v3 components imported from the doomed directories. Fixed by relocation - `ControlButtons` and `weatherCharts/` (Hourly+Daily charts) now live under `ambient/` (they are v3's dock and chart tabs; the v2 InfoPanel/WeatherInfo import them from their new home until removal), and `exportDebugCsv` (+ its `SERVICE_LABELS`/`formatUptime` helpers, shared by both debug surfaces) moved to `ui/exportDebugCsv.js`. The removal list in `CLAUDE.md` now matches the audited scope: the orphan v2-only satellites (root `AlertBanner`, `GovAlertDetail`, `UvAqiBadges`, `RangeSlider`, `Spinner`) joined it, and two genuinely dead files are deleted outright (`CurrentWeather/openweathermap_index.js` and `WeatherMap/WeatherLayer.js` - OpenWeatherMap leftovers imported by nothing).
- **Render-performance pass on the four hottest client paths (quality-audit lot C).** All four are invisible-by-design — same pixels, fewer wasted cycles on Pi-class hardware: (1) **HourlyChart** no longer rebuilds its chart.js options object inline on every render — react-chartjs-2 compares options by reference, so every app-wide re-render (context polls, dock toggles, zoom) forced a full canvas re-layout of an identical chart; the options are now memoized like DailyChart's. (2) **WeatherMap's Leaflet layer props are referentially stable** — `markerPosition`, the radius-ring `pathOptions`, the sampling-point grid (161-481 geodesic computations previously redone in the render body) and the rendered dot markers are memoized, and `RiskRing` memoizes its ring layers + skips re-rendering entirely on unchanged props; previously each render triggered no-op `setLatLng`/`setStyle` SVG work at 1-4 Hz during radar animation. (3) **Mobile pull-to-refresh no longer re-renders the whole layout at ~60 Hz during the gesture** — the pull distance moved out of React state into a ref applied imperatively to the two translated elements; only the discrete states (indicator mounted / armed / refreshing) go through React. (4) **HeroBand's clock ticks once per minute, aligned on the minute boundary** (the display has no seconds; the old 1 Hz tick re-ran the hero's astronomy + formatting work 60× too often), and its three `Intl.DateTimeFormat` instances are memoized instead of rebuilt per render. Client-only; no API change.
- **Dependabot version bumps now wait out a 7-day supply-chain cooldown.** Prompted by the June 2026 "Miasma" worm wave (compromised contributor accounts pushing malicious releases to GitHub/npm/PyPI): worm-published package versions are typically detected and yanked within days, so Dependabot now waits 7 days after a release before proposing it as a version bump (`cooldown.default-days: 7` in `dependabot.yml`, all three ecosystems — server npm, client npm, github-actions). Security updates are exempt by design — Dependabot ignores cooldown for advisory-driven PRs, so CVE fixes (and the existing auto-merge that lands patch/minor ones) still flow immediately. CI config only — no app code change.
- **Dropped the Google Fonts CDN — the app is now fully self-contained on Geist (privacy + offline resilience).** Every page load used to fetch `Rubik` from `fonts.googleapis.com` / `fonts.gstatic.com`, leaking the Pi's egress IP to Google on every boot and silently falling back to a system font whenever the Pi had no internet at boot. The hardcoded `<link>` is gone from `index.html`, and the ~15 surfaces that referenced `Rubik` (the legacy v2 tree — `Settings`, `InfoPanel`, `WeatherInfo`, `AiSummary`, `UpdateModal`, `Debug`, the Chart.js axis labels — plus the shared `GovAlertDetail`, `WeatherMap`, `IndoorTemperature`) now use **Geist**, which was already self-hosted (`client/src/ui/fonts.css`, OFL woff2 under `src/ui/fonts/`) and already the font of the default v3 "Ambient Layers" UI. **This unifies the whole app on one family with zero new files and zero added bundle weight** — and it's a deliberate, modest visual change to those secondary surfaces (Geist is more neutral than the rounder Rubik). A side benefit: Rubik used to render in Light 300 + synthesised bold (only `wght@300` was ever loaded); Geist ships real 400/500/700, so weights are now genuine. Self-hosted woff2 means identical rendering with or without connectivity and a deterministic first paint. Rebuilt `client/dist` (and removed a long-stale orphaned chunk the build had never cleaned). Client-only; no API or server change.
- **Severe-tagged Watches no longer render as red — capped at the orange "Watch" tier.** NWS routinely tags Flood / Flash Flood / Tornado *Watches* with CAP severity `Severe`, which mapped on severity alone painted them red and made a Watch shout exactly as loud as a Warning on the banner, the nearby-alerts overlay and the SenseHat pulse (observed live on a Paducah KY Flood Watch). `capWatchSeverity` in `server/govAlertSources/_shared.js` now caps a watch's normalised severity at `moderate` (keyed off the event name — "…watch" / ECCC "veille"), so the tier drops to orange **and** the SeverityChip word becomes "Watch / Veille" (it re-derives from severity) — every surface stays consistent, and watches sort below real warnings. Non-watch alerts are untouched. Server-only (no client rebuild). Verified live: four active KY/TN Flood Watches now return `severity: moderate / tier: orange`. Closes the ROADMAP "alert tier maps on CAP severity alone" item. New regression suite `test/watchTier.test.js` (231 server tests green).
- **Sense HAT radar tier-1 is now a true blue (0,110,255) instead of the legend's cyan, for LED legibility.** Field-confirmed on both v1 and v2: on the 8×8 LEDs, tier-1 cyan (0,208,208) and tier-2 green (0,200,0) differ only in the blue channel and read as the same blue-green — verified with the `--state radar` demo grid (the user read the known cyan pixel as green). Interestingly the long-suspected weak pair (green↔yellow) turned out fine; the actual confusable pair was cyan↔green. Switching tier-1 to a true blue (less green) separates it from tier-2 at a glance ("beaucoup plus visible" on the real matrix). A deliberate per-device divergence from the on-screen NEXRAD-scheme-6 legend (which keeps cyan to match the actual radar tiles) — documented at `RADAR_TIER_RGB` in `tools/sensehat_weather.py`. The warm tiers (3-6) were already well separated and are unchanged. Tools only — no client rebuild; restart `pi-sensehat` to pick up.
- **Sense HAT brightness sliders: applied live (no restart), debounce bumped, and floored at a consistent 20 %.** A field session on both HAT versions surfaced three related issues, fixed together:
  - **Radar brightness now applies live, without restarting the service.** The radar daemon re-reads `radarBrightness` from `settings.json` on a ~1 s cadence (new `read_radar_brightness()`, mirroring `horloge.py`), and `POST /api/sensehat-radar-brightness` only persists — it no longer restarts `pi-sensehat.service`. The old restart-on-change left a **stuck black frame** when restarts overlapped on fast slider moves (the daemon held the last-written frame while the restarted instance re-fetched before redrawing). Reproduced as "drop the slider low then back up → stays black until you switch modes"; most visible on the v1 HAT, which is less forgiving of the black gap. The radar render-cache key now includes the brightness so a live change forces a redraw.
  - **Brightness-save debounce raised 500 → 1500 ms.** The clock slider still restarts its (separate) daemon for immediacy; the longer debounce coalesces fast moves into one restart so the same restart-storm can't bite there.
  - **Both sliders floored at 20 % on a shared 0–100 scale.** Field-measured: the LED activation threshold is ~50 effective, so at 20 % the heavier tiers stay lit (red 230 × 0.2 ≈ 46) while tier-1 cyan just goes dark (42), and below ~15 % nothing shows — identical on v1 and v2 (the earlier apparent v1/v2 difference was the restart-storm artefact, not the hardware). The clock slider previously started at 0 % (could be driven fully black) and the radar slider at a different min, so the same "%" sat at different thumb positions; pinning both to `min=20` makes the thumb track consistently and keeps every selectable value visible. The v1/v2 detection added earlier stays purely informational (Debug panel) — no per-version floor is needed.
- **Debug panel (v3 ambient) — Phase 7 layout refresh.** The title header is gone and its actions are reorganised around the panel's task structure:
  - **Close moved to the bottom of the rail**, reusing the Settings-panel grammar: a dim, divider-separated exit that never carries the active-section accent, so it doesn't read as a sixth selectable section.
  - **Persistent action toolbar** at the top of the content pane — a live-status dot + an "Updated HH:MM:SS" timestamp (stamped on every `/api/debug` fetch so the displayed diagnostics carry their freshness) plus the **Refresh** action, all of which stay visible while the content scrolls.
  - **Export CSV and Check-for-updates moved into the About bucket** — the panel's housekeeping section — co-locating the update check with its result (local/latest SHA, version, Install). A **"MAJ" badge** now lights up on the About rail entry when an update is waiting, for discoverability.
  - The existing install flow (`POST /api/update` via the Update modal, with its detached-HEAD / non-master / dirty-tree pre-flight gating and `localhostOnly` boundary) is unchanged — Phase 7 only restyles the surface that triggers it.
  - **Rail icons are now inline SVGs** (Iconify) instead of raw Unicode glyphs. The glyphs (`⌬`/`◇` …) rendered at font-dependent sizes across platforms — fine on the Pi's Trixie font, but shrinking to a thin fallback on macOS — so Server/Services looked inconsistent. SVGs render identically everywhere (same lesson as the moon-glyph fix).
- **Rail exit divider is now visible (Settings + Debug panels).** The thin separator line above the "Close" button used `--c-border` (~0.10 alpha), which fell below the visibility floor on the cream day surface, so the divider was effectively absent. Both panels' `.railClose` now use `--c-border-hybrid` (~2× the alpha) so the separator actually reads, in all four palettes.

### Fixed
- **Air-quality math: the EPA band gaps are closed, and an off-scale 1-hour SO₂ reading now reports AQI 200 instead of nothing.** `epaAqiFromConcentration` required a concentration to fall inside a breakpoint band, but the EPA tables have deliberate gaps between bands (PM2.5 ]12.0, 12.1[, PM10 ]54, 55[, CO ]4.4, 4.5[ …) and the OpenAQ path feeds raw/converted floats — a PM2.5 reading of 12.05 µg/m³ landed in the gap and returned **null** (no AQI shown at all). Per EPA's own method, concentrations are now **truncated to each table's decimal precision before lookup** (with a 1e-6 noise-kill so IEEE-754 artifacts like 55.399999... don't truncate below a breakpoint). Also fixed a code/comment mismatch: 1-h SO₂ above 304 ppb returned null while the comment claimed a cap — per EPA (AQI > 200 for SO₂ is only defined on the 24-h average), it now reports 200. The adversarial review then caught that the PM2.5 breakpoint table itself was the **pre-May-2024** one (EPA tightened Good from 12.0 to 9.0 µg/m³ in 89 FR 16202) — updated to the current table, which also ends the OpenAQ-vs-AirNow category divergence around 9-12 µg/m³. New `test/airQuality.test.js` locks the current official EPA reference points, the gap values, AQHI/IQA category boundaries and `haversineKm`.
- **The v3 alert banner carries its leading source badge again (project-rule compliance).** The "every banner identifies its authority at a glance" rule (ECCC/NWS official vs RADAR derived) was honoured by the radar branch but lost by the gov-alert branch, where the source only appeared in a meta chip below. The `SourceBadge` now leads the banner's top row, clustered with the severity chip (chevron stays flush right).
- **Gov-alert detail no longer shows "What's happening" twice (bug C4).** NWS advisories carry both a `* WHAT...` block (the danger itself) and a `* IMPACTS...` block (what the danger does — "Frost could harm sensitive outdoor vegetation"), and the section parser folded both into the same `hazard` type, so the expanded detail rendered two identical "What's happening" headings (observed on the Klamath Falls Frost Advisory, reported by k5map). `IMPACTS` (and `Conséquences` / `Impactos`) now map to their own `impact` section type with a distinct icon (circled !) and heading — "Possible impacts" / "Impacts possibles" / "Posibles impactos". Parser + test copy synced, with a frost-advisory-shaped regression test locking the WHAT/IMPACTS distinction.
- **`settings.json` is now written atomically (tmp-write + fsync + rename).** Every write path used to truncate-then-rewrite the file in place, leaving two real failure windows on Pi hardware: a power cut mid-write could truncate the one file holding all six API keys + the Homebridge credentials (deconfigured kiosk at reboot — escalated as a ROADMAP tech-debt item by the 2026-06 security session), and the Python Sense HAT daemons — which re-read `settings.json` every ~1 s for the live brightness sliders — could catch a half-written file and fail the JSON parse (flagged independently by the 2026-06 quality audit; one fix closes both findings). All five write paths (the four HTTP handlers + the internal `patchAdvancedSubKey`) now funnel through a single atomic writer: serialise to a sibling `.tmp` (0600 from birth), fsync to the SD card, `rename()` over the target — readers see either the old complete file or the new complete file, never an in-between. Regression tests included.
- **Server logs now survive a reboot — moved off `/tmp` (a tmpfs on Trixie) to the XDG state dir.** `install.sh` pinned the systemd log to `/tmp/weather-server.log`; on RPi OS Trixie / Debian 13 `/tmp` lives in RAM, so the log and all its rotated copies vanished at every reboot — exactly the post-freeze forensic moment they exist for (the V3D incident pattern: kiosk freezes → user reboots → evidence gone). New destination: `~/.local/state/pi-weather-station/server.log`. The drop-in, the logrotate config, the debug-panel reader (which now checks the new path, the legacy `/tmp` path, and the macOS repo path), `docs/logs.md` and `CLAUDE.md` all updated together. **Fleet migration: the new path applies the next time `bash deploy/install.sh` runs on each Pi** — until then existing installs keep logging to `/tmp` and the debug panel still finds them.
- **Log rotation actually runs for non-`pi` users, and the "daily" promise is real.** The static logrotate config hardcoded `su pi pi` — on any install where the user isn't `pi` (Bookworm/Trixie ask at imaging; the Debian/Ubuntu/openSUSE desktops install.sh explicitly supports), logrotate rejected the directive and **silently skipped the config forever**, letting the log grow without bound. The repo file is now a template (`__LOG_FILE__`/`__USER__`/`__GROUP__`) that `install.sh` instantiates with the real user, group and path. Also `size 10M` → `maxsize 10M`: plain `size` *replaces* the daily schedule (rotation only ever fired on the 10 MB threshold), while `maxsize` guarantees the daily cycle with the 10 MB cap evaluated at each logrotate run (daily on stock RPi OS). On macOS (no rotation mechanism), `install.sh` now truncates a >50 MB `server.log` at re-install.
- **Re-running `install.sh` no longer silently resets ALLOW_REMOTE and DEBUG to "disabled".** The prompts defaulted to "no" regardless of the current state, so re-running the installer to add a feature (Sense HAT, brightness — an explicitly supported flow) and pressing Enter through the questions turned off remote access and the debug panel on a configured Pi. Both prompts now read the live state from the systemd drop-ins (or the launchd plist on macOS) and default to keeping it, displaying `[currently: enabled/disabled]`.
- **`install.sh` "Reconfigure settings?" is now a merge, not a destructive rewrite.** Answering "y" used to regenerate `settings.json` from the prompt answers alone: a field left empty reset `weatherApiKey`/`mapApiKey` to the `"key"` placeholder, dropped the optional keys and lat/lon, and erased every block the prompts don't cover — including `indoorTemperature` (Homebridge credentials) and the whole `advanced` tree (Sense HAT mode + brightness, AI flags, radar opacities). Now existing values survive unless replaced, unknown blocks pass through verbatim, and the previous file is backed up to `settings.json.bak` (mode 0600) first.
- **`install.sh` hardening for the unhappy paths.** (1) `npm ci` failing mid-flight (it deletes `node_modules` wholesale first) used to kill the script with no explanation, leaving the next reboot to crash-loop into a black kiosk — it now fails with a clear recovery message. (2) The NodeSource setup script was piped `curl | sudo bash`: a curl failure "succeeded" silently (no pipefail) and the following `apt-get install nodejs` pulled the distro's Node instead of v22 — now download-then-execute with an explicit error, plus a post-install version re-check that aborts before `npm ci` rather than failing cryptically later. (3) The service is `restart`ed (not `start`ed, a no-op over a running service) so a re-install actually runs the freshly-pulled code. (4) A stale `nvm.conf` drop-in is removed when the install isn't nvm-based (it pointed `ExecStart` at a deleted `nvm.sh` → crash-loop). (5) `sudo cp` of the logrotate config is guarded — a declined password no longer aborts phases 6-8. (6) The labwc autostart file is appended to, never clobbered (user entries survive). (7) The committed launchd plist uses `__INSTALL_DIR__` placeholders instead of a baked machine-specific path, matching its own header instructions.
- **`uninstall.sh` completeness.** It never touched `pi-sensehat-clock.service` — if the Sense HAT was in clock mode, the LED matrix kept displaying the time *after* "Uninstall complete", driven by an orphaned unit pointing at a deleted script. It also left `/etc/logrotate.d/weather-server` behind, and — the nastiest one — declining the "Are you sure?" confirmation for nvm removal **still scrubbed every nvm line from the shell profiles** (an `&&`/`||` fall-through), leaving nvm on disk but unloadable so `node`/`npm` vanished from new shells despite an explicit "no". All three fixed; the script also cleans the server logs (XDG + legacy `/tmp`, including rotated copies) and offers to disable systemd lingering (opt-in — it may serve other user services).
- **Sense HAT weather reads hit the shared Tomorrow.io cache again (was: silent quota burn on every poll).** `sensehatCtrl._weatherFromCache` still built the pre-2026-05 3-segment cache key (`current:lat:lon`) while proxyCtrl has been writing 4-segment keys (`type:fieldsHash:lat:lon`) since the fields-hash migration — so the shared-cache read missed **forever** and every `/api/sensehat` poll (60 s LED-daemon cadence) fell back to an internal `/api/weather/current` call, quietly consuming Tomorrow.io quota (25 req/h free tier) and likely contributing to the observed fleet 429s. Same bug class that had already hit the AI summary's paragraph 2; aiSummaryCtrl was migrated to proxyCtrl's `getCacheKey` at the time, sensehatCtrl was missed. Now imports the canonical key builder, with a new contract-regression suite (`test/sensehatWeatherKey.test.js`) so the next key-schema change breaks tests instead of the Sense HAT. Found by the 2026-06-10 quality audit (lot A).
- **Stale-position responses can no longer overwrite fresh data after a map pan.** Four location-keyed pollers (gov alerts + nearby alerts in `AppContext`, radar risk in `WeatherMap`, AI summary in both `AiSummaryInline` and the v2 `AiSummary`) lacked the in-flight cancellation flag the AQI/pollen effects already had — a slow response for the *previous* position could resolve after the effect re-ran for the new one and display the old location's alerts/rings/narrative for up to a full poll interval (10-15 min for alerts and the Claude summary). All five sites now use the same `cancelled`-flag pattern; pure harmonisation, no new design.
- **Mobile: the radar-timeline scrubber preference is no longer silently flipped off on every mount.** LayoutMobile's auto-deactivate effect (scrubber off when the radar card is minimised) also fired on the mount pass — where the lifecycle effect initialises `mobileRadarMaximized` to `false` — and `toggleRadarTimelineVisible()` persists to `localStorage`, so every mobile session start overwrote the saved preference (defeating the "default true so first-time users see the timeline" intent). The effect now keys on the maximized→mini *transition* (previous-value ref), not the value.
- **Sense HAT daemons: an active alert pulse now survives a server restart, and the LED matrix clears on service stop.** (1) `horloge.py` treated a failed `/api/sensehat` poll identically to "no active alert" — one missed poll during an in-app update restart logged "Alert cleared" and killed a live tornado pulse. Fetch failures are now distinguished (sentinel) and the last known alert state is kept through outages, bounded by a 15-min staleness window; `sensehat_weather.py` (which already kept state, but indefinitely) gets the same bounded window. (2) `sensehat_weather.py` had no SIGTERM handler, so `systemctl --user stop pi-sensehat` left the matrix frozen on the last weather frame indefinitely; it now clears the display and exits 0 (mirroring `horloge.py`), in daemon and test modes alike. (3) Mode switches (`POST /api/sensehat-mode`) are serialised through a promise chain — two rapid toggles could interleave their stop/start sequences at the systemctl boundaries and leave **both** daemons driving the matrix.
- **Settings API: boolean `false`, `0` and `""` are now accepted as setting values, and a malformed body gets a clean 400.** `setSetting`'s `!val` guard silently rejected legitimate falsy values, and a request without a parsed body threw a TypeError into the default HTML error handler instead of returning JSON.
- **Indoor temperature: enabling the feature in Settings now takes effect within one poll interval (was: required a server restart).** The Homebridge polling loop only started when `indoorTemperature.enabled` was already true at boot; the interval now always runs and each tick re-reads settings (no-op while disabled, `unref()`ed so it never holds the process). A disabled tick also drops the cached reading so re-enabling with a different sensor can't briefly serve the old sensor's values.
- **"Extended radius" toggle label now follows the distance unit.** It hardcoded "(100 km)" even in imperial mode, where the outer radar ring is actually 60 mi; the label now reads "(100 km)" / "(60 mi)" per the user's unit (Settings → Advanced → AI · radar analysis). Pre-existing label, surfaced by imperial-unit field use; client-only one-liner.
- **Radar brightness slider now affects the matrix in daytime too (was night-only).** The "Luminosité radar · nuit" slider only scaled the radar grid at night — in daytime `_render_radar` always used full brightness, so the slider had no visible effect and read as broken (reported at 20 % in the morning showing full-brightness LEDs). Unlike the radar slider, the clock slider always applied, which is why it "worked". Made the radar brightness apply in **both day and night** (same as the clock), dropped the `· nuit` from the label ("Luminosité radar" / "Radar brightness" / "Brillo radar"), and renamed the internals accordingly (`BRIGHTNESS_RADAR_NIGHT` → `BRIGHTNESS_RADAR_DEFAULT`, `radar_night` → `radar_brightness`). `is_day` is no longer part of the radar render-cache key (it no longer changes the brightness). Tools + client + server-comments + docs.
- **Toggling an advanced setting no longer wipes the Sense HAT mode + brightness.** Observed live: with the Sense HAT in **Radar** mode, enabling **"Sampling points"** (`advanced.ai.showSamplingPoints`) made the matrix fall back to **Weather** after one poll (~30 s). Cause: the client persists advanced flags by PATCHing the **whole** `advanced` blob — rebuilt from React state by `buildAdvancedSubtree()` — and `PATCH /setting` with `key: "advanced"` replaces the entire blob. But `buildAdvancedSubtree()` has no `sensehat` section (the Sense HAT mode + clock/radar brightness are owned by a separate hook + the `/api/sensehat-*` endpoints), so every advanced-flag toggle silently erased `advanced.sensehat` → `resolveMode` fell back to its `weather` default and the saved brightnesses were lost too. Fixed server-side with a pure `preserveServerOwnedAdvanced()` helper in `settingsCtrl`: when an incoming `advanced` PATCH omits `sensehat`, the existing sub-block is spliced back in. The fix lives at the server boundary (not by teaching the client to send `sensehat`) so the Sense HAT block stays owned by exactly one writer — no two-source race. 4 regression tests in `test/settingsCtrl.test.js`. Server-only change — no client rebuild; deploy with `git pull` + `systemctl --user restart pi-weather-server`.
- **Sense HAT settings no longer appear on Pis without a HAT.** The availability probe (`sensehatModeCtrl.probeAvailable`) ran `python3 -c "import sense_hat"`, which only proves the **Python package** is installed — not that the **hardware** is attached. The `sense_hat` module imports fine on any host with the apt package (and `deploy/install.sh` installs it during setup), so `GET /api/sensehat-available` returned `true` on the 6 HAT-less Pis in the fleet and the v3 SettingsPanel rendered the whole Sense HAT block (display-mode selector + brightness sliders) where it does nothing. Replaced the module-import check with `detectSenseHatHardware()`, a sysfs scan for the LED-matrix framebuffer (a `/sys/class/graphics/fb*` whose `name` or backing `device/driver` basename contains "sense", e.g. "RPi-Sense FB" / "rpisense-fb"). It mirrors the daemon's `_find_sensehat_fb` detection but **deliberately drops that function's `/dev/fb0` fallback** — fb0 is the HDMI/DSI framebuffer present on every Pi, so the fallback would report "present" everywhere. Side-effect-free (no subprocess, no device open → no contention with a running `pi-sensehat.service`); returns `false` on hosts with no sysfs (the macOS dev box). 6 regression tests in `test/sensehatDetect.test.js`, including the exact "only the HDMI framebuffer → false" case the old probe got wrong. Server-only change — no client rebuild; HAT-less Pis drop the block after `git pull` + `systemctl --user restart pi-weather-server`.

### Security
- **Install-time hardening: API keys + Homebridge credentials no longer pass through `ps`-visible argv, and the Homebridge TLS probe verifies when it can.** Two Tier-2 items from the 2026-06-09 audit (#16, #17). (1) `deploy/install.sh` passed secrets as **`python3` command-line arguments** in three places — the `settings.json` generation (six API keys + lat/lon), the Homebridge sensor-discovery probe, and the indoorTemperature writer (each carrying the Homebridge password) — and argv is world-readable (`ps aux`, `/proc/PID/cmdline`), so any local user could read the secrets for the lifetime of the process during the install window. All three now pass secrets via the **environment** (`/proc/PID/environ` is owner-only `0600`); only the non-secret output path stays on argv. (2) The Homebridge sensor-discovery probe disabled TLS verification unconditionally (`ssl.CERT_NONE`); it now **tries a verifying connection first** and only falls back to unverified — with a printed warning — when the server presents an unverifiable certificate (commonly self-signed over HTTPS), narrowing the install-time MITM window for valid-cert setups without breaking the common `http://` / self-signed cases. The third audit item (#15, the NodeSource setup script run under `sudo bash` with no integrity check) is **left as a documented decision** in `ROADMAP.md` — the genuine fix (NodeSource's GPG-keyring + apt-source method) is a node-bootstrap rewrite better landed with an on-Pi test than shipped blind. **`install.sh` only — affects the next install/re-install, not the running fleet (which deploys via `git pull` + restart); smoke-test on one Pi before relying on it, since CI can't exercise `install.sh`.**
- **Per-peer ceilings on the two resource-amplifying remote endpoints.** Two Tier-2 follow-ups from the 2026-06-09 audit, both bounding what a single `ALLOW_REMOTE` peer can drive (no effect on a localhost-only Pi). (1) **`/api/weather-summary`** — the per-process 10/min billed-Claude-call ceiling (from the earlier campaign) now has a **per-peer sub-ceiling of 4/min** layered under it, so one remote client can no longer consume the whole global budget and starve other remote clients; the local kiosk stays exempt. (2) **`/api/nearby-alerts`** — one request fans out up to ~5 outbound NWS `/points` calls, so a new **per-peer concurrency cap (3 in-flight, remote clients only)** bounds the instantaneous outbound amplification (and the risk of getting the Pi's IP throttled by NWS); a peer over the cap gets HTTP 429. Both are reusable: the per-peer Claude window keys on the socket peer in a `BoundedMap`; the new `createPerPeerConcurrencyGuard` lives in `server/singleFlight.js` alongside the single-flight guard. New per-peer cases in `test/aiSummary.cache.test.js` + `test/singleFlight.test.js` (447 server tests green). **Server-only — no client rebuild, no new dependencies.**
- **Debug CSV export hardened against spreadsheet formula injection.** The shared `q()` cell-quoting helper (`client/src/ui/exportDebugCsv.js`, used by both the v2 Debug page and the v3 panel) now prefixes formula-trigger cells with `'` — `=`, `@`, tab and CR always; `+`/`-` only when the cell isn't a plain number, so negative coordinates (`-73.076935`) keep importing as numbers in Excel. Defence in depth (the one attacker-influenced column already shows the non-spoofable socket peer since the 2026-06 audit); closes the deferred "CSV `'`-prefix" item from the audit roadmap. Regression tests in `test/exportCsvQuote.test.js`.
- **Closed the four resource-bound stragglers the 2026-06-09 campaign missed, and coalesced concurrent billed Claude calls.** Follow-through from the 2026-06-10 quality audit (lot A): the AirNow and OpenAQ coordinate-keyed caches are now `BoundedMap`s with periodic expiry sweeps (same pattern as `nws.js`); the response-time KPI middleware skips 404s so a remote URL scanner can't mint unbounded stats entries; `compressionStats`' per-frame median array is capped (FIFO window). On the paid path, `/api/weather-summary` gains **in-flight coalescing** — concurrent requests that miss the summary cache for the same key now share ONE build (one radar pipeline, one Claude call) instead of each paying for their own — and the Tomorrow.io backfill inside that path is finally counted in the quota counter + service status (it was invisible except on failure, understating real API usage on the debug panel).
- **Bounded the resources a remote read-client can consume on an `ALLOW_REMOTE=true` Pi (denial-of-wallet / quota-burn / OOM hardening).** From the 2026-06-09 audit: with remote access enabled, a hostile LAN/VPN peer could (a) bypass the rate limiter and (b) drive unbounded paid Anthropic calls + unbounded memory growth. No auth gate was bypassed — the fix closes a resource ceiling. Five parts:
  - **Rate limiters + client tracker now key on the connection's socket peer (`req.socket.remoteAddress`), not the X-Forwarded-For-spoofable `req.ip`.** Under `trust proxy` (set when `ALLOW_REMOTE`), `req.ip` honours the client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For`, so a remote client could rotate the header to mint unlimited rate-limit buckets (bypassing the 120 req/min limit entirely) and unlimited `clientTracker` entries. Both now derive from the kernel-level socket peer, which a header can't forge — extending the `e4a9e72` locality fix to the rate-limit/tracking layer. New `server/rateLimitKey.js` (wraps express-rate-limit's `ipKeyGenerator` for IPv6 normalisation).
  - **`/api/weather-summary` denial-of-wallet guards.** `lang`/`tempUnit`/`speedUnit` are now validated against their enums and snapped to defaults before reaching the cache key (junk values can no longer expand the key space); the cache key quantises lat/lon to ~1.1 km so sub-km jitter can't bust the cache; the Anthropic client is built with `maxRetries: 1` (was the SDK default 2) + a 30 s timeout; and a **per-process sliding-window ceiling caps remote-induced billed Claude calls at 10/min** regardless of distinct cache keys (remote cache-miss requests get HTTP 429 when saturated; on a 429 the client keeps showing its last cached summary). The **local kiosk is exempt** from the throttle, so a remote flood can't starve the on-device refresh. 10/min is far above the fleet's legitimate traffic (~one call per location per 15-min TTL window).
  - **Bounded every coordinate-keyed in-memory cache.** A new `server/boundedCache.js` provides `BoundedMap` (hard entry cap + FIFO/LRU eviction) and `pruneObjectCache`/`sweepExpired` sweepers. Applied to `summaryCache` (aiSummaryCtrl), `tileCache`/`analysisCache`/`riskCache` (radarAnalyzerCtrl — tileCache capped tightest at 48 since it holds ~1 MB decoded PNG buffers), the in-memory `weatherCache` (proxyCtrl — was pruned on disk but never in memory), and the point/state/area caches (nws.js). Each cap is far above the legitimate working set; periodic sweeps reclaim expired-but-under-cap entries.
  - **`clientTracker` Map capped at 1000 most-recent clients** (LRU), so it can't grow without bound on a long-running process.
  - Tests: new `test/boundedCache.test.js`, `test/rateLimitKey.test.js`, `test/clientTracker.test.js`, plus new cases in `test/aiSummary.cache.test.js` (unit validation, throttle, cache cap). 257 server tests green. **Server-only change — no client rebuild; deploy with `git pull` + `systemctl --user restart pi-weather-server`.** No new dependencies. No effect on a localhost-only (default) Pi; the guards matter only when `ALLOW_REMOTE=true`.
- **`settings.json` is now created and kept owner-only (`0600`).** The file holds the six API keys plus the `indoorTemperature` Homebridge credentials, but the default umask left it `0644` (world-readable), so any other local account on the Pi could read the secrets (a local-precondition exposure, also from the 2026-06-09 audit). Fixed on both write paths: `deploy/install.sh` `chmod 600`s the file on creation (every path — fresh config, `settings.example.json` copy, and the indoor-temperature merge), and the server now re-tightens it on every startup via `settingsCtrl.ensureSecurePermissions()` (called from `index.js`) — so an **existing fleet install created `0644` self-tightens on the next `systemctl --user restart`**, no manual step needed. Every `settingsCtrl` write also passes `mode: 0o600` so a freshly created file is locked down from the first byte. Mirrors the existing `0600` chmod of the TLS key files. 3 regression tests in `test/settingsCtrl.test.js`. **Server + install.sh — no client rebuild.**
- **Remote `GET /settings` masking is now default-deny, and every response carries baseline security headers.** Two more hardening items from the 2026-06-09 audit. (1) `maskForRemote` (the remote-client settings view) projected through the `ALLOWED_KEYS` whitelist *first*, so an **unrecognised** top-level key — one hand-added to `settings.json`, or left over from an older build — can no longer reach a remote client verbatim; the mask is allow-list driven, not deny-list. (A key deliberately added to `ALLOWED_KEYS` is whitelisted and still passes — if it carries a secret it must also be added to the booleanise/strip sets; default-deny guards the unknown-key case.) (2) A new `server/securityHeaders.js` middleware (mounted first) stamps `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, and `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'` on every response, and `X-Powered-By` is disabled. Set by hand rather than via `helmet` to keep the dependency footprint minimal; deliberately no HSTS (self-signed cert + `:8080` HTTP fallback) and no script/style CSP (the SPA relies on runtime inline styles + Leaflet). New `test/securityHeaders.test.js` + default-deny cases in `test/settingsCtrl.test.js` (265 server tests green). **Server-only — no client rebuild, no new dependencies.**
- **Disk-write hardening: debounced the API-quota counter and gave `settings.json` a single owning writer.** Two Tier-2 items from the 2026-06-09 audit. (1) `requestCounter.increment()` ran a synchronous `fs.writeFileSync` on **every** external-API call — an SD-card write that blocked the event loop and amplified any request flood. It now marks the counters dirty and a coalesced flush persists them at most once a minute (plus a flush wired into `index.js`'s existing graceful-shutdown handler, so a `systemctl restart` doesn't drop the last window). (2) `sensehatModeCtrl.persistSensehat` wrote `settings.json` directly with raw `fs` — a second, unsynchronised writer that bypassed the key whitelist and the `0600` file mode. It now routes through a new `settingsCtrl.patchAdvancedSubKey()`, so the file has exactly one owning module: the write respects the whitelist + file mode and is **serialised** against other internal writes (rapid Sense HAT slider POSTs can no longer interleave their read-modify-write). New `test/requestCounter.test.js` + merge/serialisation cases in `test/settingsCtrl.test.js` (276 server tests green). **Server-only — no client rebuild, no new dependencies.**
- **Endpoint guards: rate-limited `GET /api/brightness` and a concurrency lock on `POST /api/update`.** Two more Tier-2 items from the 2026-06-09 audit. (1) `GET /api/brightness` had no rate limiter, but on the ed-ddc backend each read forks `ed-ddc-server` (~150 ms `execSync`) — an unrated, spammable read that could block the event loop. It now carries `apiLimiter` (120 req/min) like the other public reads. (2) `POST /api/update` had no concurrency guard: two overlapping invocations would fire two `git pull` + `npm ci` runs against the same checkout and could corrupt the working tree / `node_modules`. A new single-flight guard (`server/singleFlight.js`) rejects an overlapping update with HTTP 409 (`reason: "update-in-progress"`) and auto-releases when the in-flight response settles, so a failed update can be retried. While here, the updater's two direct-exit fallbacks (the rare `systemctl restart`-failed path and the dev/macOS path) now call the graceful `shutdown()` — flushing the request counters + weather cache — instead of a bare `process.exit(0)`, matching the SIGTERM path. New `test/singleFlight.test.js` (281 server tests green). **Server-only — no client rebuild, no new dependencies.**
- **Build/CI supply-chain hardening: dist-file-set check, pinned Actions, and `output.clean`.** Three Tier-2 items from the 2026-06-09 audit. (1) **webpack `output.clean: true`** wipes `dist/` before each build so renamed/removed chunks don't linger as committed orphans (a stale `1.bundle.min.js` survived rebuilds until it was hand-deleted in the Geist PR); verified every committed dist file is re-emitted, so cleaning removes nothing that isn't regenerated. (2) **CI now verifies the committed `client/dist` file set is reproducible** — after `npm run prod` it fails if a fresh build adds or removes any file under `dist/` (an orphan chunk, or a chunk-layout change that wasn't committed). It intentionally does *not* byte-compare minified JS: the committed bundle is built on macOS and webpack/terser minification isn't byte-identical to a Linux CI build (terser mangles a few identifiers differently), so content drift across the macOS-build / ubuntu-CI boundary is expected and ignored — only the environment-independent file set is gated. (3) **GitHub Actions are pinned to full commit SHAs** (with version comments) instead of mutable tags — a moved `@v6`/`@v3` tag can't swap in malicious code; prioritised `dependabot/fetch-metadata` since it runs in the `contents: write` auto-merge job. Dependabot's `github-actions` updater keeps the pins current. CI / build config only — no app code change.
- **Deploy hardening: the cleartext fallback never binds the LAN, and the kiosk debug port is off by default.** Two Tier-2 items from the 2026-06-09 audit. (1) When the TLS cert is unavailable the server falls back to cleartext HTTP on `:8080`, but it now **binds `127.0.0.1` only — never `0.0.0.0`, even under `ALLOW_REMOTE`** — so masked settings + traffic can't be sniffed unencrypted over the LAN; remote access stays down (with a loud startup error) until a cert exists. `deploy/harden-kiosk.sh` no longer opens `:8080` in the firewall to match (only `:8443`). (2) The kiosk browser's Chrome DevTools **remote-debugging port (`:9222`) is now off by default** — while open, any local process could attach and drive the kiosk session to reach the localhost-gated endpoints. It's gated behind `KIOSK_REMOTE_DEBUG=true` in `deploy/start-server` (set the env var or add it to `browser.conf`) for the rare diagnostic session. Server + deploy scripts — no client rebuild. **Existing installs:** the `:8080` bind tightens on the next service restart; the debug-port gate and firewall change apply after `git pull` (the firewall rule only changes if you re-run `harden-kiosk.sh`).

## [2.19.0] - 2026-06-02

### Security
- **`GET /api/health` no longer leaks the internal Homebridge host:port to remote clients.** Found during a 2026-05-30 targeted security re-test of the server delta since the April PEN test. The endpoint is rate-limited but not localhost-gated, and returned each service's error comment verbatim. The indoor-temperature poll recorded that comment from the raw axios error, so a code-less Homebridge failure surfaced `connect ECONNREFUSED <host>:<port>` / `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND <host>` — disclosing the internal host:port to any remote caller (when `ALLOW_REMOTE=true`), violating the host-stripping invariant `GET /settings` already enforces for the `indoorTemperature` block. No credentials were ever exposed (auth failures surface as a 401 status). Two-layer fix: `indoorTempCtrl` now records the error **code only** (never the host-bearing message), and `getHealth` **redacts** any IP / host:port from `issues[].comment` for non-local callers (defense in depth for any present or future comment source). Covered by new `redactHostInfo` unit tests.
- **NWS zone geometry fetch refuses HTTP redirects (`maxRedirects: 0`).** The `api.weather.gov` allowlist validates the initial URL but axios does not re-check a 3xx `Location`, which could steer the fetch to an arbitrary host. Legitimate zone endpoints answer 200 directly, so this rejects nothing real while closing the SSRF redirect gap (defense in depth).
- **Localhost access gates no longer trust `X-Forwarded-For` — fixes a `localhostOnly` bypass + `GET /settings` secret-disclosure when `ALLOW_REMOTE=true`.** Found and confirmed during a 2026-05-29 security audit. When `ALLOW_REMOTE=true`, the server runs `app.set("trust proxy", 1)` and every locality check resolved `req.ip` — which, with trust proxy on, is derived from the client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For` header. A direct remote/LAN client sending `X-Forwarded-For: 127.0.0.1` had `req.ip` resolve to `127.0.0.1`, impersonating localhost. Empirically reproduced: a connection from `192.168.5.250` with that header passed `isLocalhostIp(req.ip)`, which would have **(a)** returned `GET /settings` **unmasked** — all six API keys (Tomorrow.io, Mapbox, LocationIQ, Anthropic, AirNow, OpenAQ) plus the Homebridge URL/username/password in cleartext — and **(b)** passed every `localhostOnly` write gate (`POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE /settings`, `POST /api/update`, `/api/sensehat-mode`, `/api/kiosk-location`, `/api/brightness`, `/api/debug/*`). Practically exploitable by any device on the same LAN/VPN as a Pi running `ALLOW_REMOTE=true`. Fix: the security gates (`localhostOnly`, `debugLocalhostOnly`, and the `req.isLocal` masking decision) now key off the raw TCP socket peer (`req.socket.remoteAddress`), which is the kernel-level connection origin and cannot be forged by an HTTP header. Both documented remote paths terminate at loopback so they're unaffected — SSH tunnel and RPi Connect both connect from `127.0.0.1`. A direct VPN/LAN client connects from its real IP and is now correctly treated as remote (read-masked settings, writes rejected), matching the documented "use an SSH tunnel for remote settings" workflow. The security log now records the real socket peer rather than the (spoofable) `req.ip`. `req.ip` remains in use for rate-limit keying + client tracking only. Verified with a before/after repro of the exact `index.js` middleware: pre-fix the spoofed header unmasked settings; post-fix it's blocked while genuine loopback access still passes. Server-only change, no client rebuild.
- **NWS zone-geometry fetcher now allowlists `api.weather.gov` before fetching (`nwsZones.getZoneGeometry`).** Defense-in-depth from the same audit. The zone URLs resolved for the Phase 4d polygon overlay come from the NWS alerts response (`properties.affectedZones`) and were fetched with no host validation. Blindly fetching an upstream-supplied URL is a textbook SSRF foothold: a poisoned response (or any future code path feeding user-influenced data into the function) could have reached internal targets the Pi can see — cloud metadata at `169.254.169.254`, the server's own `localhost:8443` control endpoints, or LAN hosts. New `isAllowedZoneUrl` guard rejects anything that isn't `https://api.weather.gov/...` before any network call, using exact-hostname matching (so `api.weather.gov.evil.com` and a `https://api.weather.gov@evil.com` userinfo trick are both rejected). 6 regression tests added in `test/nwsZones.test.js`. No behaviour change on the legitimate path — real NWS zone URLs all pass. Server-only change.

### Changed
- **v3.1 Phase 6 — the ambient SettingsPanel moves from a stacked + collapsible layout to a single-selection vertical tab rail, reusing the DebugPanel rail grammar.** The four sections (1 · Local preferences, 2 · Configuration & API keys, 3 · Advanced, 4 · Preview) are now rail entries (icon-above-short-label chips: `settings-adjust` / `password` / `construct` / `eye`) with exactly one section visible at a time — a radio model, unlike DebugPanel's multi-select bucket pinning. The panel always opens on section 1 and deliberately does **not** persist the last tab (a settings surface reads better when predictable). The two collapsible disclosures (Advanced + Preview) lose their `DisclosureHeader` chevrons and become plain rail sections; `DisclosureHeader` is removed. The rail copies DebugPanel's tactile hardening verbatim (no hover/active/plain-focus fill, dashed keyboard-focus only, active left-edge accent bar, `blur()` on tap) so the documented Pi-Chromium kiosk lingering-fill bug can't recur, plus the same `≤520px` compact mode (76 px rail, tighter chips) for the 7" screen. The overlay container becomes a flex column (header + flex body splitting into rail + scrollable pane); only the pane scrolls now. A per-section informational footer states where each section's changes land and how they commit (local = live; API = batched via Save; Advanced = each flag POSTs on change; Preview = on reload) — text only, no save button, since the only batched Save remains the API section's keys+coords block. Net-new UX from the approved Phase 6 design: (a) a schematic 3-segment **sleep timeline** (On → Soft sleep → Deep sleep) bound to the live `stage1Delay` / `stage2Delay` (+brightness) values, with semantics corrected from the mockup — the screen never powers off (deep sleep is a black screen + anti-burn-in dot at the brightness floor), and the deep threshold is shown as the absolute sum since `stage2Delay` is incremental; (b) **Override** pills + "Vide = géolocalisation automatique" helper + an **Auto** button (clears the field → falls back to detection) on the Latitude/Longitude overrides, with "never sent to an external service" stated inline; (c) **detechnified labels** — "Saute Claude quand le temps est stable" → "Économie d'appels IA quand le ciel est calme" (the mechanism stays honestly "AI" in the sub-text, the vendor name leaves the UI), and the sleep "Stage 1 / Stage 2" labels become "Veille douce / Veille profonde" to match the timeline. The real provider list, units (Metric/Imperial preset + four fine selectors incl. K and m/s), Sense HAT mode selector and map-style options are unchanged — those were already correct in the panel (the mockup's invented Met.no/OpenWeather/satellite values never entered the codebase). Narrow-width handling: the content's `.paneInner` was `box-sizing: content-box` (this stylesheet has no global box-sizing reset — each control sets its own), so its `width: 100%` + 36 px horizontal padding rendered a border-box 36 px WIDER than the pane and clipped off the right edge at every width — invisible at desktop but exposed once the rail narrowed the pane (reported at 1127/948/620/500 px). Fixed with `box-sizing: border-box` on `.paneInner` (+ `.paneFooter`) and `min-width: 0` so it shrinks to the pane and the grids reflow inside it. The units grid (grid8) further switched from viewport-breakpoint column counts to `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 200px), 1fr))` — because paneInner caps at 880 px, a viewport-based 8-col was always 880/8 = 110 px per cell, too narrow for the "Métrique / Impérial" selector (~145 px min) which overflowed into the next cell at every width; auto-fit reflows from the grid's own width (4 cols at the 880 px cap, 3 on the 7", 2 on a phone, 1 when extremely narrow), dropping a column the instant a cell would get too tight rather than letting text spill. The config grids' breakpoints were bumped to account for the rail (grid2@760, grid4@840). Rail layout is now responsive on two axes: the short-height 7" kiosk (`max-height: 520px`) keeps a vertical 76 px compact rail, while a narrow phone portrait (`max-width: 600px`) **flips the rail to a horizontal scrollable tab bar at the top** so the controls get the full width below it (the active-tab cue moves from a left-edge bar to a bottom underline) — the mobile layout from the approved Phase 6 design. Verified reflow + clipping-free at 500/950/1300 px. Two further refinements aligned with the Phase 6 design's mobile spec: (1) the "Paramètres" title header is removed at all sizes (the per-section header already names the location, and it reclaimed ~60 px of height) and the exit becomes a terminal **"Fermer" action on the rail** — pinned to the bottom of the vertical rail (top divider) and the right end of the horizontal mobile rail (left divider), kept dim so it never reads as a 5th section; a floating top-right × was rejected because it would collide with the section headers' right-aligned pills (MODIFIABLE / active-count). The safe-area-inset the header used to absorb moved to `.body` so the rail still clears the iOS notch on a PWA-installed iPhone. (2) The API-key rows changed from a 4-column grid (whose trailing purpose column truncated — "Tuiles de carte + …" — as the panel narrowed) to a **stacked block**: name + tier, the full purpose line, then a full-width input — the purpose always reads in full and a real key is legible at any width. Verified close-action + stacking at 500/1200 px. All new strings are inline trilingual (EN/FR/ES) per this file's `lbl()` convention; client-only, no server or API changes. Built clean (`npm run prod`, 0 errors).
- **Sense HAT `overcast` animation at night now lifts the brightness multiplier from `BRIGHTNESS_NIGHT 0.35` to `BRIGHTNESS_NIGHT_OVERCAST 0.7` so both wave phases stay above the matrix's visibility threshold — whole matrix uniformly lit instead of the previous "bright band on black" read.** The redesign went through eight iterations on `feat/overcast-night-variant` exploring palette-side fixes (warm tints, narrower contrast, per-pixel dither against RGB565 quantization strobe), each of which traded one artifact for another (sub-threshold trough → missing wave → pastel speckle). The cleanest fix turned out to be at the brightness layer, not the palette: a new per-state override in `_render` doubles the night brightness multiplier specifically for state=="overcast" while is_day=False, leaving every other night state at the default 0.35. With the day palette unchanged (GREY_DARK 75 → GREY_LIGHT 145) at × 0.7 the effective range becomes 53 → 102 (vs. previously 26 → 51) — trough crosses above the visibility floor of ~45 effective, peak doubles, contrast stays at the day variant's natural 1.92×. The night-specific palette / period / dither constants (`_OVERCAST_NIGHT_DARK/LIGHT/PERIOD_TICKS/DITHER_AMPLITUDE`) are kept as named references to the day values plus dither amplitude 0 — inert structure preserved so a future redesign can re-decouple night from day by editing one constant without re-introducing the `_overcast_frame` branching. The eight-iteration calibration history is captured both as a comment block above the constants and as `incident_sensehat_overcast_night_visibility_floor.md` in the agent memory. Day path unchanged (still GREY_DARK/LIGHT + 12 s period + sun-glow halo blend toward `_OVERCAST_SUN_GLOW_COLOR` at × 1.0 brightness). New CLI slug `overcast-night` exposed in `_TEST_STATE_SLUGS` so the variant can be validated in isolation via `python3 tools/sensehat_weather.py --state overcast-night` (the existing `overcast` slug still maps to the day variant). No protocol or API changes — purely a `tools/sensehat_weather.py` brightness override; restart `pi-sensehat.service` to pick up. Pre-change, the night variant reused the day palette (`GREY_DARK 75` → `GREY_LIGHT 145`, period 12 s) with just the sun-glow disabled — at `BRIGHTNESS_NIGHT × 0.35`, the trough landed at effective 26 (below the matrix's practical visibility threshold) so the dim phase pixels read as off, and the visible bright band crossed the matrix every ~6 s reading as "something moving in grey" rather than overnight cloud cover. Iterations 1-3 (commits `5daf507` → `90d1b01` → `2318313`) tried progressively brighter palettes bounded by `GREY_LIGHT`, all still too dim or only partially visible; iteration 4 (commit `bfafb6e`) lifted that ceiling but field-tested with two side effects (yellow cast too intense + light stroboscopic flicker). Iteration 5 (`bca3b2e`) addressed both but narrowed contrast to 1.10× to give the dither more headroom — wave was no longer perceptible. Iteration 6 (`1a0db97`) widened contrast back to 1.26× to restore the wave but surfaced a "pastel speckle" — individual pixels took green/cyan/magenta casts instead of uniform warm-grey, traced to the dither × asymmetric RGB565 quantization interaction (G's 4-step boundary can be crossed by a ±4 dither while R/B's 8-step boundary stays untouched, breaking the intended R > G > B channel ordering). Iteration 7 (this commit) halves the dither amplitude. Final constants: `_OVERCAST_NIGHT_DARK = (155, 150, 147)` and `_OVERCAST_NIGHT_LIGHT = (195, 190, 187)` — raw values exceed `GREY_LIGHT 145` so that after the × 0.35 multiplier the matrix lands at effective 51-68, comfortably above the visibility floor at both wave phases. The unequal R > G > B channels (~8 raw units of R over B, half iteration 4's offset) give a subtle warm cast (~3 effective units of R-B differential) without the previous sodium-yellow read. Period stays at `_OVERCAST_NIGHT_PERIOD_TICKS = 250` (~30 s, 2.5× slower than the day variant's 12 s). A new per-pixel static dither (`_OVERCAST_NIGHT_PIXEL_DITHER`, ±2 RGB units after the iter-7 reduction from ±4, identical pattern to fog's `_FOG_PIXEL_DITHER` with seed 43 to keep speckle distinct) is applied to the night-only output — at the moderate 1.26× contrast iteration 6 lands at, wave-induced shifts of ~1 RGB unit per tick can move many pixels across RGB565 quantization boundaries (R/B in 8-unit steps, G in 4-unit steps) simultaneously, reading as synchronous LED clicks rather than smooth drift; the per-pixel dither breaks that synchrony so transitions appear continuous. Iterations 1-3 didn't surface this strobe because pixels under the visibility floor never lit, masking the artifact. Day path unchanged (still GREY_DARK/LIGHT + 12 s period + sun-glow halo blend toward `_OVERCAST_SUN_GLOW_COLOR`, no dither). New CLI slug `overcast-night` exposed in `_TEST_STATE_SLUGS` so the variant can be validated in isolation via `python3 tools/sensehat_weather.py --state overcast-night` (the existing `overcast` slug still maps to the day variant). The seven-iteration calibration history is captured both as a comment block above the constants and as `incident_sensehat_overcast_night_visibility_floor.md` in the agent memory, with the lesson that the SenseHat matrix has two distinct thresholds (per-channel RGB565 activation ~25 raw effective, and perceptual visibility ~45 effective) plus a synchronous-quantization artifact at narrow contrast that must each be respected — a palette can light up channels without being perceived, and can be perceived without appearing smooth. No protocol or API changes — purely a `tools/sensehat_weather.py` palette/timing/dither tweak; restart `pi-sensehat.service` to pick up.
- **Alert overlay toggle labels shortened to "Afficher la zone" / "Masquer la zone" (FR), "Show zone" / "Hide zone" (EN), "Mostrar zona" / "Ocultar zona" (ES).** Field follow-up to the action-footer alignment polish below (commit `9d69925`). Even with the buttons growing to claim the full row width on a narrow rail, the FR label "AFFICHER SUR LA CARTE" (21 chars) still wrapped mid-text after the icon ate ~42 px of the 237 px-wide button — leaving "AFFICHER SUR LA" on one line and "CARTE" detached on the next. Two paths considered: (a) reduce icon size / padding to claw back ~10 px, (b) shorten the label. Picked (b) — semantically "la zone" is more precise than "sur la carte" because the polygon paints the **alert zone** (the geographic region the warning covers), not "something on the map" generically. The aria-labels keep their explicit phrasing ("Afficher la zone d'alerte sur la carte radar" etc.) since screen readers benefit from full context. The map-icon glyph stays as the visual signal for "this acts on the map" — the verb pair just no longer competes with that context. All three locales aligned: pair length now 14-16 chars across the board, fits comfortably on the 7" Pi rail and looks more compact on Desktop too.
- **Alert detail action footer ("Réduire" / "Afficher sur la carte") now fills the row width and stacks cleanly to full-width buttons on narrow rails.** Field observation on a 7" Pi night-mode screenshot 2026-05-29: the "AFFICHER SUR LA CARTE" label was wrapping mid-text inside its button ("AFFICHER SUR LA" + "CARTE" on a second line, with "CARTE" visually detached from the map icon because it aligned with the span's left edge — the right side of the icon's gap). Root cause: the `.actionButton` rule was `display: inline-flex` (buttons stay at their natural content width) without a `flex` grow setting, so a button whose content can't fit a single row neither widened nor cleanly took the whole row — it just wrapped its text mid-flight. Two CSS changes: `align-items: center` → `align-items: stretch` on `.actionFooter` (so when buttons wrap to separate rows each row sizes uniformly), and `flex: 1 1 auto` + explicit `justify-content: flex-start` on `.actionButton` (so buttons fill the available row width, sitting side-by-side on a wide rail and stacking to full-row width on a narrow rail). Effect: on the 7" Pi rail the two buttons stack with each label fully readable on a single line; on Desktop the buttons remain side-by-side but now each claims half the available width (slightly more "filled" visual rhythm vs. the prior natural-width pair). Pure CSS change; no JSX.
- **"Voir sur la carte" → "Afficher sur la carte" on the alert overlay toggle (FR locale only).** Immediate follow-up to the "Cacher → Masquer" polish below. After the verb on the active side switched to "Masquer", the inactive side still said "Voir", which left a mixed register (CTA-style "Voir" against state-toggle "Masquer"). The FR convention across every major OS UI (Apple, Microsoft, Google, GNOME) for visibility toggles is the symmetric pair "Afficher / Masquer" — "Voir" is reserved for CTAs and links ("Voir tout", "Voir les détails"), not for state toggles. Aligning the visible button with the aria-label which already said "Afficher" closes a pre-existing asymmetry noted in the previous polish entry. `alert.showOnMap` updated; aria-label was already correct. EN ("Show on map") and ES ("Ver en el mapa") unchanged — same register-decision is already right in those locales.
- **"Cacher de la carte" → "Masquer de la carte" on the alert overlay toggle (FR locale only).** Polish pass after field-testing the Phase 4d / PR #2 stack on three Pis. Maintainer's read: the rest of the v3 UI carries a polished register (palette `ambient`, Geist typography, severity chip vocabulary), and "Cacher" came off as the most colloquial verb in the otherwise composed wording set — closer to a casual UI than the rest of the surface. "Masquer" is the standard FR UI verb across professional software (macOS / iOS / Apple FR, Google Maps FR, Microsoft 365 FR) and matches the tone better. `alert.hideOnMap` and `alert.hideOnMapAria` both updated. Aria pair retains its "Masquer" verb consistency. The active-state label flip ("Voir sur la carte" → "Masquer de la carte") still works the same way — only the verb register shifts. EN ("Hide from map") and ES ("Ocultar del mapa") are unchanged — the equivalent register decision is already correct in those locales. Side observation: the FR `showOnMap` text still says "Voir" while `showOnMapAria` already says "Afficher" — a pre-existing asymmetry left in place for now (a separate decision whether to unify to "Afficher / Masquer" for pure UI-pair symmetry, or keep the slightly warmer "Voir / Masquer" mix).

- **Right-rail chevron removed on LayoutPi and LayoutDesktop; replaced by the Leaflet RadarFocusControl already used as the topleft "focus radar" toggle.** v3.1 consolidation following the Claude Design Phase 4 audit (2026-05-28). Pre-consolidation, three layouts shipped three different collapse mental models: LayoutMobile used a custom 44 px `mapMaximizeButton` floated on the map card, LayoutPi + LayoutDesktop used a vertical-bar chevron pinned to the map's right edge driving `infoPanelCollapsed`, and LayoutDesktop additionally exposed a Leaflet RadarFocusControl in the topleft for full-viewport "focus radar" mode — two intersecting toggles on the same layout, different positions, different glyphs, two different bits of context state. Reconciled to one mental model: tap the Leaflet ⛶ control in the topleft (sitting under zoom +/−) to hide the panels and let the radar fill the column; tap again to restore. Same control now renders on Pi + Desktop, conditioned on whichever of `piRadarMaximized` or `desktopRadarMaximized` is the active layout's sentinel. New `piRadarMaximized` state in `AppContext` mirrors the existing `desktopRadarMaximized` / `mobileRadarMaximized` sentinel pattern (null when layout is inactive, false on mount, true on user tap, null on unmount). LayoutPi's `infoPanelCollapsed`-driven `.layout.collapsed` rules renamed to `.layout.focused` and now react to `piRadarMaximized === true`; LayoutDesktop's `.layout.collapsed` rules deleted entirely (`.layout.focused` already covered the focus-mode case). `infoPanelCollapsed` itself remains in AppContext for v2 InfoPanel back-compat (the legacy `experimentalUiC=false` path still reads it via the v2 component tree) but carries no v3 layout role anymore. `FloatingMiniBanner` keeps its severe-alert safety-net role: still rendered when `focused === true` AND an eligible red/orange alert is active, so a Tornado Warning is never invisible behind a "give me more map" tap. Side effect: audit finding B2 (chevron `:hover` sticky on tactile Chromium, "drop don't gate" lesson from `incident_hover_gate_insufficient_chromium_touch.md`) is resolved by deletion — the component carrying the problematic `:hover` rule no longer exists. Two i18n keys retired (`controls.expandPanel`, `controls.collapsePanel`); the existing `controls.focusRadar` / `controls.restorePanels` cover the new control on Pi as well.

### Fixed
- **Settings (and other essential) dock buttons no longer fall off the right edge of the BottomDock on a narrowed desktop window.** Found while field-testing the Phase 6 settings panel at mobile width: the dock's "hide secondary buttons" collapse was gated at `max-width: 479px`, but a desktop browser can't be resized below its ~500 px minimum window width — so the gate never fired on a narrowed desktop window, the full 13-icon dock stayed overcrowded, and the rightmost essentials (the ⚙ settings gear, the health dot) overlapped or pushed past the dock edge and became un-tappable. The user couldn't even open Settings to reach the panel. Bumped the collapse breakpoint to `max-width: 600px` (still `and (orientation: portrait)`), which covers both the desktop-minimum case and every phone in portrait while leaving the 800 px-wide 7" kiosk (landscape) untouched. No JS or markup change — one breakpoint in `BottomDock/styles.css`; the essentials were already validated to fit at ≤479 px (iPhone SE 375 px), so they fit comfortably at 500-600 px. Pre-existing issue, unrelated to the Phase 6 panel changes.
- **Active-alert counter no longer counts sub-threshold (yellow-tier) gov alerts — fixes "1 / 2 but no second card".** Follow-up to the counter-overflow fix below, from the same Nicolet field report (2026-05-29/30): the banner showed "1 / 2" while only one alert was ever displayable and no second mini-card existed. Root cause: the four ambient gov-alert surfaces each derived "the displayed set + the current alert" independently and disagreed. `AlertBanner` (counter + primary index) and `AlertDetailInline` indexed into the dismissal-filtered list of **all tiers**, so a yellow-tier ECCC alert (`severityToTier` → `"yellow"` for minor/low severity) inflated the denominator without ever clearing the red/orange SHOW gate that `AlertMiniCards` uses — hence a count of 2 with one card. `FloatingMiniBanner` was worse: it indexed the **raw** `govAlerts` array, not even applying the dismissal filter. Consolidated all four onto one shared derivation: new `selectEligibleGovAlerts()` helper in `ui/alertLogic.js` (keeps only red/orange tiers) + new `useEligibleGovAlerts()` hook that layers the dismissal filter, the tier gate, and the `govAlertIdx % length` resolution in one place. `AlertBanner`, `AlertDetailInline`, `FloatingMiniBanner` and `AlertMiniCards` now all read `{ eligibleGovAlerts, safeIdx, currentAlert }` from the hook, so the counter, the detail body, the collapsed-rail overlay and the mini-cards list can never disagree on the set again. Mini-card taps now pass an index into the eligible list (was the visible-list index), kept consistent with the banner's re-mod; the AppContext bound `idx < govAlerts.length` still holds since the eligible list is a subset. Nicolet now reads "1 / 1" with no orphan card. Side benefit: `FloatingMiniBanner` now respects dismissals. 4 regression tests added in `test/alertLogic.test.js` (incl. the Nicolet yellow-inflation case). Legacy v2 banner (`components/AlertBanner`, `GovAlertDetail`) intentionally untouched — it's a separate, removal-queued render path. Client-only change.
- **Active-alert counter no longer overflows the card's right edge on the 7" Pi rail.** Field observation on a SenseHat Pi screenshot 2026-05-29: with two ECCC alerts active, the meta-row counter rendered the full "1 / 2 alertes actives" string, which bled past the right edge of the alert card. Root cause: `.metaRow` (`ambient/AlertBanner`) is a non-wrapping flex holding the (internally-wrapping) `AlertMetaChips` row plus the counter, and the counter carries `flex-shrink: 0` — so once the widest chip plus the verbose counter text exceeded the narrow rail width, the counter refused to shrink or wrap and overflowed instead. Fix: the visible counter now uses a compact `"{{current}} / {{count}}"` form (new `alert.activeAlertsCountShort` key in all three locales) while the full "1 / 2 active alerts" phrasing is retained on the `aria-label` for screen readers. Matches the design's footer-counter intent (the JSDoc already described it as "mirrors the design's footer counter"). Client-only change; i18n keys added, no server work.
- **Synchronized Tomorrow.io request burst is now spaced out, eliminating the self-inflicted 429s at the source.** Companion to the health-chip fix below. The client fired `current` + `hourly` + `daily` in the same instant on mount (and again whenever the hourly/daily interval realigned with a current tick — every 60 min / 24 h), and the AI-summary path re-fetched `current` on a cache miss — up to four calls hitting Tomorrow.io's free tier (~3 req/s cap) in the same second, which returned 429s the health chip then surfaced. Two layers, defending the constraint where it lives. **Server (hard guarantee):** a lightweight dispatch spacer in `proxyCtrl` (`createSpacer` / `spaceTomorrowCall`, 350 ms minimum gap) serializes the *kick-off* of every Tomorrow.io call — not their completion, so calls still run concurrently on the wire, just started far enough apart to stay under the per-second cap. It sits at the API-key boundary and is shared by all four call sites (the three proxy endpoints + the AI-summary re-fetch in `aiSummaryCtrl`), so it holds no matter how many clients (kiosk + remote viewers) drive one server. **Client (defence-in-depth):** the three weather pollers in `AppContext` now start staggered (0 / 2 s / 4 s offsets), which both avoids the synchronized burst and permanently phase-shifts the intervals so they don't realign later. New tests in `test/proxyRetry.test.js` cover the spacer (first-call immediacy, concurrent drain spacing, no queue-wedge on a slow caller).
- **Health chip no longer flips red on a transient Tomorrow.io blip.** A single upstream HTTP 500 ("Temporary issue due to an unknown internal error" — recovers in <21 s) or a burst-induced 429 used to paint the health dot critical-red and keep it red until the next successful poll: up to 1 h for the hourly poller and 24 h for the daily one, because their last success always predated the 35 min `RECENT_SUCCESS_WINDOW_MS`, so the recent-success suppression never applied to them. Two layered fixes. **(A) Resilience** — the three Tomorrow.io proxy calls (`weatherCurrent` / `weatherHourly` / `weatherDaily`) now retry once after a ~1.5 s backoff on transient errors only (HTTP 5xx + 429), via a shared `retryTransient` / `weatherGet` helper in `proxyCtrl.js`; deterministic 4xx (invalid key, bad coords) and network timeouts are not retried (a timeout retry would just delay the stale-on-error fallback). This absorbs the vast majority of blips before they're ever recorded as a failure. **(B) Classifier** — a service must now fail `MIN_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES` (2) polls in a row before the health classifier flags it. A new `consecutiveFailures` counter on each `serviceStatus` entry increments on failure and resets to 0 on any success, so one blip bumps the counter to 1 and stays invisible; only a sustained failure reaches the threshold and surfaces. The recent-success window is retained as the fast-suppress for the duplicate AI-summary Tomorrow.io call path on the 10 min current poll. Trade-off: a genuine outage now needs two failed polls to surface (worst case ~48 h for the daily endpoint, but the 10 min current poll still surfaces a real Tomorrow.io outage within ~20 min). New tests: `test/proxyRetry.test.js` + `test/healthClassifier.test.js`. Server-only change, no client rebuild.
- **AlertDetailInline map overlay no longer orphan-paints after the user collapses the detail panel.** Design audit B1 (2026-05-28). When the user tapped "Voir sur la carte" on an expanded gov-alert detail, the `highlightedAlertId` set into AppContext drove a tier-coloured GeoJSON polygon on the radar (Phase 4d shipped in `765da0b`). Two paths could leave that polygon painted with no visible toggle to dismiss it: (1) the user collapsing the panel via the AlertBanner head row or the "Réduire" button (`setGovAlertExpanded(false)`) — the action footer that owns the "Cacher de la carte" toggle then unmounts, leaving the polygon stranded on the map; (2) the component itself unmounting on a layout switch (Pi → Mobile via window resize). AppContext already cleared `highlightedAlertId` on `govAlertIdx` change (cycling alerts), but neither the collapse nor the unmount paths cleared it — until the user re-expanded the detail AND re-tapped the same button, the overlay was effectively orphaned. New cleanup in `AlertDetailInline/index.js`: two `useEffect`s placed **before** the early `return null` guards (so they respect Rules of Hooks despite the component's conditional rendering paths) — one tracking `govAlertExpanded` and clearing the overlay when it flips to false, one bare-cleanup on unmount. Pure client change; no server or i18n work needed. Aligns the overlay's lifecycle with the panel's expanded-state lifecycle.
- **Pull-to-refresh listeners no longer tear down and re-install at ~60 FPS during a pull (LayoutMobile).** Design audit B3 (2026-05-28). The PTR effect in `LayoutMobile/index.js` had `[pullArmed, pullDistance, refreshing]` in its dependency array. Because `setPullDistance` fires on every `touchmove` (the indicator follows the finger as the user pulls), `pullDistance` changed ~60 ×/s during a pull, which tore down the four touch listeners (`touchstart`, `touchmove`, `touchend`, `touchcancel`) and re-installed them at the same rate — ~240 add/remove ops per second of pull. No events were dropped (cleanup + setup are synchronous in the same React commit on a single-threaded JS runtime) but the work was pure waste, and a code-smell red flag for anyone auditing the effect later. Migrated the three state values read inside the handlers into mirror refs (`pullArmedRef`, `pullDistanceRef`, `refreshingRef`) kept in sync via three single-state `useEffect`s. The PTR effect itself now subscribes once on mount (deps `[]`, with an inline `eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` comment explaining the intent) and the touch handlers read the latest values from refs. A pull now only triggers the React renders that update the indicator's visual position — not the listener-rebuild churn. Pure perf change; behaviour identical (gesture still arms at threshold, reloads on release-above-threshold, springs back below). Existing `ptrStateRef` pattern unchanged. Out-of-scope follow-up noted by the same audit: `touchmove` is still `{ passive: true }`, so `preventDefault()` is impossible — iOS PWA rubber-band may double-paint with the indicator. Separate ticket if observed on device.
- **Debug panel rail tabs — third pass: dropped `:hover` background entirely + reset `:active`.** The two previous attempts (commit `cecb320` adding `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` gate, commit `654f027` neutralising the user-agent `:focus` default) **didn't fully resolve** the residual-fill bug on the Pi 7" kiosk; maintainer confirmed 2026-05-28 that previously-tapped (but now inactive) tabs still kept a subtle tint, despite the cleaner active-state diagnosis (left-edge accent bar present = truly active vs. residual fill without bar = bug). Following the same arc the BottomDock investigation took (commit `f20b8a2` removed dock hover entirely after gating proved insufficient), this commit drops the rail-tab `:hover` background outright.
