The `.railButtonActive` selector (left-edge accent bar + accent-soft fill) remains the canonical "this tab is active" indicator — the only signal the maintainer needs since the bar is the unambiguous cue. Hover on desktop becomes a no-op, but the affordance loss is minor: the active indicator is already strong enough. Also added a defensive `.railButton:active { background-color: transparent }` to neutralise any user-agent `:active` default that might also paint a faint fill on touch. Hover on other interactive elements outside the rail (header action buttons, etc.) is unchanged.
- **Debug panel rail tabs no longer keep a residual fill on previously-tapped (but now inactive) tabs.** Follow-up to the `:hover`-gating fix in commit `cecb320`. Maintainer reported on 2026-05-28 that even after the hover fix landed, switching between rail tabs left previously-tapped tabs visibly tinted — with a fill subtler than `.railButtonActive` (no left-edge accent bar, the canonical active-state cue), yet still visibly lit against the cream day palette. Root cause: Chromium's user-agent default `:focus` style paints a faint background on a tapped `<button>` that lingers until focus moves elsewhere. The React handler does call `e.currentTarget.blur()` in onClick, but on some Pi Chromium configurations the blur fires one re-render frame after the focus is applied, which is enough for the user to perceive the residual fill. Existing CSS only handled `:focus-visible` (the keyboard-focus variant), leaving the mouse / touch `:focus` case to the browser default. New explicit `.railButton:focus { background-color: transparent; outline: none }` neutralises that user-agent default; `.railButton:focus-visible` continues to layer the dashed accent outline on top for keyboard nav. The left-edge accent bar (`.railButtonActive::before`) remains the canonical "this tab is active" indicator — unaffected by this change.
- **Settings panel — copy-notice button + dropdown options no longer stick "hovered" after a tap on the Pi.** Sibling audit after the Debug-panel rail-tab fix below surfaced two more `:hover` rules in `SettingsPanel/styles.css` with the same un-gated bug class: `.remoteNoticeCopyBtn:hover` and `.dropdownOption:hover`, both painting `var(--c-accent-soft)`. The dropdown case is particularly impactful because `.dropdownOptionSelected` paints the exact same colour — without the gate, a tap that briefly hovers an option on Pi tactile leaves that option looking selected until the user taps elsewhere, which is misleading (user might think they did pick the value). Both rules now sit behind `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`, identical to the dock and Debug panel fixes. macOS desktop hover behaviour is unchanged.
- **Debug panel left-rail tabs no longer look "stuck pressed" after a tap on the Pi 7" kiosk.** Same root cause as the BottomDock investigation closed by commit `faef3c3`: a `:hover` background rule (`.railButton:hover` painting `color-mix(in srgb, var(--c-accent-soft) 60%, transparent)`) was applied without an `@media (hover: hover)` gate. On Pi Chromium kiosques, `(hover: hover)` evaluates falsely as true on some configurations and there's no natural pointer-leave event on touch, so the just-tapped tab kept its hover fill until the user tapped elsewhere — which made it visually indistinguishable from the actually-active tab (`.railButtonActive` paints `var(--c-accent-soft)` plain, a colour very close to the hover's 60 %-mixed variant). Maintainer reported the symptom 2026-05-28: after navigating SERVEUR → SERVICES → SERVEUR, both SERVICES and À PROPOS continued to look pressed alongside the actually-active SERVEUR. Fix: same gate pattern as the dock — `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` requires the primary input to be precise (mouse / trackpad), which excludes a tactile Pi as primary even when a USB mouse is plugged in (touch stays primary, pointer reports as coarse). CSS-only; no JSX changes.

### Added
- **HTTPS latency now has its own colour-coded value + a second mini-scale below TCP's.** Follow-up to the dual-latency split landed in `66536d8`. The HTTPS value (full HEAD round-trip, including DNS + TCP + TLS) was previously rendered as raw diagnostic info without colour, while the TCP value carried a traffic-light tag and a mini-scale. After visual review with Claude Design and the maintainer, HTTPS gets the same treatment — its own tier helper (`httpsLatencyTier`) with **wider thresholds** (≤ 400 ms green, 400-800 ms yellow, > 800 ms red) calibrated to acknowledge the TLS handshake's natural 200-300 ms cost. The two mini-scales now stack vertically below the indicator, each prefixed by a `TCP` / `HTTPS` label in mono 9 px so the user can tell which marker reads which value. HTTPS scale uses a 0-2000 ms domain (vs. TCP's 0-1000) because HTTPS calls realistically range further than raw TCP — 2 s is the point where the `/api/update` 90 s exec timeout starts feeling tight. Segments and percentages: TCP unchanged (20 % green / 30 % yellow / 50 % red), HTTPS at 20 % / 20 % / 60 %. Either row suppresses independently if its probe failed (e.g. a middlebox blocking HTTPS but not TCP, or vice versa).
- **Internet indicator in the Debug panel now reports its probe target and splits latency into TCP + HTTPS, with traffic-light colour coding on the TCP value.** Followed up on a 2026-05-27 user question — "Internet: EN LIGNE · 431 ms… what does 431 ms even measure?" The historical indicator was a single number that conflated raw link latency with TLS handshake cost, which made it impossible to tell whether a high value meant "your link is slow" or "this is normal cold-HTTPS overhead". New format: `Internet: EN LIGNE · 1.1.1.1 · TCP 14 ms · HTTPS 297 ms`. The host (`1.1.1.1`, Cloudflare's neutral anycast endpoint) is surfaced explicitly. TCP latency is measured via a raw `net.Socket` 3-way handshake — no shell-out to `/bin/ping` so no `setcap` headaches on Linux. HTTPS latency stays as it was (full HEAD round-trip, including DNS + TCP + TLS + HEAD response). Traffic-light colour coding applies ONLY to the TCP value (the "raw ping" mental model): ≤ 200 ms green, 200-500 ms yellow, > 500 ms red. Thresholds borrowed from a Claude Design recommendation that originally targeted the single ambiguous value — applied here to TCP because that's the measurement they actually correspond to (TLS handshake alone routinely puts HTTPS in the 200-300 ms range even on a healthy link, which would yield false yellows). HTTPS remains uncoloured as raw diagnostic info — the gap between TCP and HTTPS reveals whether a slow indicator is link-bound (TCP high) or TLS-bound (TCP low, HTTPS high). New mini scale visual below the indicator: a 240 px-max horizontal bar split into three coloured segments (20 % green / 30 % yellow / 50 % red on a 0-1000 ms scale) with a vertical marker positioned at `tcpLatencyMs / 1000 × 100 %` (clamped); helps users internalise the threshold scale visually without needing to memorise the numeric tiers. `online` now resolves to true if EITHER probe succeeds (TCP or HTTPS) — defends against middleboxes that mangle one protocol but not the other.

### Fixed
- **Sense HAT alert pulse no longer drops when Tomorrow.io rate-limits (429).** Observed live on the Saskatoon kiosk 2026-05-28: the LED matrix stopped pulsing red on an active ECCC heat warning. The pi-weather-server's Debug panel showed "Services · Critique" — Tomorrow.io was 429-ing. Root cause: `sensehatCtrl.getSenseHatData` treated the weather fetch as critical path — any axios failure returned `502 "Could not fetch current weather"` immediately, **before** the alert fetch at step 5. So a Tomorrow.io rate-limit dropped the entire `/api/sensehat` response, including the alert override the LED matrix needed to keep pulsing. Inverted: the weather fetch now leaves `weatherData = null` on failure and the rest of the pipeline continues. The optional-chained unwrap at step 4 (`values = weatherData?.…?.values ?? {}`) propagates nulls into the response without crashing, and the Python script's `classify(weather_code, ...)` already handles `weather_code = null` as 1000 (Clear) — so worst case on a Tomorrow.io outage is "default weather animation for a few minutes" instead of "alert pulse silently drops". Server-side only, no client rebuild required.
- **AlertBanner gov-alert title moved to its own row so it isn't squeezed by the severity chip.** Follow-up to the partial fix that landed earlier the same day. The line-clamp / overflow-wrap tweaks did let the title wrap, but on the Pi 7" rail (~250 px) the title still only had ~100 px after the severity chip + chevron + dismiss button took their share — wrapping to 2 lines produced `"A"` then `"V.."` instead of the actual "Avertissement de chaleur". Real fix: the title was promoted out of `.topRow` (which now contains only `[chip] ... [chevron]`, chevron flush-right via `justify-content: space-between`) and onto its own row below, where it gets the full container width. This matches the Phase 4a CHANGELOG entry that said *"title now sits on its own line with the chevron at the far right"* — a structural intent that was lost in the original implementation. Cap raised from 2 to 3 lines (still defensive against pathological upstream titles) since a full-width row easily fits ~30 chars per line at 15 px. Companion `.topRow` change: `align-items` returns from `flex-start` to `center` since chip and chevron are both single-line elements again.
- **AlertBanner gov-alert title no longer truncates to a single letter on the Pi 7" rail.** Observed live on the Montreal kiosk 2026-05-28 with a Saskatoon ECCC heat warning: the head row showed `[ AVERTISSEMENT ] A. ⌃ ✕` — the title "Avertissement de chaleur" was clipped to just "A." with no recovery path. Root cause: `.govAlert .title` had `white-space: nowrap` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` + `flex: 1`, which combined with the severity chip eating 80-120 px on FR (uppercase "AVERTISSEMENT" is 12 chars) left ~100 px for the title in the 250 px-wide Pi rail. Even on shorter labels (English "WARNING" is 7 chars), titles like "Severe Thunderstorm Warning" exceeded the available width. Fix: title now wraps onto a second line via `overflow-wrap: anywhere` + a 2-line cap through `-webkit-line-clamp: 2` (so a pathologically long upstream title can't blow up the head height). Companion `.topRow { align-items: flex-start }` (was `center`) keeps the severity chip and chevron anchored to the top when the title grows downward, matching the natural reading direction. Headline alerts now read fully on the rail; the line-clamp only kicks in for titles over ~70 chars, which is unusual in NWS / ECCC vocabularies.

### Fixed
- **Sense HAT overcast animation no longer paints "blue / white / blue" diagonal waves at night.** Observed live on the Montreal kiosk 2026-05-28: with weatherCode 1001 (Cloudy) at night, the Sense HAT matrix displayed alternating blue-cyan and white-grey waves rolling diagonally — instead of the intended three-shade greyscale wave (GREY_DARK ↔ GREY_MID ↔ GREY_LIGHT). Photographed evidence confirmed clearly blue pixels in the dark phase of the wave. Root cause: the previous `GREY_*` constants had `B = R + 5` (an imperceptible blue tint at full brightness). At night, `BRIGHTNESS_NIGHT = 0.35` scales GREY_DARK to `(24, 24, 26)` — a level where R and G fall **below the LED's activation threshold** but B (one unit higher) crosses it, painting a pure-blue pixel. The Sense HAT's RGB565 quantisation (5-bit R, 6-bit G, 5-bit B) amplifies this asymmetry near the threshold. Fix: equalised R = G = B across the three shades (now `(75, 75, 75)`, `(110, 110, 110)`, `(145, 145, 145)`, keeping the higher of the old R/B as canonical so brightness is preserved). All three channels now light together or stay off together, painting a true grey regardless of brightness multiplier. Python-only change, no server restart required — the script auto-reloads at the next 60 s poll cycle.

### Fixed
- **NWS zone-based alerts (Red Flag Warning, Heat Advisory, etc.) now show their polygon overlay on the radar.** Phase 4d follow-up after the maintainer confirmed live on 2026-05-28 that Winslow AZ had two active Red Flag Warnings without the "Voir sur la carte" button — the symptom Claude Design's chat had flagged: most NWS alert types publish against `properties.affectedZones` (URLs to `/zones/forecast/{id}` or `/zones/fire/{id}`) rather than carrying an inline `feature.geometry`. AccuWeather visibly does the zone resolution; our Phase 4d had punted on it. New `server/govAlertSources/nwsZones.js` module: in-memory cache `Map<zoneUrl, {geometry, expiresAt}>` (24h TTL), `getZoneGeometry(url)` with a 5 s per-zone fetch timeout and null-fallback on any failure, `mergeAsMultiPolygon(geometries)` to combine the resolved polygons into a single MultiPolygon Leaflet renders directly. In `nws.js` `tryAlerts`, after the normalize+filter pass, alerts with null geometry trigger a parallel `Promise.all` over their `affectedZones` URLs, then assign the merged polygon back to the alert before caching the result. Performance: first call after a restart for a previously-unseen zone set takes ~750 ms (3 fetches × 2 alerts at Winslow, all in parallel); subsequent calls hit the 24h zone cache and resolve in ~50 ms total. Cache is shared across alerts — Red Flag Warnings clustered in a state typically reuse the same handful of fire zones, so the first alert pays the network cost and every sibling is free. Graceful degradation: any per-zone fetch error is silently dropped, all-fail leaves `alert.geometry: null` so the button simply doesn't render (same UX as before this commit, no regression). NWS service status comment now also reports how many alerts have a resolved geometry: `2 alert(s) at 35.0200,-110.6900 (2 with geometry)`.
- **ECCC alert text no longer drowns the real content in 100+ repeated footer lines.** Observed live 2026-05-28 on a Saskatoon heat warning fetched via the ECCC pygeoapi instance: the `alert_text_fr` field carried the actual alert content (~3 KB of weather forecast + health instructions) followed by **127 consecutive repetitions** of "Émis par Environnement Canada et le gouvernement de la Saskatchewan", bloating the payload to 12 KB and rendering the alert detail unreadable on the kiosk (the user had to scroll past hundreds of lines of footer to find the next paragraph of useful content). Likely caused upstream by ECCC's multi-region alert fusion where the issuing-authority footer is appended for each merged region without dedup. New `dedupeConsecutiveParagraphs` helper in `_shared.js` splits the text on blank-line boundaries (`\n{2,}`) and skips any paragraph whose trimmed content matches the previous paragraph's trimmed content. The first occurrence is always preserved, so the legitimate single footer remains. Net effect on the Saskatoon payload: 12 037 chars → 3 412 chars (-72 %), 127 → 2 occurrences of "Environnement Canada" (the one inside the prose + the one in the final footer). Applied defensively to both NWS and ECCC normalisers — no-op on healthy payloads.

### Added
- **Gov-alert polygon overlay on the radar (v3.1 Phase 4d).** Final slice of the v3.1 alert-panel refonte deferred from Phase 4c. The expanded `AlertDetailInline` now ends with a two-button action footer above the QR section: **« Réduire »** (always present, collapses the detail slab so the radar map underneath reclaims focus) and **« Voir sur la carte »** (conditional — only renders when the current alert carries a `geometry` field). Tapping the second button sets a new `highlightedAlertId` in `AppContext` and triggers a Leaflet `<GeoJSON>` overlay on the `WeatherMap`, styled by tier (red `#e60000` for warnings, orange `#ee7710` for watches, yellow `#f0c000` for advisories) with a 2 px border and 15 % fill so the polygon reads clearly against the radar tiles without obscuring them. A `map.fitBounds(bounds, { padding: [40, 40], maxZoom: 11 })` runs on highlight change so the user actually sees the zone (generous padding gives surrounding context; the `maxZoom: 11` cap prevents over-zoom on a small single-county polygon). Second tap on the button clears the overlay; the button label and `aria-pressed` flip to « Cacher de la carte » while active. Active state styled with `--c-accent-soft` background + accent border so the user sees "this is engaged". **Server side**: `nws.js` and `eccc.js` normalisers now propagate `geometry: feature.geometry || null`. ECCC always carries one (the server-side `pointInPolygon` filter depends on it), NWS carries it for geo-targeted alerts (Tornado Warning, Severe Thunderstorm Warning) but not for broad zone-based ones like Special Weather Statement — when null, the "Voir sur la carte" button simply doesn't render, while "Réduire" stays put as the universal affordance. **Decision on shape**: Claude Design originally proposed three flavours (always-shown button with disabled state when no geometry, always-shown button that fetches `affectedZones` from NWS API to merge polygons, or replace with a plain "collapse" button). We landed on the hybrid: collapse always + show-on-map when meaningful. Disabled-state was rejected because the maintainer's most common NWS payload (Special Weather Statement, used as the mockup reference) has no geometry, which would have left the button disabled most of the time. Fetching affected zones was rejected because of the extra network round-trips and complexity on an embedded Pi. The hybrid honours both intents — universal control via "Réduire", precision overlay when the upstream payload supports it. 6 new i18n keys × 3 locales: `alert.collapse` + `collapseAria`, `alert.showOnMap` + `showOnMapAria`, `alert.hideOnMap` + `hideOnMapAria`. Defensive CSS state-reset on the new buttons (`:focus { background: transparent; outline: none }`, `:active { background: transparent }`, `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent`) follows the pattern locked in by `incident_hover_gate_insufficient_chromium_touch.md` so a freshly-tapped action button doesn't keep a residual fill on Pi tactile.

### Added
- **GitHub Git-Operations status now surfaces in the HealthIndicator popover.** Motivated by the 2026-05-27 morning incident where GitHub's Git Operations was in degraded performance, causing the in-app `git pull` to time out without any upstream-side hint visible to the kiosk owner. GitHub is special among our upstreams because we never call it from runtime (the in-app updater spawns `git pull` as a one-shot child process — no `recordServiceCall` ever fires for it), so the statuspage is our only signal for it. Implementation: `server/debugCtrl.js` `PROVIDER_STATUS_APIS` gains a 7th entry (`statuspage-component` against `https://www.githubstatus.com/api/v2/components.json` for the "Git Operations" component); `fetchProviderStatus` is now exported so `healthCtrl.js` can consume it. `/api/health` response gains a new `providerStatus: { github: { name, indicator, description } }` field. The HealthIndicator popover renders a new "Upstream services" section below the existing issue list, with a colour-coded dot keyed by the statuspage indicator (`none` → green, `minor` → yellow, `major` → orange, `critical` → red, `maintenance` → blue, `unknown` → grey) and the localised status label ("Opérationnel" / "Performance dégradée" / "Panne partielle" / "Panne majeure" / "En maintenance" / "État inconnu"). The chip's color classification is **unchanged** — `providerStatus` does NOT influence the green/yellow/red logic. Rationale: the kiosk is fully functional without GitHub (only the update flow is affected), so painting the chip yellow for a GitHub-only issue would mis-represent the system state. The popover section is the right level of detail — visible when the user taps the chip, invisible otherwise. Other providers in `PROVIDER_STATUS_APIS` (Tomorrow.io, Mapbox, etc.) keep their statuspages in the Debug panel only — those have direct `recordServiceCall`-backed signals through the regular service pipeline, so surfacing their statuspage in the popover would be redundant or confusingly conflicting. 7 new i18n keys × 3 locales (EN / FR / ES): `health.providerStatusHeader`, `health.provider.github`, and the six `health.providerIndicator.*` entries.

### Fixed
- **Debug panel key/value rows now wrap long values instead of clipping with an ellipsis.** Observed on the SenseHat Pi at 800×480: the `VERSION` line (`pi-weather-station v2.18.1 · 260df42`) was cut mid-commit-SHA, and the `REPO` line (`github.com/thicla01/pi-weather-station`) ended in `…pi-weather-st` with the trailing characters invisible. Both values carried genuinely useful diagnostic data that's awkward to recover on the kiosk (no mouse for the hover tooltip, no easy access to the CSV export). Loosened `.kvValue` to allow wrapping: `white-space: normal` (default) + `overflow-wrap: anywhere`. The first break attempt is at whitespace (so `v2.18.1 · 260df42` splits cleanly at the spaces or middle dot before any character-level break), and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` is the safety net for single unbreakable tokens like a long URL. Row heights become slightly variable as a result, but completeness wins over strict grid regularity for a debug panel that's already an in-depth view. `.rowName` (Services tab — service status rows) and `.quotaName` (Services tab — quota cards) keep their ellipsis since they live in grid layouts at ~140 px column widths where wrapping would make the grid jagged; those are revisitable if field testing shows the same problem there.
- **Post-update reload now reliably picks up the new bundle (Chromium heuristic cache fix).** Observed on the SenseHat Pi on 2026-05-27: in-app updater ran, `git pull` confirmed the device was on the latest commit, but the UI didn't show the changes until a manual hard refresh. Root cause: webpack outputs `bundle.min.js` (and split chunks `1.bundle.min.js`, `810.bundle.min.js`) with FIXED filenames across builds, and `express.static` was serving them without an explicit `Cache-Control` header. Chromium then computed a heuristic `max-age` from `Last-Modified` (~10 % of the file's age — for a bundle that's been on disk a few hours, that's tens of minutes of cache the browser serves WITHOUT revalidating). So after `systemctl restart pi-weather-server` wrote a new `bundle.min.js`, the kiosk's `window.location.reload()` would happily serve the pre-update copy from the local cache until the heuristic timer expired. Tiered Cache-Control now applied to `dist/`: (1) hashed assets (`[contenthash][ext]` — fonts, JSON, etc.) get `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` since the filename itself changes on content change; (2) bundle / chunks / HTML / license txt (fixed names, content changes per build) get `no-cache` — forces an `If-Modified-Since` revalidation on every request, server replies 304 Not Modified when unchanged (~150 bytes per check, negligible cost), guaranteeing a post-update reload always picks up the fresh bundle; (3) PWA icons / favicon (fixed names, change rarely) get a 24 h max-age so they stay out of the reload path without locking them in forever. The existing `Cache-Control: no-store` on `/api/*` is unchanged. Server-side only; no client rebuild required.
- **`/api/update` no longer fails with a cryptic message on slow / VPN'd Pis.** Observed on a Pi reached via VPN + RPi Connect (the HMIRaspiFR field unit): the in-app updater returned `git pull failed: Command failed: git pull --ff-only` with no stderr, but a follow-up SSH `git status` showed the branch had actually pulled cleanly and was on the latest commit. Root cause: the `exec` call wrapping `git pull` had a 30 s timeout, but a Pi reaching github.com through a VPN tunnel routinely needs 40-60 s to complete handshake + fetch. Node's `exec` killed the child with SIGTERM at the 30 s mark and propagated `pullErr.message = "Command failed: git pull --ff-only"` with empty stderr (git hadn't had time to write anything). The pull usually completed in the background after the kill — explaining the "clean status" anomaly — but the API call still returned an error. Two changes: (1) bumped `exec` timeout to 90 s so VPN'd / mobile / congested-Wi-Fi pulls have comfortable margin (local pulls stay sub-second so the bump is invisible there); (2) added explicit timeout detection in the error handler (`pullErr.killed` or `pullErr.signal === "SIGTERM"`) that returns a 504 with a clear actionable message — "git pull took longer than 90 seconds and was aborted… the pull may have completed in the background, wait a moment then retry" — instead of the bare "Command failed" string. Server-side only; no client rebuild needed.
- **HealthIndicator suppression window widened to outlive the slowest poll cadence.** Companion to the unconfigured-alternative fix below. The `RECENT_SUCCESS_WINDOW_MS` threshold that gates both `isFailure` (a service is allowed to flake once if it succeeded recently) and `suppressedByGroupSibling` (an alternative-chain service's failure is suppressed if a regional sibling succeeded recently) was at 10 min — but the AQ refresh poll runs at 30 min (`AQI_REFRESH_MS` in `AppContext.js`). So for 20 min out of every 30 min cycle, MELCC RSQA's last success had aged out of the window, and any sibling failure (EPA AirNow 401 because of an invalid key, OpenAQ 401, etc.) re-surfaced on the chip even though MELCC was actively serving the user's AQI. Bumped the window to 35 min (slowest poll + 5 min buffer) so the previous-cycle success stays counted until the next cycle's success lands. Trade-off: a service that genuinely starts failing now takes up to ~35 min to surface, but we can't detect a regression faster than the poll itself — narrowing the window below the poll cadence just creates false positives without speeding up real-failure detection. Particularly relevant for a kiosque that physically travels between regions (e.g. demo unit tested in US, then brought back to Canada) where a previously-successful EPA AirNow develops a regional 401 — the bumped window plus `isUnconfiguredAlternative` suppression covers both transitions cleanly.
- **HealthIndicator no longer reports "degraded" for air-quality sources that have never been configured / never been relevant to the user's region.** Observed live on 2026-05-26 at a Montreal kiosk: chip turned amber listing `EPA AirNow — HTTP 401 · fetch failed` and `OpenAQ — HTTP 401 · locations search failed`, while `MELCC RSQA (Montreal)` was responding correctly with the user's actual AQI (35 IQA). Two layered causes: (1) AirNow's `pointInUSBox` geofence is intentionally permissive — `lonMin: -180, lonMax: -65` to cover Alaska + Hawaii + Puerto Rico — but that also includes Eastern Canada (Montreal at -73.6° lon falls inside). So AirNow gets called for Montreal, hits a 401 (either no key configured or the key is invalid for this region), and records a failure. (2) OpenAQ has no geofence at all and tries every lat/lon as long as a key is set — same 401 outcome for the same reason. Existing `ALTERNATIVE_GROUPS` suppression compares `lastSuccess` ages, but if a service has NEVER succeeded (no `lastSuccess` at all), the sibling-suppression check returns false. New suppression rule in `healthCtrl.js`: a service in an alternative-chain group with `lastSuccess === null` is ignored as a failure — by definition it hasn't regressed (it's never been up), so reporting it as "degraded" is misleading. Once a user properly configures the service and it succeeds once, `lastSuccess` is populated and any subsequent failure goes back to being reported normally. Critical services (Tomorrow.io, Mapbox, etc.) are NOT in any alternative group, so a critical service that's never succeeded still surfaces — that's a real config problem the user needs to see. Net effect for the Montreal kiosk: `MELCC RSQA (Montreal)` works, MELCC RSQAQ + ECCC AQHI are dormant siblings (also `lastSuccess: null`, suppressed by the same rule when they're not the active region), EPA AirNow + OpenAQ are dormant unconfigured-for-this-region siblings (suppressed by the new rule), so the chip stays green as it should.
- **Unstructured alert paragraphs now use the same dividers as parsed sections.** When the alert parser can't find structure (e.g. an ECCC English thunderstorm watch with no `*WHAT...` NWS keywords or `Dangers :` French headings — observed live on the 2026-05-26 Portneuf QC alert), it falls back to splitting the description on `\n\n` and rendering plain paragraphs. The previous styling left those paragraphs as a wall of prose with only a small `margin-bottom: 10px` between them — visually inconsistent with the structured-section path right next to it which renders sections separated by 14 px margin + 14 px padding + a 1 px border-top divider. New `.text + .text` rule applies the exact same divider treatment between consecutive paragraphs in the fallback. The dividers carry no semantic load (no icon, no lead row) — they're purely a rhythm cue so an unstructured ECCC EN advisory and a structured NWS Severe Thunderstorm Warning use the same visual grammar. Applies in both the top-level fallback path (when the entire description has no structure) and inside `.intro` (the leading prose before the first heading when the parser DID find structure further down). Pure CSS change; no JSX or parser logic touched.
- **Hover affordance restored on dock buttons (desktop-only), now safely gated.** With Chromium's tap-highlight overlay neutralized (commit `8a229df`), the `:hover` background can come back on the dock without re-introducing the sticky-grey symptom users reported on the Pi. New gate: `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` instead of just `(hover: hover)`. The `(pointer: fine)` clause requires the **primary** input to be precise (mouse / trackpad) — a Pi tactile kiosk reports `pointer: coarse` as its primary even with a USB mouse plugged in (touch stays primary), so this combination matches only real desktop sessions. Defensive: even if a hover event accidentally fires on a tactile Pi (which the previous `(hover: hover)` solo guard couldn't always prevent), the painted overlay is `color-mix(in oklab, var(--c-text) 8%, transparent)` — a neutral 8% text tint that's visibly distinct from `--c-accent-soft` (the `.button-down` toggle-ON fill), so even a hypothetical residual misfire wouldn't be misread as a stuck toggle. macOS Chrome / Firefox / Safari users see hover restored exactly as it was after commit `1c1ea02`.

### Fixed
- **Chromium's default tap-highlight overlay no longer sticks on dock buttons after a tap (Pi-only bug).** Final piece of the dock-button "stuck-looking after tap" investigation. Earlier passes targeted `:active` (commit `1c1ea02`), then `:hover` (commit `f20b8a2`), but the user reported the symptom still reproducing on both Pis tested (one with USB mouse, one without; Multitouch DSI confirmed enabled on at least one of them). Root cause: `-webkit-tap-highlight-color` was never overridden on `.container > div` in `ControlButtons/styles.css`, so Chromium's browser-level default (`rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.12)` translucent grey rectangle painted on tap) was the actual sticky overlay — not anything our CSS was doing. The overlay is touch-only (mouse events don't trigger it, explaining why macOS Chrome / Firefox never showed the bug), persists until the next pointerdown elsewhere on the Pi (no natural pointer-leave on touch), and matches the user's description verbatim ("a grey colour that looks like mouse-over"). Reproducible on every Pi regardless of Multitouch DSI mode or USB-mouse presence because it's browser-level, not platform-level. Fix: `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on `.container > div`. The property inherits, so the grouped-mode `.container.grouped .group > div` buttons are covered too without needing a duplicate rule. Same pattern `HealthIndicator` already uses on `.dotButton` (line 38) and `.chipButton` (line 255) — which is exactly why the chip never exhibited the same bug. The CSS now matches that precedent across the dock. Mental-model note: the `-webkit-` prefix is misleading (it's not Safari-specific) — the property is implemented in Chromium / WebKit-derived browsers and ignored elsewhere; Firefox Android has its own equivalent (`tap-highlight-color: none` in some builds, no effect in others) but doesn't paint a default highlight, so kiosk-Firefox users aren't affected.
- **Dock buttons no longer paint a sticky hover overlay on Pi touchscreens.** Follow-up to `1c1ea02` — that pass had remapped the dock's `:hover` background from `--c-accent-soft` (which collided with `.button-down`'s toggle-ON fill) to a neutral 8% text overlay, and gated the rule behind `@media (hover: hover)` so it would only paint on hover-capable devices. Field-tested on the Pi 7" kiosk: Chromium evaluates `(hover: hover)` as true on some configurations (Multitouch DSI mode off, mouse-emulation pointer events from the official touchscreen, a USB mouse plugged in for kiosk maintenance), so the `@media` guard does NOT reliably exclude tactile devices. When it falsely matches on a tactile Pi, the `:hover` painted at tap-time stays applied until the next pointerdown elsewhere (touch has no natural pointer-leave event) — the just-tapped button reads as "stuck in a hover state" with the neutral overlay. Dropped the `:hover` background rule entirely. Aligned with v2.14.74 which had already removed the hover `color` change on the SVG icons for the same touch-sticky reason, and with HealthIndicator's chip (no hover state, `[aria-expanded]` as the sole accent signal). Affordance loss is minor — dock buttons are identifiable by grouped icon layout, and `.button-down !important` still paints `--c-accent-soft` on real ON toggles (timeline, legend, automatic, night-red, AI summary, radar rings, settings-open, debug-open). The complementary maintenance recommendation — enabling Multitouch on DSI-1 per `docs/troubleshooting-touchscreen.md` — also helps because mouse-emulation taps are what made `(hover: hover)` falsely match in the first place, but the CSS fix lands the behaviour deterministically regardless of per-Pi config.
- **Mobile dock toast now sizes to its content instead of spanning the entire viewport.** An earlier fix (`bcb5669`) set `left: 12px` + `right: 12px` on the toast when the viewport was ≤ 600 px, to defeat a clipping bug where per-button anchoring near the dock's right edge made the browser's shrink-to-fit width starve the box ("6+ line wraps on a 70-char French toast that should fit on 2 lines"). That solved the clipping, but flipped to the opposite problem: short toasts like "Marqueur affiché" / "Mode jour activé" stretched the full viewport-width on phones with masses of empty horizontal space, reading as a much heavier banner than they should. New approach on narrow viewports: `left: 50%` + the existing CSS default `--toast-tx: -50%` (the same translate the wide-viewport per-button mode already uses) centres the toast on the VIEWPORT rather than the tapped button. Width becomes intrinsic to the text content, capped by the pre-existing CSS `max-width: min(360px, calc(100vw - 24px))` so long toasts still get ~360 px (the 2-line budget on Geist 13) and short toasts stay as wide as their text. The per-button visual anchor is intentionally dropped on phones — accepted trade-off since the dock is small, the user's finger is right there, and honest sizing wins over precise pointing. The `useLayoutEffect` clamp that nudges the toast away from a viewport edge now runs in both modes (was skipped in the old full-width mode); it's a no-op when the max-width does its job but defends against any future content path that overflows via padding / border math.
- **BottomDock toast no longer hides behind the radar map on small screens.** Third iteration on a recurring class of bug — previous fixes were `position: absolute → fixed` (commit `282e53a`, to escape the LayoutPi `.layout` overflow clip) and `z-index: 20 → 1500` (commit `0018c32`, to clear Leaflet's panes at z-index 1000 and the radar scrubber at z-index 500). Both fixes were silently invalidated by Phase 1 v3.1 (commit `7aeb430`) when `backdrop-filter: blur(8px)` was added to `.dock` for the frosted-glass look. Per the CSS Filter Effects spec, a non-`none` `backdrop-filter` creates BOTH a containing block AND a stacking context — which confined the toast's z-index 1500 to the dock's internal stacking context. On LayoutDesktop the dock is `position: absolute; z-index: 10`, so the confined toast still rode on top of the map via the dock's own positioned context. On LayoutPi (CSS grid, dock cell statically positioned) and LayoutMobile (flex column, dock as last child with no z-index), the dock had no stacking context of its own in the root, so Leaflet's panes at z-index 1000 painted over the dock entirely — including the toast confined inside it. Fix: lift the dock cell into the root stacking context with `position: relative; z-index: 1100` in both `LayoutPi/styles.css .layout > :last-child` and `LayoutMobile/styles.css .layout > :last-child`. 1100 wins against Leaflet's panes (1000) and the scrubber (500), stays below SettingsPanel / DebugPanel (5000) so opening either still covers the dock. The bug was reproducible across the Pi fleet (variable Chromium versions) and on macOS Firefox + Chrome with the browser window resized below ~1280 px (LayoutPi threshold). Cross-referenced in `incident_dock_toast_stacking_context.md` so the next contributor to add `filter` / `backdrop-filter` / `transform` / `will-change` to any dock-ancestor element knows the pattern.
- **BottomDock buttons no longer look "stuck on" after a tap or hover.** Two unrelated CSS rules were independently painting the dock's `--c-accent-soft` token outside of the canonical `.button-down` (toggle-ON) state, creating a visible ambiguity where non-toggle actions (recenter, marker, contrast, refresh) and dismissed toggles (settings close, debug close) looked indistinguishable from a real ON toggle until the user moved on to the next button. (1) `BottomDock/styles.css` set `--ctrl-btn-active: var(--c-accent-soft)` — same fill as `.button-down`. On touchscreens `:active` stays applied after the tap until the next pointerdown elsewhere (Chromium and Firefox both — the touch pointer has no natural "leave" event), so the just-tapped button kept its accent fill. (2) `ControlButtons/styles.css` `@media (hover: hover)` set `:hover { background-color: var(--c-accent-soft) }` on grouped-mode buttons — also matching `.button-down`. On desktop the cursor parks on the just-clicked button, `:hover` persists until the user moves the mouse, and a non-toggle button reads "still on" until then. Fixes: (1) `--ctrl-btn-active: transparent` in the dock — no `:active` press flash on touch; the `notify()` toast already confirms tap registration. (2) `:hover` background changed to a neutral `color-mix(in oklab, var(--c-text) 8%, transparent)` overlay tied to the palette's text token, so the hover affordance stays present on desktop but visibly different from the accent-soft toggle-ON fill. `.button-down !important` continues to win in both directions, so real ON toggles (timeline, legend, automatic, night-red, AI summary, radar rings, settings-open, debug-open) keep their accent fill as designed. Discrimination is intrinsic to which JSX nodes carry the `styles.buttonDown` class — non-toggles (recenter, marker, refresh) and OFF-state toggles drop back to a clean transparent surface the moment the press / hover ends.
- **Moon-phase glyph now renders as an inline SVG so the orientation is consistent across macOS and the Pi kiosk.** Previously the moon phases (`🌑🌒🌓🌔🌕🌖🌗🌘`) rendered as Unicode emoji, which relied on the host OS's emoji font: Apple Color Emoji on macOS draws waxing phases with the lit side on the right (Northern Hemisphere convention) and Noto Color Emoji on the Pi (Trixie / Bookworm Chromium) draws them with the orientation reversed. The Pi rendering looked "inverted" in night + nightRed modes specifically — the dark slab background made the high-contrast lit shape obvious, so the wrong orientation became unmistakable. (Dusk and clair were technically affected too but the lower contrast in those palettes masked the issue.) New `~/components/ambient/MoonGlyph` is a parameterised SVG: a `<circle>` filled with `--c-moon-dark` (the shadow side) with a `<path>` filled with `--c-moon-lit` (the lit phase) drawn on top. The path is generated by a pure `moonLitPath(fraction, cx, cy, r)` helper in `~/ui/astronomy` — bounds the lit area with one half of the disc edge and one half of the terminator ellipse (an ellipse whose horizontal radius `r × |cos(2π × fraction)|` collapses to a vertical line at the quarters and to the disc edge at new / full). Sweep flags choose left vs right side of the disc (NH waxing on the right, waning on the left) and the inner ellipse's bulge direction (crescent bulges into the lit side, gibbous bulges into the dark side). Defensive: empty path at near-new-moon (k < 0.005) skips drawing entirely; a full-disc double-arc shape at near-full-moon (k > 0.995) avoids near-degenerate ellipse artefacts; non-finite / out-of-range fractions wrap safely. New palette tokens `moonLit` / `moonDark` × 4 palettes propagated as `--c-moon-lit` / `--c-moon-dark` via `AmbientLayers`. Day palette tokens were initially mirrored from the v3.1 synthesis design (`moonLit: #1c1810` dark + `moonDark: #d8cfb8` light — silhouette style with a dark "lit" shape on a light disc) but field-tested as confusing: users read the dark crescent as the shadow side and mistook a waxing crescent for waning gibbous. Aligned day on the same "dark disc + bright lit shape" convention every other palette uses (`moonLit: #f4e4a8` cream-yellow, `moonDark: #7a6a48` warm brown) — matches universal emoji iconography (Apple Color Emoji, all standard moon icons) so the visual reading is consistent across all four modes. **Also fixed**: portaled `DetailsPopover` was missing `--c-moon-lit` / `--c-moon-dark` in its explicit CSS-var copy whitelist, causing `MoonDetailsPopover` to render the moon as a fully-black disc regardless of palette (the portal renders to `document.body`, outside `.ambientRoot`'s variable injection scope). Added both tokens to the whitelist so the popover inherits the active palette like the rest of the moon UI. 12 new tests in `test/moonLitPath.test.js` lock the eight phases (new, waxing crescent, first quarter, waxing gibbous, full, waning gibbous, last quarter, waning crescent) plus edge cases (non-finite input, out-of-range fractions wrapping modulo 1, terminator-width tracking the cosine). Verified end-to-end across day, night, and night-red palettes on macOS — Pi validation deferred to the next field session but the SVG is fully platform-agnostic so the original bug class is now structurally eliminated.

### Added
- **Multi-alert mini-cards list + passive counter + restore dismissed (v3.1 Phase 4c).** Third and final slice of the v3.1 alert-panel refonte. The cycle pill "1 / 2" in the head that used to scroll between active alerts is replaced by a **list of mini-cards** below the primary card, each showing one of the OTHER active alerts (severity chip + truncated title + chevron, tier-coloured left border). Cards sort by severity descending — warnings rise above watches rise above advisories — with ties broken by expiration (earlier expiry → higher in the list). Tap a card to swap it into the primary slot; the previous primary moves down into the list (or disappears if dismissed/expired). New component `AlertMiniCards` lives as a sibling under `AlertDetailInline` in all three layouts (LayoutDesktop, LayoutPi, LayoutMobile). The head's cycle pill becomes a **passive informational counter** ("1 / 3 active alerts") — same position in the meta-chips row but no longer a button, since cycling moves to tap-on-card. `AppContext` gains `selectGovAlert(idx)` — bounds-checked, used by the mini-cards' tap handler to jump to a specific alert by its position in the visible alerts list (the same list AlertBanner picks from, NOT the absolute govAlerts list — keeping the index space consistent across components prevents the "primary appears as both head and mini-card" duplication that surfaces when dismissals shift the visible position). **Restore-dismissed pill** at the bottom of the alert area surfaces when `localStorage.dismissedAlerts` holds at least one non-stale entry — closes the UX recovery loop from 4a feedback ("how do I get back an alert I accidentally hid?"). Tap calls `restoreAll()` from `useDismissedAlerts`; the pill disappears the moment the count drops to 0. Pill uses Geist Mono 10 px in `--c-text-dim` to read as a calm maintenance affordance, not an alert. **Cross-component dismiss-state sync** — the hook now dispatches a `CustomEvent("pi-weather-dismissed-alerts-changed")` on every write and every instance in the tab listens for it. Without this, each instance had its own React state copy and only re-read localStorage on its 60 s purge tick, leading to a noticeable 1-2 min lag between a restore in AlertMiniCards and AlertBanner stopping to show the radar fallback. Custom event closes that gap to a single render frame. Also listens for the native `storage` event so multi-tab kiosk setups stay in sync. 5 new i18n keys × 3 locales: `alert.activeAlertsCount` (with `_one`/`_other` plural variants), `alert.selectAlertAria`, `alert.restoreDismissed` (also pluralised), `alert.restoreDismissedAria`. End-to-end verified on a 2-alert NWS scenario at Panama City FL (Special Marine Warning + Rip Current Statement) — dismiss/restore flow, swap-by-mini-card, counter updates, restore pill appearance/disappearance all live across day/night/nightRed palettes.
- **Government-alert body — structured-section parser for NWS + ECCC text (v3.1 Phase 4b).** Second slice of the v3.1 alert-panel refonte. The wall-of-text description that used to render as flat `\n\n`-split paragraphs now flows through a unified parser (`~/ui/alertParser.js`) that produces a list of `{ type, lead, detail }` sections — each rendered by `AlertDetailInline` with a tier-specific icon, a localized lead row, and the upstream detail text. Fallback to paragraph-split rendering when the parser detects no structure, so unstructured ECCC summaries (e.g. simple watch alerts) still display readably. Six canonical section types, each with a dedicated Carbon icon: `hazard` ⚠ (HAZARD / IMPACTS / Dangers / Risques — the danger itself), `observation` 🔭 (binoculars; the timestamped sighting in NWS `At <time>, severe thunderstorms were located...` leads), `source` 📡 (radar-weather; the `SOURCE...Radar indicated.` instrument meta), `where` 📍 (Affected areas — `WHERE...` / `Locations impacted include...` / `Special X Warning for...`), `when` 🕒 (`WHEN...` / `Until <time>` / `Timing:` / `Période :`), `action` ⚡ (`PRECAUTIONARY/PREPAREDNESS ACTIONS...` / `What to do:` / `Mesures à prendre :`), plus an `intro` paragraph and a neutral `section` fallback (document icon) for headings the parser recognises but can't classify. **Parser strategy:** (1) try NWS asterisk split (`/(?:^|\n)\s*\*\s+/`), with pre-processing that promotes embedded `HAZARD...` / `SOURCE...` / `IMPACT...` ALL-CAPS keywords to their own section starts (NWS Special Marine Warnings embed these inside bullet bodies rather than as bullets themselves); (2) try ECCC heading split on `^([A-Z][\w' À-ÿ]+)\s*:\s*$`; (3) fall back to single intro section. Heading extraction handles three shapes — ALL-CAPS-keyword + `...` (canonical NWS), mixed-case lead + `...`/`:` (NWS Marine Warning's `Special Marine Warning for...`, `Locations impacted include...`), and time-prefix + comma (`At 538 PM CDT,` / `Until 845 PM CDT`). Classification uses ordered regex matching so longer-specific patterns (`what to do`) beat the bare prefix (`what`). **NWS metadata scrubber** strips three parasite patterns from the intro before rendering: (a) the `<Alert Type> issued <date> at <time> by NWS <Office>` header (already shown in title + meta chips), (b) NWS internal product codes like `SMWTAE` / `TORWWS`, (c) `The National Weather Service in <City> has issued a` dangling sentence that NWS deliberately writes as incomplete (the first asterisked bullet completes it, but post-parsing the dangling `a` reads as truncated). After scrubbing, NWS alerts with no remaining intro content skip the intro section entirely. ECCC intros pass through untouched. **Server side**, the `eccc.js` `sentAt` extractor now reads `publication_datetime` (the actual ECCC pygeoapi field name) instead of looking up the CAP-spec `sent` which the pygeoapi instance renames. **Layout details:** the `SectionBlock` falls back to using the parser's `lead` as the detail when `detail` is empty, so short single-line sections like `Until 845 PM CDT.` (lead-only, no body) still render visibly instead of silently dropping. Section icons use `--c-accent` at full opacity (not `--c-accent-soft` — the soft variant is a 16-18 % alpha tint meant for backgrounds, not foregrounds; it rendered icons as invisible washouts on the night palette). Three intensity tiers (full text / accent icon / dim detail) keep the visual hierarchy readable across all four palettes. 6 new i18n keys × 3 locales: `alert.sectionHazard` / `sectionObservation` / `sectionSource` / `sectionWhere` / `sectionWhen` / `sectionAction`. New module `~/ui/alertParser.js` covered by 22 tests in `test/alertParser.test.js` — including the real NWS Special Marine Warning payload from Bay County FL (2026-05-25), ECCC English + French heading formats from Thunder Bay, and edge cases (empty input, asterisks-without-leading-newline, embedded ALL-CAPS keywords, mixed-case leads with comma delimiters). Verified end-to-end against a live NWS Flood Watch in Pensacola FL, a Special Marine Warning at Panama City FL, and an ECCC severe thunderstorm watch at Lac St-Jean QC — across day, night, and night-red palettes.
- **Government-alert banner — severity chip, meta chips row, and click-to-expand head (v3.1 Phase 4a).** First slice of the v3.1 alert-panel refonte proposed by Claude Design — resolves the audit's F15, F16, F17 findings and lays the groundwork for the structured-body parser (Phase 4b) and the multi-alert mini-cards list (Phase 4c) coming next. **Head row now leads with a `SeverityChip`** — triangle icon + uppercase label ("Advisory" / "Watch" / "Warning") + palette colour. Three signals carried redundantly so the chip survives night-red mode (where all tier colours collapse to red but the icon + word still discriminate) and colour blindness. Severity values from the alert payload (`minor` / `moderate` / `severe` / `extreme`) map to the three tier slugs; `extreme` collapses to `warning` visually because the wall-of-red palette + the label inside the chip already convey the extra urgency. 9 new palette tokens per mode (`sevAdvBg/Border/Ink` × 3 tiers) propagated to CSS as `--sev-{adv|watch|warn}-{bg|border|ink}`. **Title now sits on its own line** with the chevron at the far right — the whole head is the toggle (F15: stop the chevron-aiming game), tappable anywhere to expand. **Meta-chips row under the title** carries the issuing office (`NWS Gray ME` / `ECCC ON`), a `Issued Nh ago` chip in the palette accent for freshness (F16), and an `Expires <weekday> HH:MM` chip — three pieces of glanceable info that used to require expanding the banner to read. Server-side, both `nws.js` and `eccc.js` normalisers gain `sentAt` (from upstream `properties.sent`) and `senderName` (from `properties.senderName` for NWS, `ECCC <province>` fallback for ECCC) so the client can render the meta chips for both sources without branching. **`AlertDetailInline` simplified** — the in-component toggle button is gone; the slab reads `govAlertExpanded` from a freshly-hoisted `AppContext` state and renders only when the banner is expanded. A separate cycle pill (`1 / 2`) lives at the right end of the meta-chips row as a transitional affordance until Phase 4c's mini-cards list lands and makes multi-alert navigation tappable inline. New components: `SeverityChip`, `AlertMetaChips` (with internal `formatIssued` / `formatExpires` time-formatting helpers). 11 new i18n keys × 3 locales under `alert.*` (severity labels, "Issued N ago" forms in min/hour/day granularity, "Expires {when}", expand/collapse ARIA strings). Verified visually on EDT-zone NWS feed (Severe Thunderstorm Warning + Special Weather Statement multi-alert scenario).
- **Toolbar grouped into three labelled sections + traffic-light status chip (v3.1 Phase 1).** The flat row of dock icons in `BottomDock` now splits into three labelled groups — **Map · Display · System** (FR: Carte / Affichage / Système, ES: Mapa / Visualización / Sistema) — separated by 1 px hairline rules. Scanning three groups of 4-7 icons reads in one fixation; the previous 13-icon row required pattern-matching against memorized positions. Group labels are uppercase Geist Mono 9 px and CSS-hide on viewports ≤ 980 px (7" Pi kiosk + phone landscape) so the dock fits the icons themselves first; the visual grouping is preserved by the separators alone at those widths. The `HealthIndicator` dot also lands its first real surface upgrade — converted into a pill-shaped status chip pinned to the right edge of the dock, containing the coloured disc (✓ glyph on green, ! glyph on yellow/red) and the short label `Services · OK / Dégradé / Critique / Hors ligne`. The text portion CSS-collapses on the 7" Pi (icon-only pill) and shrinks to a transparent disc target on phone portrait, so the glanceable signal survives every breakpoint. Implementation: `ControlButtons` gains a `grouped` boolean prop (default false — the legacy v2 `InfoPanel` consumer keeps the flat row untouched), `HealthIndicator` gains a `chip` boolean prop with the same backward-compat default, and `BottomDock` passes both flags. The shared toast positioning logic still anchors against `e.currentTarget.getBoundingClientRect()` — group wrapping doesn't affect the toast's viewport coordinates. The chip background uses `--c-surface-hybrid` (96 % alpha) so it lifts visually off the dock's `--c-surface` (85 %), matching the "surface-2" elevation step in the synthesis design. From the v3.1 synthesis mockup proposed by Claude Design; deltas P2/P4/P5 (compact alert, fixed 2×2 metrics, side-column reorg) deferred. 6 new i18n keys × 3 locales (`controls.groupMap/groupDisplay/groupSystem`, `health.chipPrefix/shortOk/shortDegraded/shortCritical/shortOffline`).
- **Tap-for-details popover on the location name (city chip).** Mirrors the moon / sun popover pattern shipped earlier in this Unreleased cycle — tapping the location in the hero band (`HeroBand` on desktop, `HeroCompact` on Pi/mobile) opens a `LocationDetailsPopover` listing the full LocationIQ reverse-geocode payload that `LocationName` necessarily truncates to fit the panel: city / town / village, neighbourhood, county or MRC, state or region, country (with ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code), postal code, and the marker's precise coordinates to 5 decimals. Rows render only when LocationIQ returned a value for that field, so a sparsely-mapped point doesn't show empty rows. Fallback row when the reverse geocode failed (ocean, unmapped area, no API key) still shows the marker coords + a "no address found" note so the popover is never empty. Coordinates use `mapGeo` (the marker position) rather than LocationIQ's snapped `lat`/`lon` — matches the user's mental model of "where am I looking?" since LocationIQ may shift up to a few hundred metres to the nearest mapped feature. Implementation hoists the reverse-geocode fetch out of `LocationName` into `AppContext` as `reverseGeoResult` so both the name component and the popover read from the same source without duplicating the request; `LocationName` becomes a pure display component. Three-state sentinel (`undefined` = in-flight, `null` = settled empty, object = data) avoids the brief lat/lon flash on cold boot, and the previous payload stays in place across `mapGeo` changes until the new fetch settles — same UX as the pre-hoist behaviour. The underlying `reverseGeocode` service already cached by coord pair, so re-pointing the marker at a previously seen location costs zero extra requests. 10 new i18n keys × 3 locales under `location.*`. Reuses the generic `DetailsPopover` shell (palette-faithful portal mode, viewport clipping, backdrop + Esc dismissal) so the popover inherits all the polish already shipped for the moon / sun / UV / AQ surfaces.
- **Tap-for-details popover on the sunrise / sunset chips.** Parallels the moon-phase chip's `MoonDetailsPopover` (shipped in v2.17). Tapping either the ☀ sunrise or 🌙 sunset chip in the clock panel opens a `SunDetailsPopover` with two sections: **Today** (first light, last light, day length — sunrise/sunset themselves are intentionally omitted because they're already on the chip that opened the popover) and **Tomorrow** (first light, sunrise, sunset, last light, day length). "First light" and "last light" are the civil twilight begin/end (~30 min before sunrise / after sunset) — useful for outdoor planning and the metric the maintainer specifically asked for. Day length renders as "Xh YYmin" computed from upstream `day_length` seconds; em-dash fallback on polar edge cases (sun never rises / never sets) or when an upstream call failed. Both chips open the same popover (single `sunOpen` state, sunrise chip is the anchor for positioning). Mutual exclusivity with the moon popover — opening one closes the other so the two never visually collide in the narrow Pi/mobile rail. Data source: `sunrise-sunset.org` (same as the chip values themselves, so the popover times are guaranteed consistent with what the user already sees on the kiosk surface — Tomorrow.io's daily-payload sunriseTime/sunsetTime would have introduced ±1-2 min divergence from a different horizon algorithm). Server-side, `/api/sunrise-sunset` gains an optional `tomorrow=1` query param that fan-outs to two parallel upstream calls and returns `{ results, tomorrowResults }`; tomorrow's failure is non-fatal (logged, omitted from response, popover renders em-dashes for that section). Backward-compatible: without `tomorrow=1` the endpoint's shape is unchanged. AppContext gains `sunriseSunsetToday` / `sunriseSunsetTomorrow` as the full upstream payloads (not just sunrise/sunset — kept the raw shape so future popover iterations can pull additional fields like nautical/astronomical twilight without another schema migration). 8 new i18n keys × 3 locales under `astronomy.*`.
- **Dependabot security PRs auto-merge for patch + minor bumps.** Triggered by the CVE-2026-8723 `qs` follow-up: Dependabot had already opened PR #157 with the fix 24 h earlier but it sat unmerged until a direct manual push superseded it. New workflow `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml` watches PRs from `dependabot[bot]`, reads the PR's metadata via `dependabot/fetch-metadata@v2`, and enables auto-merge (`gh pr merge --auto --squash`) when both: (1) the PR fixes a published security advisory (`ghsa-id` output non-empty), and (2) the bump is `version-update:semver-patch` or `version-update:semver-minor`. Major-version security bumps still need manual review (breaking-change risk). Non-security weekly maintenance bumps also stay manual — keeps the maintainer in the loop for the regular cadence. Prerequisites set up alongside: `allow_auto_merge` enabled at repo level, plus branch protection on `master` requiring `server tests` + `client build (lint + webpack)` to pass before any PR merges. Branch protection runs with `enforce_admins: false` so the maintainer's existing direct-push-to-master workflow is unaffected — only Dependabot's PRs (which aren't admins) are gated by the CI requirement, which is exactly the safety net we want.
- **Sense HAT overcast — sun glow shows where the sun is behind the clouds.** Captures the familiar real-world cue where you can usually guess where the sun is on an overcast day because that patch of sky is noticeably brighter. New `_overcast_sun_glow(row, col, sun_row, sun_col)` helper produces a Gaussian-shaped (σ=1.5) brightness contribution centred on the sun's position; the overcast frame adds it on top of the diagonal wave when `is_day` is True. Peak boost is `+30 R, +25 G, +10 B` (warm tint — slightly more red/green than blue, suggesting diffused sunlight rather than a pure-white spotlight). At sun centre + wave peak the pixel reaches ~(170, 165, 155), distinctly brighter than the surrounding wave's (140, 140, 145) without going off-scale. Sun position uses the same `_compute_sun_pos` arc that drives `clear-day` and `sunset`, so as the real sun arcs from east to west the bright spot on the overcast sky tracks the same path. Disabled at night (no analogous moon glow yet — possible future iteration). `--state overcast` test mode loops the sun arc over 30 s so the glow drifts across the matrix during validation.
- **Sense HAT fog — drifting density layer + occasional "rift" event.** Once the breath was strobe-free, the unified pulse felt too clinical — real fog has spatial variations and momentary thinnings. Two layers added on top of the existing breath:
  - **Drift (continuous):** slow horizontal sine wave (~60 s period) that modulates each column's brightness by ±4 %, so denser/thinner patches of fog glide horizontally across the matrix like wind-driven mist. The breath remains the dominant effect; this is a quieter second-order character.
  - **Rift (event):** every ~90 s, a Gaussian-shaped brighter zone (peak +15 % brightness, σ=1.2 columns) traverses the matrix from off-screen left to off-screen right over ~3 s. Like a brief patch of sunlight breaking through the fog. Same design philosophy as the clear-night shooting star: 97 % of the time the animation is calm, every minute or two a delightful moment you'd only catch if glancing.
  - Layered additively over the breath + random dither, all clamped to [0, 1]. Max consecutive-tick mean-R delta rises from 5.4 to 6.7 (rift entry/exit creates brief brightening bursts, by design).

### Fixed
- **Settings — narrow-viewport layout: sliders and AI-section toggles no longer overflow their cells.** Three coupled regressions surfaced when testing the panel at sub-720 px viewports (iPhone responsive mode, narrow PWA windows):
  - **Opacity sliders overflowed horizontally past the cell border**, clipping the `%` readout outside the panel. Root cause: native `<input type="range">` has `min-width: auto` ≈ 129 px (intrinsic width), so even with `flex: 1` it floored the `.brightnessRow` min-content at ~193 px. When a `.grid4` cell shrank below that, the row pushed past the cell. Latent since [`6f2a84b`](https://github.com/thicla01/pi-weather-station/commit/6f2a84b) (May 19) — that commit converted grid columns to `minmax(0, 1fr)` and added `min-width: 0` to `.apiKeyInput` as "belt-and-braces" but missed applying the same to `.brightnessSlider`. Fix: `min-width: 0` on `.brightnessSlider`, matching the documented pattern from 6f2a84b.
  - **AI-section toggles' content overflowed sideways into the adjacent cell**, visibly overlapping the next toggle's track/label/sub at ~430 px and below. Root cause: `.toggle` was `display: inline-flex`, which sized the toggle to its content's preferred width (~400 px when label + sub-text are summed) rather than constraining it to the grid cell. Fix: switched to `display: flex` so the toggle fills its cell width and the children wrap inside.
  - **`.grid2` (AI · radar analysis section) stayed at 2 columns even at viewports too narrow to fit a toggle + sub-text per cell**, forcing label and sub to compete for horizontal space and visually overlap. Fix: mobile-first responsive — `.grid2` defaults to 1 column, expands to 2 at ≥ 720 px (same breakpoint as `.grid4`). Each toggle gets the full panel width (~340 px on a 375 px iPhone) at narrow widths.
  - **Visual polish**: AI-section toggle labels now use a fixed-width column (150 px, scoped via `.grid2 .toggleLabel`) so the sub-text starts at the same X coordinate across all rows — labels and subs line up like a tidy two-column table regardless of label length. Width sized to fit the longest FR label "Points d'échantillonnage" without wrapping. Scoped to `.grid2` so `.toggleRow` sleep toggles (no sub) keep their content-sized layout. Also added `min-width: 0` on `.toggleLabel` / `.toggleSub` as defence at very narrow viewports (< ~360 px) so long single words don't push past the cell.
- **Settings — sleep-stage delay dropdown now follows the active palette in night and night-red modes.** The native `<select>` popup is owned by the browser process and ignores CSS-defined option colours — Chromium on macOS forced white text on a system-dark surface (illegible on `nightRed` where `--c-text` should be red; merely lucky-looking on `night` because `--c-text` happens to be near-white cream); Firefox forced white text on a system-light surface (white-on-white in `day`, off-theme otherwise). Iterated through every CSS hack — `option { color }` / `option { background-color }`, `color-scheme: light/dark` on the select, the same at the ambient-root level, explicit `select { background-color }` override — and confirmed none of them is respected consistently in the popup chrome. Replaced `DelaySelect` with a custom `<button>` + `<ul role="listbox">` rendered in the React DOM. The trigger reuses the existing `.fieldBox` visuals (border, accent-soft fill, 30 px height) so the closed state is unchanged; the popup uses `var(--c-bg)` + `var(--c-text)`, with the selected option highlighted in `var(--c-accent-soft)` + `var(--c-accent)` — full palette fidelity in all four modes across Chromium and Firefox on both Pi kiosk and macOS dev. Dismissal mirrors `DetailsPopover`: pointerdown outside + Escape key, with the pointerdown listener deferred via `setTimeout(_, 0)` so the click that opened the menu doesn't immediately close it. ARIA wired up — `aria-haspopup="listbox"` + `aria-expanded` on the trigger, `role="option"` + `aria-selected` on each item. Side-improvement noticed during the investigation: `colorScheme: tod === "day" ? "light" : "dark"` added to the AmbientLayers root so any native form chrome (scrollbars on `.heroSlot`, future native controls) under the ambient tree matches the palette tone — harmless in the current absence of native controls there, defends against the same class of regression on any future addition.
- **`sensehat_weather.py --test` / `--state` refuses to start when `pi-sensehat.service` is active.** Field-test loss: during 2026-05-24 fog validation the user ran `--state fog` while the production daemon was still running, both wrote to `/dev/fb*` in parallel at ~8 Hz each, and the resulting interleaved frames read as "fog with rain superimposed" (the production daemon was rendering rain because it was actually raining outside, mixed with fog from the test). Added a `subprocess` check on `systemctl --user is-active pi-sensehat.service` at both test entry points; if the service is active, log a clear error with the `stop`/`start` recipe and `sys.exit(1)`. Gracefully degrades on hosts without systemctl (dev macOS, non-systemd Linux) — the check returns False and the test proceeds as before.
- **Sense HAT — fog breath stopped reading as stroboscopic.** Field-test feedback after shipping `--state fog`: the supposedly-smooth 6 s breath produced a clear strobe effect. Root cause is RGB565 quantization on a spatially-uniform animation — the framebuffer uses 5-bit R/B and 6-bit G, so the slow breath crosses ~11 R-quantization levels in 3 s (half-cycle), and because all 64 pixels share one identical brightness value, they cross each threshold on the same tick → one synchronized jump every ~270 ms → 3.7 Hz flash perceived as strobe by the eye. Added a static 8×8 Bayer dither pattern that gives each pixel a fixed sub-threshold offset (calibrated to ±0.5 of one R-quantization step in brightness units); neighbouring pixels now cross thresholds at slightly different base brightnesses, so transitions look like smooth fades instead of synchronized flashes. Max consecutive-tick mean-R delta drops from 8.0 (one full R-step jump) to 5.4 (gradual smear). The Bayer pattern is visible as a fine spatial texture only at mid-cycle and trough — at peak brightness it collapses to 1-2 displayed values (high-bit channel ceiling) and is invisible. Overcast doesn't need the same fix because its diagonal wave is already spatially varied — each pixel has its own phase, so quantization crossings happen at different ticks per pixel and no synchronization-strobe arises.

### Added
- **`sensehat_weather.py --state <slug>` — show a single weather state continuously.** Validation aid for inspecting one animation in isolation, without the strobe-like 15 s cycling through 12 states that `--test` does (and that field-tested as visually unpleasant once all states gained animation). Slugs are kebab-case: `clear-day`, `clear-night`, `sunset`, `partly-cloudy-day`, `partly-cloudy-night`, `overcast`, `fog`, `rain`, `rain-light`, `snow`, `ice`, `storm`. Argparse rejects unknown slugs with the full list, so a typo gives an actionable error. The sun-arc states (`clear-day`, `sunset`) loop the full east→west traversal on a 30 s period — fast enough to see the motion, slow enough that it doesn't read as cartoonish.
- **Sense HAT — `overcast` and `fog` are now animated.** The last two static weather frames in the rotation gain motion. Overcast is a rolling diagonal wave that drifts south-west → north-east across the matrix, modulating each pixel through the three canonical grey shades (`GREY_DARK ↔ GREY_MID ↔ GREY_LIGHT`) on a 12 s sine cycle — same three-tone palette as the original static checkerboard, just sliding past instead of held. The math is set up so the sine midpoint lands exactly on `GREY_MID`, which means the wave reproduces the checkerboard's tonal balance in smooth transition. Fog becomes a uniform breathing pulse — all 64 pixels exhale together between 55 % and 100 % of `FOG_COLOR` on a 6 s cycle, slower than the 4 s star twinkle because fog feels heavy. Brightness floor at 55 % keeps the dim phase clearly visible after the night-time `BRIGHTNESS_NIGHT × 0.35` multiplier (≈ 19 % effective), above the visibility threshold the star calibration earlier in the cycle established. Both states promoted to `_ANIMATED_STATES` so the main-loop tick advances every frame.
- **Sense HAT now follows the kiosk's currently-viewed map location.** Before: `/api/sensehat` resolved coordinates from `settings.json` `startingLat`/`startingLon`, falling back to ipapi. So if the user clicked a Texas point with an active Severe Thunderstorm Warning on the kiosk, the Sense HAT at home kept showing the Canada-default location and never lit up — even though the AlertBanner on the same screen was clearly displaying the red alert. New `POST /api/kiosk-location` endpoint (localhostOnly) backed by a tiny in-memory cache in `server/kioskLocationCtrl.js`. The client posts the current `mapGeo` (debounced 1 s after the pan settles) every time the user moves the map view. `/api/sensehat` now resolves the location in this order: kiosk cache → `startingLat`/`startingLon` → ipapi. No TTL on the cache — last write wins, lifetime of the process, server restart reverts to the persistent default. The `startingLat`/`startingLon` semantics are preserved (still "user-chosen persistent default", set via Settings → Avancé → Position personnalisée). Behavior on Pis without an interactive kiosk (display-passif setups with no touch/mouse) is unchanged: nobody clicks, the cache stays empty, `/api/sensehat` falls straight through to settings → ipapi as before. 14 new tests in `test/kioskLocationCtrl.test.js` lock the validation surface (out-of-range lat/lon, non-numeric, NaN, Infinity), the happy path (numeric and string-encoded coords), edge coords (poles + dateline), last-write-wins semantics, and the read-path's clean-copy guarantee.
- **Sense HAT — gov alert breathing-pulse override.** When ECCC or NWS publishes an alert of tier `red` (extreme/severe) or `orange` (moderate) for the resolved coordinates, the 8×8 LED matrix swaps every other display state (weather frames AND clock digits) for a tier-coloured breathing pulse — red `(255, 30, 30)` pulsing every ~3 s for tornado / hurricane / severe-thunderstorm-class events, orange `(255, 140, 0)` pulsing every ~4 s for moderate-tier events like winter storm warnings or freeze warnings. Yellow / minor advisories don't trigger the override (they'd just spam the matrix; the AlertBanner on the kiosk display is the right channel for those). Because the Sense HAT script runs independently of the browser kiosk, the alert remains visible during screensaver stages 1 and 2 — a red glow in the user's peripheral vision at 3 a.m. is the entire point, and is achieved without waking the screen itself. The full-matrix override is intentional: the compromise version (coloured border around a continuing weather animation) was tried in mockups and reads as "fancy weather decoration" rather than "look at me," which isn't what an alert needs to convey across a dark room. Server-side, `/api/sensehat` now includes an optional `alert: { tier, severity, source, event }` field — see `docs/api.md`. Poll cadence on the SenseHat scripts dropped from 10 min to 60 s so an incoming tornado warning is reflected on the LED matrix within a minute of appearing in the upstream feed; the server's 5-minute internal alert-source caches absorb the extra calls without an upstream-fetch increase, and the "Updated" log line is now gated on actual state change so 60 lines/hour of redundant noise don't accumulate.

### Changed
- **AI summary polling now suspends during sleep mode, resumes instantly on wake.** Before: the 15-min Anthropic poll kept ticking through sleep stages 1 and 2, burning one `claude-haiku-4-5` call per cache miss for a screen nobody was looking at (~32 needless summaries on an 8 h overnight sleep). The `ScreenSaver` overlay covers the UI visually but doesn't unmount anything underneath, so `AiSummaryInline` / `AiSummary` kept polling. Hoisted the runtime sleep stage (0/1/2) out of `App/index.js` into `AppContext` so background-fetching components can read it without prop-drilling through `AmbientLayers → LayoutPi → AiSummaryInline`. Both AI summary components now gate their fetch effect on `sleepStage > 0` (early return + cleanup), and since `sleepStage` is in the effect deps, the natural re-run on the `2 → 0` transition triggers an immediate `fetchSummary()` so the slab is fresh by the time the screensaver's 300 ms fade-out completes. Server-side 15-min cache still absorbs back-to-back wake events without paying Anthropic twice.

### Added
- **Sense HAT — partly-cloudy day and night now animate (cloud drift + star twinkle).** Both partly-cloudy frames were static pre-2026-05; they now share a drifting-cloud builder that shifts the canonical cloud shape one column every ~12 s, wrapping at the screen edge so the cloud reappears on the other side. At night the same drift overlays a sky of twinkling stars in the lower half (the upper half is occupied by the cloud), reusing the sine-pulse twinkle from the clear-night animation. The shooting star stays exclusive to the clear-night frame — its diagonal path would clip in and out of the cloud occlusion zone and read as broken.

### Fixed
- **Sense HAT — clear-night twinkle and shooting star never actually played.** Yesterday's `acfbf97` commit added the animation builder but didn't widen the main loop's animation gate, so the clear-night cache key `("clear", False, 0, 3)` was static and only the first frame ever reached the framebuffer. Replaced the bare `state in _ANIMATED_STATES` checks in both `run()` and `run_test()` with a new `_is_animated(state, is_day)` helper that ORs the explicit animated set with the "clear at night" case. Same helper now covers the partly-cloudy day/night drift (`partly_cloudy` is now in `_ANIMATED_STATES` outright).
- **Sense HAT — twinkling stars dropped to "off" for ~1.5 s on every cycle.** Field-test feedback: stars appeared to fully extinguish during the dim phase of the 4 s sine pulse rather than just dimming. Root cause was a math chain I missed when picking the floor: `_clear_night_frame` produces star pixels at brightness in [0.40, 1.00], then `apply_brightness` multiplies the whole frame by `BRIGHTNESS_NIGHT = 0.35` for night-time dimming — combined floor is `0.40 × 0.35 = 0.14` (14 %), below the Sense HAT LED's perceived-on threshold. The sine spends roughly a third of each cycle near its extrema, which matches the reported "1-2 seconds off" perfectly. Bumped `_STAR_BRIGHTNESS_FLOOR` from 0.40 → 0.65 so the effective floor lands at ~23 %, clearly visible, while keeping a ~12 % perceptible swing against the 35 % ceiling.

## [2.18.1] - 2026-05-22

### Fixed
- **v3 default UI showed missing weather data + raw lat/lon location** — regression introduced by the v3-as-default flip in v2.18.0. The v2 `WeatherInfo` component was the trigger for `getWeatherApiKey()` and `getReverseGeoApiKey()` (it ran them in a mount-time useEffect); v3 layouts don't mount WeatherInfo, so the two API keys stayed null on the client, the polling useEffect (gated on `weatherApiKey && mapGeo`) never fired, and `LocationName` fell back to printing the raw latitude/longitude because `reverseGeoApiKey` was missing. Symptom on first paint after upgrade: location row read `45.5604, -73.5341` instead of "Montreal", `MetricsGrid` wind/humidity/UV tiles rendered "—", and `HeroCompact` collapsed to its empty-state slab (the LocationName row alone, no temperature or icon). Air quality kept working because that fetch had already been lifted into AppContext for exactly this kind of mount-dependence bug. Fix: an AppContext-level useEffect that runs both getters on mount, gated on the key not already being set so the optimistic-update path from WeatherInfo's own effect (still present for the v2 fallback) stays a no-op. Validated end-to-end via Chrome DevTools — the missing `/api/weather/current`, `/api/weather/hourly`, `/api/weather/daily`, `/api/reverse-geocode`, `/api/sunrise-sunset` requests now fire on first paint and the UI renders the full Montreal forecast (15 °C / Dégagé / 9 kph / 22% / UV 5 / sunrise 05:13 / sunset 20:27 + hourly strip).

## [2.18.0] - 2026-05-22

### Changed
- **v3 "Ambient Layers" interface is now the default** (`experimentalUiC` flag flipped from `false` to `true` in `AppContext`). Every fresh install now boots into the v3 layout — `LayoutDesktop` / `LayoutMobile` / `LayoutPi`, `HeroBand`, `MetricsGrid`, `BottomDock`, `ChartTabs`, the ambient `SettingsPanel` and `DebugPanel`, the alert banner with collapsible detail slab, etc. Decision made during the Phase 3 tech-debt session after surfacing that active development had been on v3 for weeks (every May 2026 feature shipped first into the `components/ambient/` tree) while v2 only saw bug fixes mirrored from v3 — the dual-UI maintenance cost was paying itself daily. The toggle stays in Settings → Advanced as an escape hatch ("disable to fall back to v2 if you hit an issue") so anyone who finds a v3-only regression can roll back per-device while the fix lands; the v2 `components/Settings/`, `components/Debug/`, and the rest of the non-ambient subtree will be removed wholesale in a future minor version once a few weeks of field testing pass without user-visible regressions. i18n strings updated in en/fr/es so the toggle no longer reads "preview"/"off by default".

### Added
